# R24 Full Audit — 2026-09-05/06

Scope: 16 angles (regressions in W1-W6 code, hardcoded values live+research, every config
parameter, entry/exit strategy logic, P&L, time/calendar, concurrency, ML leakage, test
honesty, ops substrate incl. C:/IBC, data integrity, docs-vs-code) + triage of all 123
previously registered findings.

Method: 16 finders -> 137 raw candidates -> STRICT two-lens adversarial verification
(code-reading + execution-probe; a finding survives ONLY if BOTH refuters concede) ->
109 standing, 28 refuted. 80 agents, 0 errors.

Severity: live_money 21 | research_validity 36 | ops_reporting 34 | cosmetic 18

TRIAGE of the 123 prior findings: 28 FIXED, 19 PARTIAL, 78 OPEN, 1 OBSOLETE.
The W1-W6 waves closed the headline clusters, NOT the register.

STATUS: REGISTERED, NOT FIXED.

---

## concurrency-01-claim-before-book-is-at-most-once  [bug | live_money | live_path=True]

**Where:** src/ait/bot/orchestrator.py:740 (claim), :790-800 (get_daily_stats / record_trade_result / update_daily_stats AFTER the claim, outside any try); src/ait/bot/orchestrator.py:679 (drain claim), :693-700 (drain booking), :720-723 (drain except swallows); src/ait/bot/state.py:763-771 (claim_exit_booking = DELETE, committed on its own connection); PLAN.md:1472-1474 ('single DELETE, sqlite-serialized => exactly-once')

**Claim:** W1's outbox is at-most-once, not exactly-once: the claim is a committed DELETE performed BEFORE any booking write, so any failure between the claim and update_daily_stats (a sqlite error, or a process kill in that window) deletes the only record of the obligation while nothing has been booked. The drain cannot recover it because the row is gone.

**Evidence:** state.py:768-771: `with sqlite3.connect(self._db_path) as conn: cur = conn.execute("DELETE FROM exit_outbox WHERE trade_id = ?" ...); return cur.rowcount > 0` (commits on exit). orchestrator.py:740 `_claimed = self._state.claim_exit_booking(trade_id)` then :790 `stats = self._state.get_daily_stats()` ... :799 `self._circuit_breaker.record_trade_result(realized_pnl)` :800 `self._state.update_daily_stats(stats)` with no transaction and no try. Executed probe (real StateManager + real _process_completed_exits, update_daily_stats raising OperationalError('database is locked') once): `outbox rows after failed booking: []` | `daily_stats.total_pnl: 0.0 | breaker daily_pnl: -272.29 | consecutive_losses: 1` | `after drain -> daily_stats.total_pnl: 0.0 | trades_lost: 0`. Same in the drain itself (P1b): `exit_outbox_booking_failed ... error='database is locked'` -> `outbox: [] | breaker daily_pnl: -272.29 | daily_stats.total_pnl: 0.0`, second drain books nothing. Breaker blob and daily_stats now permanently disagree (the MTM brake reads daily_stats.total_pnl at orchestrator.py:905, the realized halt reads the breaker).

**Trigger:** A -$272 stop-out close is committed; the executor callback claims the row (DELETE commits); then a hard kill lands before orchestrator.py:800 (keeper_ait.bat:6 documents python being killed 'on a ~30-60 min cycle'; the claim->update_daily_stats window spans several sqlite round trips: total_commission, count_executions, get_trade_by_id, get_daily_stats, breaker _persist) OR update_daily_stats raises (P2b shows a concurrent writer makes StateManager writes raise after the 5s busy timeout). Result: the loss never reaches daily_stats/PDT/Thompson (and, on a kill, not the breaker either) and there is nothing left to drain.

**Consequence:** A committed stop-out loss is deleted from the outbox and then never reaches daily_stats (the MTM brake's realized term at orchestrator.py:916, the DAILY SUMMARY, PDT, Thompson) and — if the failure lands before :799 — never reaches the circuit breaker either; nothing remains to drain, so the daily-loss halt / MTM brake under-count for the rest of the day exactly as in the pre-W1 defect, only through a narrower window.

**Suggested fix:** Book first, retire last: one StateManager.book_exit(trade_id, stats, breaker_blob) that writes daily_stats and the breaker blob and DELETEs the outbox row in a single transaction (the claim becomes a claimed_at marker or a BEGIN IMMEDIATE), so a failure anywhere leaves the row drainable. Executing test: raise in update_daily_stats after the claim -> assert the row is still returned by pending_exit_bookings and a second drain books it exactly once.

## concurrency-02-claim-exception-books-anyway-then-drain-double-books  [logic | live_money | live_path=True]

**Where:** src/ait/bot/orchestrator.py:739-742 (`except Exception: _claimed = True  # outbox unavailable: book as before`), :790-800 (books), :668-700 (drain later claims the surviving row and books it again)

**Claim:** When claim_exit_booking raises, _process_completed_exits treats the close as claimed and books it, but the outbox row survives; 120s later the cycle drain claims that row and books the same close a second time into daily stats, the circuit breaker (consecutive_losses/daily_pnl), PDT and Thompson. 'Book as before' was safe pre-W1 only because no second booker existed.

**Evidence:** orchestrator.py:739-742: `try: _claimed = self._state.claim_exit_booking(trade_id)\nexcept Exception:  # noqa: BLE001 — outbox unavailable: book as before\n    _claimed = True`. Executed probe with claim raising OperationalError('database is locked') once, then a healthy drain: `after callback: total_pnl -272.29 trades_lost 1 breaker losses 1 | outbox rows: 1` -> `orphan_close_booked ... pnl=-272.29` -> `after drain: total_pnl -544.58 trades_lost 2 breaker losses 2 thompson calls 2`. Reachability of the exception (P2b, real sqlite, default 5s timeout): a second connection holding `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` on the same DB -> `claim raised OperationalError: database is locked after 5.0s`.

**Trigger:** Any writer holding ait_state.db's write lock for >5s at the instant of the claim: an operator restatement script run against the live ledger (bot_state carries d1_restatement / r16_executions_restatement / straddle_restatement markers, so such scripts are run) or an I/O stall. A doubled loss trips the 3-loss pause after two real losses and double-counts toward the daily-loss cap; a doubled WIN inflates daily_pnl (masking a later breach of the cap) and double-credits Thompson.

**Consequence:** One real loss is counted twice in the breaker's consecutive_losses and daily_pnl (3-loss pause trips after two real losses; daily-loss cap reached early) and in daily_stats; a doubled WIN inflates daily_pnl and masks a later genuine daily-loss breach, and Thompson double-credits the arm.

**Suggested fix:** On a claim exception do NOT book: log exit_booking_claim_failed at warning and `continue` — the outbox drain is now the designed recovery path and will book it once the DB is writable. Executing test: claim raises -> callback books nothing -> drain books exactly once (stats/breaker/Thompson each moved once).

## concurrency-03-cas-gate-misreads-concurrent-promotion-as-terminal  [regression | live_money | live_path=True]

**Where:** src/ait/execution/executor.py:2470-2492 (_update_trade_filled: refused CAS -> log 'row already closed/cancelled', set alert_fill_after_close, return without update_trade_entry_price/insert_open_position/update_trade_quantity), :2549-2565 (_update_trade_partial same); src/ait/execution/reconciler.py:832-836 (sweep promotes PENDING->FILLED and _ensure_open_position_row), :130-135 (that row is inserted at the SIGNAL entry_price), :965-967 (reconcile promotion); ordering: src/ait/bot/orchestrator.py:1114 -> :3616 (cycle step 2 runs _sweep_stale_pending) before :1154 (step 6 check_fills_safe); executor.py:896 (check_fills skipped while disconnected), :1585-1609 (240s cancel only sendable when connected); reconciler.py:114 STALE_PENDING_MINUTES = 30

**Claim:** The W1 CAS gate treats EVERY refused PENDING/PARTIAL->FILLED transition as 'fill landed on a terminal row'. A legitimate concurrent promotion of the same PENDING row to FILLED by the sweep/reconcile (not a terminal state) now makes the executor discard the REAL fill price and filled quantity, leave open_positions at the signal price, and fire a false CRITICAL 'a REAL position may be live at the broker with no managing trade row' page. Pre-W1 the same interleaving wrote the real price/quantity (the blind UPDATEs were correct for this case).

**Evidence:** Executed probe (real StateManager + TradeExecutor._update_trade_filled): seed PENDING @1.90 qty 1 -> `st.transition('T-1', (PENDING,), FILLED)` (the reconciler's promotion CAS) -> `_update_trade_filled(pending, 1.62, filled_qty=2)` -> `illegal_transition_blocked from_statuses=['pending','partial'] to_status=filled`, `fill_landed_on_terminal_row fill_price=1.62 note='row already closed/cancelled — a REAL position may be live at the broker with no managing trade row'`; trades row = ('filled', 1.9, 1) -- fill was 1.62 x2; `alert_fill_after_close: T-1|QQQ|1.62`. executor.py:2466-2467 CAS from-set is `(TradeStatus.PENDING, TradeStatus.PARTIAL)` so a row already FILLED is refused exactly like CLOSED. Also the flag is ONE KV key (executor.py:2487-2489 set_state('alert_fill_after_close', ...)): a second refused fill overwrote the first in the probe (`alert_fill_after_close now: T-2|QQQ|3.1`), so only the last is paged by orchestrator.py:657-663.

**Trigger:** A PENDING entry that reaches STALE_PENDING_MINUTES (30) with its tracker alive and then fills where the fill is first visible in the position cache rather than to check_fills: a >=30-min connection loss with a resting entry (check_fills skipped at executor.py:896, the 240s cancel cannot be sent), then reconnect -> the next _trading_cycle's step-2 sweep (orchestrator.py:1114->3616) sees legs live and promotes -> step 6 / the 1s event task's 'filled' verdict (via _broker_fill_evidence) hits the gate. Same for a halt/cancel-reject that keeps an entry alive past 30 min, and for reconcile() promotions overlapping a late event task. Outcome: entry_price = signal price (R2/C1 slippage overstatement returns for that trade), quantity = signal quantity (if the fill qty differs the exit combo at orchestrator.py:3132 is sized at trade.quantity, leaving contracts unmanaged - the R12 F4.4 defect), plus a false CRITICAL page.

**Consequence:** For that trade: entry_price stays the optimistic signal price (realized_pnl overstated by entry slippage — a go-live verdict input), quantity stays the signal quantity (a partial fill promoted to FILLED at full size means the reverse-combo exit is sized larger than the live position and can OPEN an inverse position on the unfilled remainder; a larger fill leaves contracts unmanaged), plus a false CRITICAL 'position live with no managing row' page; concurrent refusals overwrite each other in the single alert key so only the last is paged.

**Suggested fix:** After a refused CAS re-read the row: if status is FILLED/PARTIAL (a promotion won the race) write entry_price/quantity and settle open_positions at info level; only CLOSED/CANCELLED/REJECTED is terminal and pages. Key the alert per trade (alert_fill_after_close_<trade_id>) and drain all such keys. Executing test: promote then fill -> entry_price == real fill, quantity == filled_qty, no alert.

## data-integrity-07  [logic | live_money | live_path=True]

**Where:** src/ait/bot/orchestrator.py:2596-2598 (stats.trades_taken += 1 right after record_trade, before any fill), :1944-1959 (entry budget = tier - trades_taken; tiers 2 / 4 / max_daily_trades); src/ait/execution/reconciler.py:753-857 (_sweep_stale_pending books NEVER_FILLED_EXIT_REASON without touching daily_stats); data/ait_state.db daily_stats rows 2026-07-06, 2026-07-15, 2026-07-16

**Claim:** daily_stats.trades_taken counts every order PLACED and is never decremented when the order is cancelled or swept as never-filled, so the intraday entry budget is consumed by phantom trades: on 07-06 trades_taken=12 although 6 of the 12 rows are stale_pending_never_filled $0 closes; on 07-15 trades_taken=2 with both rows status='cancelled'; on 07-16 trades_taken=3 with one cancelled.

**Evidence:** Executed: daily_stats 2026-07-06 (12,1,0,126.4); trades entered 07-06: 12 rows of which T-20260706-132529, -132658, -132704, -132822, -141829, -152823 have exit_reason_detailed='stale_pending_never_filled', exit_price 0. daily_stats 2026-07-15 (2,0,0,0.0) while T-20260715-113937-4d0918 and T-20260715-143802-758420 are status='cancelled'. grep 'trades_taken -=' / 'trades_taken = max' across src: no matches; the only writer is orchestrator.py:2597 `stats.trades_taken += 1`. orchestrator.py:1951-1959: `budget = 2` before 10:30, `4` before 14:00, `max_trades` after; `return min(budget, max_trades) - taken`.

**Trigger:** Two entry orders placed in the first hour that go stale/cancel (executor.py:114-138 documents this happening on healthy quotes) leave zero first-hour budget with nothing filled; after 10:30 the same with four.

**Consequence:** The intraday entry gate is spent by orders that never became positions. Two placements that go stale or cancel before 10:30 ET zero the first-hour budget with nothing filled; four do the same to the mid-day tier — observed on 2026-07-06, where the entire pre-14:00 tier of 4 was consumed by four never-filled orders and the day exhausted the 12-trade cap with 6 phantom rows. Because the counter is also the reported 'Trades Today' (dashboard/app.py:638, portfolio.py:1107 today_trades, analytics.py:267), that day reports 12 trades against 6 real ones.

**Suggested fix:** Increment trades_taken when the entry actually FILLS (the fill/promotion path that already writes open_positions), or decrement it in the same StateManager transaction used by _sweep_stale_pending's NEVER_FILLED close and by the PENDING->CANCELLED transitions in executor.py. Better: derive the budget from filled + still-working entry orders rather than from a monotone placement counter. Pin with an executing test that places two orders, sweeps both as never-filled, and asserts _get_trade_budget() has returned to its tier value.

## docs-policy-04  [doc_vs_code | live_money | live_path=True]

**Where:** docs/RUNBOOK.md:12 ('Full stop'), :41-42 (manual intervention: 'stop the bot processes if you're closing the whole book'), :93 (only mention of keeper_ait.bat); keeper_ait.bat:43-46

**Claim:** RUNBOOK's 'Full stop' and manual-intervention procedures tell the operator to stop the bot processes but never to stop keeper_ait.bat, the production supervisor that relaunches run_orchestrator.py within <=90 s whenever no run_orchestrator/ait.main process exists. Following the RUNBOOK during a manual close brings the exit engine and startup reconcile back mid-procedure — the exact R13 'rebuild what you just closed' hazard the procedure exists to prevent.

**Evidence:** keeper_ait.bat:43-46: `powershell ... -match 'run_orchestrator|ait\.main' ... ; if errorlevel 1 ( echo ... orchestrator DOWN - relaunching ; start "" /min %PY% run_orchestrator.py )` on a 90 s loop. Executed evidence in keeper.log: '02-09-2026 20:04:56 orchestrator DOWN - relaunching' then '02-09-2026 20:11:00 orchestrator DOWN - relaunching' — a stop at ~20:10 was undone by the keeper 6 minutes after the first relaunch. RUNBOOK kill-switch table (lines 6-12) and manual-intervention steps 1-5 (34-50) never mention the keeper; grep of both registers for 'Full stop|stop processes' finds no coverage.

**Trigger:** Operator executes RUNBOOK:12 or :40-42 while keeper_ait.bat is running (its normal state).

**Consequence:** An operator executing RUNBOOK 'Full stop' or manual-intervention step 2 while the keeper runs (its normal production state) does not achieve a stop: within <=90 s the keeper relaunches run_orchestrator.py, the exit engine and startup reconcile come back mid-procedure, and the bot competes with the operator's in-flight manual close — double-closing into an inverted position on a live condor, the exact R13 hazard the procedure was written to prevent. The one control the RUNBOOK offers for 'closing the whole book' is unachievable as documented.

**Suggested fix:** Add step 0 to both the kill-switch 'Full stop' row and the manual-intervention procedure: close the 'AIT Bot Keeper' console window (title is set at keeper_ait.bat:11) before stopping any python process, and re-open it at step 5. Better, make it enforceable rather than remembered: teach keeper_ait.bat to skip the relaunch branch while a data/KEEPER_PAUSE file exists (or while data/HALT exists, if that is the intended semantics), log 'keeper paused by <file>' each tick so the pause is observable, and document the file in the kill-switch table alongside data/HALT.

## logic-entry-01  [bug | live_money | live_path=True]

**Where:** src/ait/strategies/iron_condor.py:119-123 (budget width cap), :278-285 (AIT_IC_MIN_CREDIT floor), :325-329 (max_loss_over_budget); src/ait/bot/orchestrator.py:1229 (risk_budget = NLV x risk.max_position_risk_pct), :1959-2007 _launch_size_coherence_check (esp. :1976 min_width = max(1.0, min_credit/0.45), :1984 budget < min_viable_cost); PLAN.md:502-503 (Phase-2 recipe: AIT_SIMULATED_CAPITAL=2100 + max_position_risk_pct 0.07)

**Claim:** At the documented launch account the live condor builder cannot emit a single signal: the budget cap forces $1-2 wings whose 0.20-delta credit ($0.31-0.64) can never reach the $0.70 absolute credit floor, so every candidate dies at credit_floor; the first per-trade budget that trades is ~$230 (NLV >= ~$7.7k at 3% or ~$3.3k at 7%). The launch-size coherence check that exists to catch exactly this reports COHERENT at $2.1k/7%, $3k/3%, $4.5k, $6k because it models a $1.56 'minimum viable' wing (min_credit/0.45) that the builder never constructs (cap yields width 1.0-2.0, and the R16 re-check at :325 rejects anything wider than the budget).

**Evidence:** Executed IronCondor.generate_signals on a Black-Scholes chain (SPY 640, 21 DTE, put skew per config skew_slope 0.0736), contract values wing_k 1.6 / min_cw 0.10 / min_credit 0.70: 'NLV 3000 budget $90 width_target 1.00 -> (rejected, credit_floor) credit=0.315 width=1.0'; 'NLV 4500 budget $135 width_target 1.50 -> credit_floor credit=0.315 width=1.0'; 'NLV 6000 budget $180 width_target 2.00 -> credit_floor credit=0.61 width=2.0'; 'NLV 8000 budget $240 -> generated credit=0.90 width=3.0 max_loss=210'. Sweep at atm_iv 0.12/0.15/0.20 and IWM-like S=570: 'Phase-2 recipe budget $147 -> (rejected, credit_floor) credit=0.59-0.64 width=2.0 | first budget that trades: $230 => NLV >= $7,667 at 3% / $3,286 at 7%' (credit at fixed delta is IV-invariant, so no IV regime rescues it). Executed TradingOrchestrator._launch_size_coherence_check with the live Python313 interpreter: 'launch_size_coherence breaker=42 budget=147 min_viable_ic=86 nlv=2100 problems=0 -> COHERENT (no alert)'; same at nlv=3000 (budget=90, problems=0) and 6000. Code: iron_condor.py:121 'affordable = self.risk_budget / (100.0 * max(0.5, 1.0 - min_ratio))' (= budget/90) vs orchestrator.py:1976 'min_width = max(1.0, min_credit / 0.45)' and :1977 'min_viable_cost = (min_width - min_credit) * 100' = $86.

**Trigger:** Go-live at the planned $2.1k-$3k account (or any NLV below ~$7.7k at 3% / ~$3.3k at 7%): zero entries indefinitely, every scan logging rejected/credit_floor, and the only self-test that could page says the cap grid is coherent.

**Consequence:** Fail-CLOSED, no capital at risk, but the pre-funding gate is wrong: at NLV $3k/3% (budget $90) the cap forces $1 wings whose credit (~$0.32-0.40) can never reach $0.70 -> zero entries deterministically while the self-test logs problems=0; at the PLAN:502-504 recipe ($2.1k/7%, budget $147) $2 wings sit at $0.62 modelled / $0.665-0.72 in the July ledger -> most scans rejected credit_floor, occasional entries only; first reliably tradeable budget ~$210-230 (width 3) = NLV >= ~$7k at 3% / ~$3.1k at 7%. The 'confirm the coherence self-test passes' step of the Phase-2 recipe is satisfied by a false COHERENT for budgets $86-$130 (bricked) and $130-$210 (marginal).

**Suggested fix:** Make the coherence check exercise the real builder: run IronCondor.generate_signals on a synthetic $1-grid chain at the resolved budget with 0.20-delta shorts and the config skew, and page when no grid width satisfies both max_loss <= budget and credit >= max(min_credit, ratio x width); at minimum replace min_credit/0.45 with the smallest grid width w such that ~0.35 x w >= min_credit (w=2 today) and cost (w - min_credit) x 100 = $130. Pin with executing tests at NLV 3000/0.03 (must page) and 7667/0.03 (must not).

## logic-exit-risk-01  [bug | live_money | live_path=True]

**Where:** src/ait/bot/orchestrator.py:3161-3189 (BAG quote poll), :3299-3326 (no-quote fallback branch), :3307-3311 (fallback price), :3249-3259 (R16 mark-anchor formula), :3153-3160 (R16 comment claiming the raw-Bag quote works); PLAN.md:876; logs/ait.log.4 (2026-08-31 session)

**Claim:** Every multi-leg exit the bot has placed since the R16 'quote the raw Bag' fix took the NO-QUOTE fallback, and that fallback prices a credit close at max(2*mark_cost, entry_credit + 0.25*width) - at the promoted $39-60 wings that is 6-10x the marked cost, so routine take-profit exits were sent to IBKR as BUY LMT 14.04 (SPY, mark 1.40) and 18.60 (QQQ, mark 2.94). IBKR's price band rejected 61 of 63; the two fills only landed because the exchange filled at NBBO, not because any bot-side bound protected the price.

**Evidence:** Counts over every retained ait.log*: '"event": "combo_exit_limit"' = 0 in all files; combo_exit_no_quote_pricing_at_wing_width = 63, exit_order_placed = 63, exit_order_cancelled = 61, exit_fill_processed = 2 (all in ait.log.4). First attempt: 15:11:09Z combo_exit_no_quote_pricing_at_wing_width {'symbol': 'SPY', 'wing_width': 39.0, 'mark_cost': 1.4006, 'limit_price': 14.04}; 15:11:12Z {'symbol': 'QQQ', 'wing_width': 49.0, 'mark_cost': 2.943, 'limit_price': 18.6}. Arithmetic matches orchestrator.py:3308-3309 `fallback = round(min(width, max(2.0 * mark_cost, float(trade.entry_price or 0) + 0.25 * width)), 2)`: 4.29 + 0.25*39 = 14.04; 6.35 + 0.25*49 = 18.60 - the width term dominates 2*mark (2.80 / 5.89). IBKR: 'Warning 202, reqId 1578968: Order Canceled - reason:We cannot accept an order at a limit price at or more aggressive than 1.745. Please submit your order using a limit price that is closer to the current market price of 1.42.' and reqId 1578974 '... more aggressive than 3.5 ... current market price of 2.98' (61 such warnings). The same max() sits in the quoted branch at :3258-3259 so even a real ask is only capped at 2.0-2.3x fair: probe with the live 08-24 QQQ touch stop (width 54.8, entry 6.12, fill 8.76) -> anchor 19.82 = 2.26x mark, worst overpay $1,106/contract vs the $264 loss actually taken; current QQQ 680/735 (w=60, entry 6.43) -> anchor 21.43. Contrast: the entry-side fetch on the same account logged combo_entry_priced twice on 09-01 behind W2's fail-closed NBBO gate, so BAG quotes do arrive for entries; root cause of the exit-side silence (reversed-leg Bag? cold subscription? Error 10091 x2,727 that session) not determined from logs.

**Trigger:** Any credit-structure exit on this account (0/63 obtained a quote); the loss materialises when a stale or thin complex-order-book offer sits between fair value and the limit, or when IBKR's band does not apply (the two fills went through at limit 14.04/18.60 with no Warning 202). A fill at the limit turns SPY's +$280.36 TP into (4.29-14.04)*100 = -$975 and QQQ's +$329.36 into -$1,225; on a touch stop it permits paying up to 2.3x the marked cost (about 4x the normal touch loss).

**Consequence:** Every credit-structure exit is sent as BUY LMT at entry+25%-of-width (6-10x the marked cost at $36-60 wings). 61/63 attempts were rejected by IBKR's price band (the only thing that protected the price), turning a routine take-profit into a 32 min (SPY) / 2h55m (QQQ) loop; the 2 accepted orders filled only because ISE/NBBO improved them. Any resting complex-order-book offer between fair value and the limit would be lifted: at the limit, SPY's +$280 TP becomes -$975 and QQQ's +$329 becomes -$1,225; on a touch stop the same anchor permits paying up to 2.3x the marked cost (~4x the normal touch loss).

**Suggested fix:** Remove the entry+0.25*width term from both the fallback price and the quoted-branch anchor; bound the no-quote fallback at k*mark_cost + exit_cross (k ~1.3-1.5, floor at mark+cross) and, with a stale mark, at entry + k*expected_move; synthesize the exit NBBO from the four leg quotes when the Bag stays silent; add an executing test that a 60-wide condor marked at 3.00 never receives a limit above ~4.50, and instrument the exit poll (log ticker bid/ask/NaN per attempt) so the raw-Bag silence can be root-caused.

## logic-exit-risk-02  [logic | live_money | live_path=True]

**Where:** src/ait/execution/executor.py:1234-1251 (cancelled -> CAS revert, reject reason discarded), :2228-2265 (_determine_exit_fill_status maps rejected/inactive to 'cancelled'); src/ait/bot/orchestrator.py:2701-2709 & 3869-3926 (_exit_retry_ready backoff 30/60/120/180s, no price adaptation); orchestrator.py:944-951 + src/ait/execution/portfolio.py:276-277 (CLOSING trades excluded from check_positions, so no touch-stop evaluation while an exit order works)

**Claim:** A broker price-band rejection is handled exactly like a cancel: revert CLOSING->FILLED, wait a backoff, then re-place the SAME rejected limit. Nothing reads Warning 202's 'acceptable price', nothing steps the price, so the exit is unexecutable until the fallback price happens to be accepted. On 08-31 the SPY take-profit was demanded at 15:11:06Z and filled at 15:43:16Z (32 min); the QQQ take-profit was demanded at 15:11:09Z and filled at 18:06:32Z (2 h 55 min) after 30+ identical rejections. The touch stop - the only loss exit on a credit structure - runs through this same _close_multi_leg path, and during each ~30 s CLOSING window the position is invisible to the touch stop.

**Evidence:** ait.log.4 sequence (SPY): 15:11:09 exit_order_placed limit 14.04 -> 15:11:42 exit_order_cancelled age 33s -> 15:12:14 exit_retry_backoff strikes 2 -> 15:12:16 placed 14.04 -> 15:12:49 cancelled ... repeated; strikes histogram {2:16, 3:15, 4:14, 5:2}; backoff capped at _EXIT_REJECT_CAP_S = 180.0 (orchestrator.py:3870). executor.py:1246-1250 logs only `"exit_order_cancelled", trade_id=..., age_seconds=...` - the IBKR reason text (which names the acceptable price) never reaches the pricing code. IBKR: 61x 'Warning 202 ... Order Canceled - reason:We cannot accept an order at a limit price at or more aggressive than ...'. portfolio.py:276-277 `if trade.status not in (TradeStatus.FILLED, TradeStatus.PARTIAL): continue` - a CLOSING condor gets no rule-1b evaluation.

**Trigger:** Any exit whose fallback limit is outside IBKR's band (finding 01 makes that the normal case). For a take-profit the cost is time; for a short-strike touch on a trending underlying the loss keeps growing for the duration of the loop: at W5's gap-pricing figures a further 1%/2%/3.5% through the short strike costs $305/$681/$1,344 per contract, versus the $264-306 (0.43-0.475x credit) a same-tick touch exit costs.

**Consequence:** A broker price-band reject is indistinguishable from a cancel, so the bot re-submits the identical unexecutable limit up to every 180s, paging once per 30 min. For a take-profit the cost was 32 min and 2h55m on 08-31 (winners exposed to reversal); for a short-strike touch on a trending underlying the loss keeps growing for the duration of the loop - at W5's gap figures a further 1%/2%/3.5% through the short strike costs $305/$681/$1,344 per contract versus the $264-306 a same-tick touch exit costs.

**Suggested fix:** Hook ib.errorEvent, capture errorCode 202/110/10318 with the reason text per orderId, surface it on exit_order_cancelled, and re-price the next attempt at min(band price, ask + exit_cross); escalate price per strike rather than time-only backoff; treat >=2 fast rejects of a touch/stop exit as an immediate CRITICAL page; executing test: a fill checker fed a 202 with 'more aggressive than 1.745' must re-place at <=1.745 within one monitor pass.

## logic-exit-risk-03  [logic | live_money | live_path=True]

**Where:** src/ait/bot/orchestrator.py:2152-2156 (cooldown query `WHERE symbol=?`), :84-120 (_post_stop_cooldown_cutoff); src/ait/risk/correlation.py:24 (SECTOR_GROUPS['index'] = {SPY,QQQ,IWM,DIA}), :73-89 (check_correlation counts OPEN positions only); config.yaml:21 (`universe: [SPY, QQQ, IWM]`); src/ait/execution/portfolio.py:530-553 (R12-B4 spec: 'the same trend that just stopped it')

**Claim:** The post-stop cooldown (R12-B4, fixed twice: R21 reason match, W4 trading-day window) is keyed on SYMBOL, but the universe is three ETFs the bot's own correlation module treats as one 0.95 cluster. After a SPY short-strike touch stop the bot is free to open QQQ or IWM on the very next scan into the identical move - the autocorrelated re-entry the rule exists to prevent - because the correlation guard only counts positions still OPEN and the cooldown never consults the cluster.

**Evidence:** Executed probe (real StateManager schema, verbatim query from orchestrator.py:2152-2166, verbatim cutoff from :84-120): a SPY row with exit_reason_detailed 'short_strike_touch (spot 630.00 <= put 630.00)' and exit_time 5 minutes ago -> `SPY cooldown_blocked_until = 2026-09-03T16:58:39`, `QQQ cooldown_blocked_until = None`, `IWM cooldown_blocked_until = None`. correlation.py:73-79 builds `correlated` from `open_symbols` only; after the stop the SPY row is closed, so len(correlated)=0 < cap 2 and the QQQ entry passes gate 5. Register numeric-pairs-02 covers only the window length (30h vs one trading day), not scope.

**Trigger:** Any touch/stop-loss exit on one index ETF while the scan finds a candidate on another the same or next session (the 08-24 QQQ stop at 09:47 was followed by a re-entry 13 minutes later; the cluster variant would have passed even after the R21/W4 fixes). Cost per occurrence: a second touch-stop loss of 0.43-0.475x credit ($184-306 on the live credits) or, on a continuing move, a gap through the new short strike.

**Consequence:** After a touch/stop-loss exit on one index ETF the bot may open another index ETF condor on the same or next scan (the 08-24 QQQ re-entry 13 min after its own stop is the same-symbol instance; the cluster variant passes even after R21/W4). Cost per occurrence: a second touch-stop loss of 0.43-0.475x credit ($184-306 at live credits) or a gap through the new short strike. The adjacent fail-open means a DB hiccup removes the cooldown entirely without a log line.

**Suggested fix:** Scope the cooldown query to the stopped symbol's SECTOR_GROUPS cluster (`symbol IN (...)`, one authority shared with correlation.py); route the query through StateManager (its db_path) and make the wrapper fail CLOSED with a warning like GUARD #2; executing tests: (a) SPY touch stop 5 min ago blocks a QQQ entry until the next session; (b) a sqlite OperationalError in the cooldown refuses the entry.

**Claim:** When the trading bot is dead while IB Gateway is logged off, the supervisor never restarts the Gateway and the stack is wedged until a human logs on: the substrate has NO Gateway auto-restart (IBC AutoRestartTime empty; the Gateway does a full logoff/exit at 23:45 every day and no scheduled task or IBC command relaunches it), and master.py's bot-down branch returns on 'port closed' without ever calling ensure_gateway(), which is reachable only from BotManager.start() - itself reached from health_check only when the port is ALREADY open. The alert text and RUNBOOK promise it 'will resume automatically when Gateway returns'; on this box nothing returns it.

'autorestart file not found' ... 'Normal exit' ... 'IBC running GATEWAY 1044 has finished at 04-09-2026 23:45:02'. Same on 09-01, 09-03, 08-08. Get-ScheduledTask: no IBC/Gateway task exists (only \AIT-Bot-Start and a one-shot liveness task); the only launchers are the Startup-folder shortcut 'IB Gateway (IBC auto-login).lnk' (logon only) and master.py:374-377 `from ait.orchestration.gateway import ensure_gateway ... if not ensure_gateway(port=gw_port): _log('error','bot_start_aborted'...); return`. master.py:575-583: `if not _gateway_listening(gw_port): _log('info','bot_restart_deferred_gateway_down', port=gw_port) ... _alert(f'IB Gateway unreachable on port {gw_port} (likely daily maintenance). Bot start deferred; will resume automatically when Gateway returns. No budget spent.'); return` - no ensure_gateway call; grep confirms master.py:374/376 are the only call sites. Executed log probe (orchestrator.log): 2026-06-24/25/26 `07:32:52 ERROR bot_start_aborted | reason=gateway_not_available` then `bot_restart_deferred_gateway_down` 07:34:54 -> 08:50:54 (38-40 checks each); 2026-07-01 07:34:55 -> 12:44:55 (156 checks = 5h10m, 3h15m of it inside RTH). All four masters were task-launched at 07:30:01 while IBC's single 120s wait (gateway.py:119) expired. Today (Sat 09-05 13:07) Get-NetTCPConnection -LocalPort 4002 returns nothing: the Gateway has been down since Fri 23:45 and the live bot pid 14452 (started 09-04 07:31:51) is disconnected; the ONLY thing that will bring the Gateway back Monday is the 07:30 retrain marker restart of a still-RUNNING bot (master.py:518-540 -> stop()/start()), which the bot-dead case never reaches.

**Trigger:** Any bot process death between the 23:45 Gateway logoff and the next successful BotManager.start() (e.g. the c0000005 python313.dll crash class recorded in logs/fatal.log at 2026-09-03 08:38 landing overnight, or a bot_launch_failed_immediately exit_code=1 at 07:31 while the Gateway login is still in progress), OR ensure_gateway's single 120s wait expiring at 07:30 on a cold box (observed 06-24, 06-25, 06-26, 07-01). Master stays alive, so keeper_ait.bat:43 ('run_orchestrator' matches) never relaunches; Telegram says 'likely daily maintenance ... will resume automatically' every 30 min; open condors have no touch-stop/TP engine until someone logs on.

**Consequence:** Bot dead + Gateway port closed with the master alive is an absorbing state: the supervisor polls the port every 2 min forever and never attempts to bring the Gateway back, so open condors get no touch-stop/TP/reconcile until a human intervenes. Already materialized: 2026-07-01, 5h10m of it, 3h15m inside RTH.

**Suggested fix:** In the bot-down branch (master.py:575) call ensure_gateway(port=gw_port) on a rate limiter (e.g. one attempt per 10 min, its own alert on repeated failure) instead of only polling _gateway_listening; set IBC AutoRestartTime (e.g. 23:50) so the nightly logoff becomes a credential-free restart; fix the Telegram text at master.py:581-583 to say the Gateway is being relaunched (or that a manual login is required), matching what RUNBOOK already tells the operator. Executing test: BotManager with _gateway_listening patched False and a spy ensure_gateway, assert the spy is invoked from health_check's bot-down path (fails pre-fix).

P&L $0.00'. Same for UNKNOWN_EXIT_REASON: 'trades_lost=2 | breaker consecutive_losses=0'. **Trigger:** Any entry order still PENDING after STALE_PENDING_MINUTES=30 with no working order and no legs at the broker (sweep runs every trading cycle via _sync_risk_manager_positions and every reconcile); >=120s later the cycle drain books it. Worst case: two real stop-outs (streak=2) then one never-filled entry -> streak 0 -> third real loss does NOT trip the 3-loss pause.

**Consequence:** A $0 never-filled entry (any PENDING >30 min with no legs and no working order, swept every cycle) or a reconciler $0 needs-review sentinel is booked as a LOSS: daily_stats.trades_lost inflated (DAILY SUMMARY W-L wrong), an active consecutive-loss streak reset to 0 so a subsequent real loss does not trip the 3-loss pause (2 real losses + 1 sweep + 1 real loss = streak 1, no halt), a phantom iron_condor loss recorded in Thompson strategy ranking, and a spurious ORPHAN CLOSE BOOKED page per row.

**Suggested fix:** In _drain_exit_outbox (and defensively in _process_completed_exits) claim the row, then if not is_real_close(row['exit_reason']) log 'not_real_close_booking_skipped' and continue before touching stats/breaker/PDT/Thompson/page. Executing test: streak=2, sweep-style close_trade with NEVER_FILLED_EXIT_REASON, backdated outbox, drain -> streak still 2, trades_lost unchanged, Thompson not called, no notification; repeat for UNKNOWN_EXIT_REASON.

## pnl-money-02  [bug | live_money | live_path=True]

**Where:** src/ait/bot/orchestrator.py:641-723 (_drain_exit_outbox: books row['realized_pnl'] at :681 verbatim; no commission true-up, no update_trade_commission); vs src/ait/bot/orchestrator.py:745-780 (callback path true-up: _delta = commission_estimate - ledger at :770) and :829-833 (update_trade_commission); src/ait/execution/executor.py:2402-2446 (_calculate_realized_pnl subtracts flat commission_estimate 0.65/leg/side); src/ait/bot/state.py:887-892 (update_trade_realized_pnl)

**Claim:** Two booking paths for the SAME executor close produce different trades.realized_pnl / trades.commission. The callback path swaps the fill-time $0.65/leg/side estimate for the executions-ledger truth and stamps trades.commission; the W1 drain path (crash between close_trade and the callback, or a callback that arrives >120s after the close) books the estimate-based P&L permanently, leaves trades.commission=0.0, and never re-ingests DuckDB. For a condor the estimate is $5.20 vs the measured ~$7.33 round trip (W5), so trades.realized_pnl (the go-live PF input) is overstated by ~$2.13 and SUM(trades.commission) disagrees with SUM(executions.commission) by the whole $7.33 on every drain-booked close.

**Evidence:** Executed probe on identical seeded closes with a 10-row ledger (8 legs x 0.9168 + 2 BAG rows): 'callback: fill-time pnl=256.8 -> trades.realized_pnl=254.67 trades.commission=7.3344 ledger commission=7.3344 executions=10' vs 'drain   : fill-time pnl=256.8 -> trades.realized_pnl=256.8 trades.commission=0.0 ledger commission=7.3344 executions=10'. Drain code :681 'pnl = float(row["realized_pnl"] or 0.0)' then :684-699 stats/breaker only; grep of _drain_exit_outbox for total_commission/commission_estimate/update_trade_commission: none. Callback path :743 'if not _claimed: ... continue' means once the drain wins the race the true-up never runs for that trade.

**Trigger:** Executor close whose _process_completed_exits callback is lost (process death / keeper kill between executor.check_fills close_trade and the orchestrator callback) or delayed >120s; the next _trading_cycle drain books it. Bounded ~$2-3 per condor on realized_pnl, $7.33 on trades.commission, per affected close.

**Consequence:** Every executor close that ends up booked by the drain instead of the callback carries an estimate-based trades.realized_pnl (~$2.13 too high per 1-lot condor at the measured $7.33 round trip vs the $5.20 estimate) and trades.commission=0.0 permanently — the go-live PF/net input and the 'commissions recorded' line disagree with the executions ledger by the full round-trip commission for that trade, daily_stats/breaker see the optimistic number, and no later pass repairs it because trueup_pending is never set.

**Suggested fix:** Factor the callback's true-up + update_trade_commission into one book_close(trade_id, pnl, reason) used by both _process_completed_exits and _drain_exit_outbox; in the drain, when count_executions >= 2*legs and total_commission > 0 true up before booking, otherwise set trueup_pending_<id> exactly as the callback does. Executing test: seed a complete ledger, drain-book the close, assert trades.commission == total_commission and realized_pnl == the callback result; second test with a partial ledger asserts trueup_pending_<id> is set.

**Trigger:** Any never-filled entry swept by _sweep_stale_pending during RTH (two real losses at 10:00 and 10:30, a never-filled condor swept at 11:05 -> streak 2 -> 0; the third real loss at 11:30 no longer trips the 3-loss pause). Register string-contracts-1 counted 6 never-filled rows in 24 on the live DB, so this fires most weeks.
