## regress-live-02  [bug | live_money | live_path=True]

**Where:** src/ait/bot/orchestrator.py:738-745 (_process_completed_exits claim try/except -> _claimed = True); src/ait/bot/state.py:763-771 (claim_exit_booking DELETE); src/ait/bot/orchestrator.py:669-717 (_drain_exit_outbox re-claims the surviving row)

**Claim:** When claim_exit_booking raises (sqlite 'database is locked' past the 5s busy timeout, the exact multi-process-DB condition the R23 register documents), _process_completed_exits sets _claimed = True and books the close; the outbox row is never deleted, so 120s later _drain_exit_outbox claims it successfully and books the SAME close a second time into daily stats, breaker daily P&L and streak, and Thompson. W1's 'exactly-once' becomes at-least-twice under the one failure mode that motivated it.

**Evidence:** orchestrator.py:740-742: `try: _claimed = self._state.claim_exit_booking(trade_id)\nexcept Exception:  # noqa: BLE001 — outbox unavailable: book as before\n    _claimed = True`. Executing probe (claim raises OperationalError once, then real): 'P2 after callback: total_pnl=-150.00 trades_lost=1 breaker_daily=-150.00 streak=1 outbox_left=1' then after the drain: 'P2 after drain: total_pnl=-300.00 trades_lost=2 breaker_daily=-300.00 streak=2 thompson_calls=2'.

**Trigger:** An exit fills while status.py/master.py/dashboard holds a write lock on ait_state.db (multiprocess-db-1 family) so the DELETE times out; the next _trading_cycle (>=120s later) double-books. Two such -$150 exits then count as four losses and trip the 3-loss pause on a two-loss day, or a single -$1,100 loss reads as -$2,200 against the 2% daily cap.

**Consequence:** One -$150 stop-out whose claim hits a locked DB is booked twice ~2-5 minutes apart: breaker daily P&L -$300 and streak +2 for one loss, so two such exits trip the 3-loss pause on a two-loss day and a single -1.1% loss reads as -2.2% against the 2% daily cap — a false halt that blocks entries for the rest of the session, with Thompson and daily stats double-counted as well.

**Suggested fix:** On claim exception do NOT book: `log.error('exit_booking_claim_failed_deferred', trade_id=..., error=...)` and `continue` — the outbox row survives and the 120s drain books it exactly once. Executing test: wrap claim to raise once, run _process_completed_exits then _drain_exit_outbox, assert total_pnl==-150, trades_lost==1, breaker daily==-150, streak==1, thompson called once.

## regress-live-04  [regression | live_money | live_path=True]

**Where:** src/ait/risk/circuit_breaker.py:96-112 (_load_persisted fail-closed: tripped, streak 0, daily 0, resume_time now+pause); 167-176 (is_tripped auto-resume -> _consecutive_losses = 0; _persist()); 215-232 (record_trade_result -> _persist()); 142-155 (_persist writes the in-memory zeros over the store)

**Claim:** fail-direction-10's fail-closed load is a 30-minute AUTO-RESUMING pause with zeroed streak/daily P&L, and the first _persist() after the fail-closed load (auto-resume at +30 min, or any record_trade_result before that) overwrites the still-intact real blob with tripped=false / streak 0 / daily 0. A daily_loss or MTM halt (designed pause_seconds=0, no auto-resume until the daily reset) or a 3-loss streak that was merely UNREADABLE for one relaunch is therefore lifted 30 minutes after relaunch and its evidence destroyed for every later restart — the same defeat the fix claims closed, delayed by one pause window.

**Evidence:** Executing probe (store whose first read raises 'database is locked', real blob = daily_loss halt, streak 3, daily -2100, resume_time 0): 'after fail-closed load: tripped=True reason=state_unreadable_fail_closed resume_in_s=1799 streak=0 daily=0' / 'stored blob still real? -> daily_loss (2.1% of $100000)' / after the window: 'is_tripped after pause window: False' / 'stored blob NOW: {"date": "2026-09-03", "consecutive_losses": 0, "daily_pnl": 0.0, "tripped": false, "reason": "", "resume_time": 0.0}' / 'healthy relaunch reads: tripped=False streak=0 daily=0'. Code: circuit_breaker.py:110-112 `self._resume_time = time.time() + max(60.0, pause_minutes*60)`; 171-176 `self._tripped = False ... self._consecutive_losses = 0; self._persist()`.

**Trigger:** Keeper relaunch during a transient bot_state lock (the documented ~30-60 min kill cycle) on a day already under a daily-loss halt: entries resume at relaunch+30 min with daily_pnl=0 in the breaker; the next relaunch reads a clean zero blob.

**Consequence:** A keeper relaunch during a transient bot_state lock lifts the fail-closed trip 30 minutes later and, on the first _persist, overwrites the intact breaker blob: a real 2-loss streak becomes 0 for good (the 3-loss pause then needs three fresh losses), the realized daily-loss gate reads $0 for the rest of the day, and a daily-loss halt survives only because the separate MTM brake re-derives it from daily_stats one tick after resume — the fix that claimed to close fail-direction-10 defers the same erasure by one pause window and then makes it permanent.

**Suggested fix:** On an unreadable load set self._store_unverified=True; make is_tripped re-attempt _load_persisted while that latch is set and lift the fail-closed trip only on a SUCCESSFUL read that shows no active halt (adopting the read streak/daily/trip); make _persist refuse to write while the latch is set (log 'breaker_persist_suppressed_store_unverified'). Executing test: flaky store, advance past resume_time, assert the stored blob is byte-identical and the daily_loss halt is still honored; second test: record_trade_result inside the window does not overwrite the blob.

## regress-live-05  [regression | live_money | live_path=False]

**Where:** src/ait/risk/pdt_guard.py:66-88 (_load_state sets _state_unreadable=True with an EMPTY deque); 110-117 (record_day_trade appends and calls _save_state unconditionally); 93-98 (_save_state overwrites pdt_day_trades with the in-memory deque); config.yaml:26 (pdt_account_under_25k: false -> guard disabled on this account)

**Claim:** fail-direction-09's sticky fail-closed flag protects the CURRENT process, but the first same-day close after a transient unreadable load calls record_day_trade -> _save_state, which overwrites the (still intact) 5-day history with [today's one trade]. The next restart reads that cleanly, clears _state_unreadable, and under-counts the window — the guard converts a transient lock into a permanent history loss and then fails open on the following relaunch. Same shape as regress-live-04.

**Evidence:** pdt_guard.py:80-82 `self._day_trades = deque(); self._state_unreadable = True`; 116-117 `self._day_trades.append((today, symbol)); self._save_state()`; 97-98 `trades = [{...} for d, s in self._day_trades]; self._state.set_state('pdt_day_trades', json.dumps(trades))` — no check of _state_unreadable before the write. Latent only: pdt_guard.py:47 `self._enabled = config.pdt_protection and config.pdt_account_under_25k` and config.yaml:26 sets the second flag false (record_day_trade returns at 112-113 when disabled).

**Trigger:** Live only when pdt_account_under_25k is flipped true (real-money account under $25k): relaunch during a DB lock, one same-day close, next relaunch -> day-trade count 1 instead of 3 -> 4th day trade allowed -> PDT flag.

**Consequence:** Latent today (guard disabled on the $198k paper account); on a sub-$25k live account a relaunch during a transient DB lock followed by one same-day close rewrites the 5-day history to a single entry, and the next relaunch reads it cleanly, clears the fail-closed latch and allows a 4th day trade -> PDT flag, a 90-day restriction that cannot be unwound.

**Suggested fix:** In _save_state, refuse to write while _state_unreadable (log.error 'pdt_history_write_suppressed_store_unverified') or re-read pdt_day_trades and merge before writing; have record_day_trade retry _load_state first so a transient lock can recover. Executing test: flaky store, record_day_trade, assert the stored JSON still contains the original entries; assert a fresh guard counts 4 and blocks.

## regress-live-06  [regression | live_money | live_path=True]

**Where:** src/ait/execution/executor.py:739-743 (_execute_multi_leg step-0 placement anchored to the LIVE mid, no floor check); 1352-1359 (_reprice_pending_entries floor re-check, only on escalation, then parks); 1355-1356 (comment: 'The resting order keeps its current (floor-compliant) price' — false for step 0); 1373-1399 (_credit_floors_hold); src/ait/strategies/iron_condor.py:278-279 (signal-time floor on the SIGNAL mid)

**Claim:** W4's credit-floor re-check (trade-life-credit-econ-floors-signal-time-only) is incomplete: the initial order is priced off the live combo mid minus 25% of the marketable offset with no floor check, so any condor whose signal credit is within ~0.25*max(0.10, 0.15*mid) (~$0.03) of AIT_IC_MIN_CREDIT rests BELOW the floor from the first second and can fill there; the reprice re-check then 'parks' the ladder at that already-breaching resting price. Given the shipped floor 0.70 and typical QQQ credits ~0.72 (executor comment at the R6 cost floor), this is the common case, not the edge.

**Evidence:** Executing probe against the real helpers: 'AIT_IC_MIN_CREDIT floor: 0.7' / 'offset=0.11  step0 limit=-0.68  (|0.68| < floor 0.7 -> True)  step1=-0.64' / '_credit_floors_hold(step0) = False   <- the price the order is RESTING at' / '_credit_floors_hold(step1) = False   <- the only place the floor is re-checked'. Code: executor.py:739-743 `_base_mag = _live_mid_mag if _live_mid_mag else abs(signal.entry_price) ... limit_price = _ladder_limit(_base_mag, aggressive_offset, _start_frac, is_credit)` with no call to _credit_floors_hold; the check exists only at 1352 inside the reprice loop.

**Trigger:** Signal mid 0.72 (passes iron_condor.py:278), live mid 0.71 at placement -> step-0 limit -0.68 -> fills at $0.68 credit, below the $0.70 economics floor that justified the entry; PLAN W4 claims 'Credit floors re-checked at REPRICE; ladder remainder refused on breach' and the code comment claims the resting price is floor-compliant.

**Consequence:** A condor whose signal mid is 0.72 (passes the 0.70 floor) with a live mid of 0.71 at placement is submitted at a $0.68 limit and can fill there — below the economics floor that justified the entry — and the reprice re-check then parks the ladder at that already-breaching price, so the shipped floor is enforced at neither the price the order rests at nor any later step.

**Suggested fix:** In _execute_multi_leg, after computing limit_price for a credit combo, run the same floor check against |limit_price| (min credit and credit/width ratio from the signal legs) and either clamp to -max(min_credit, |limit|) with a log or CAS the trade to CANCELLED and return None; in _reprice_pending_entries clamp new_limit to the floor instead of parking. Executing test: signal 0.72, live combo mid 0.71, floor 0.70 -> placed order lmtPrice magnitude >= 0.70 (fails pre-fix at 0.68).

## regress-live-07  [enforcement_gap | live_money | live_path=True]

**Where:** src/ait/bot/orchestrator.py:2345-2352 (_try_execute cooldown call wrapped in `except Exception: pass`); 2166 (_post_stop_cooldown_until -> _post_stop_cooldown_cutoff()); 84-120 (_post_stop_cooldown_cutoff -> is_trading_day at 112, cache populated only on success); src/ait/utils/time.py:70-74 (is_trading_day -> _nyse.schedule)

**Claim:** W4 replaced a pure-arithmetic 30h window with a market-calendar lookup, but left the caller's pre-existing bare `except Exception: pass`. Any exception from pandas_market_calendars (schedule query failure, holiday-table/pandas incompatibility — the same class fail-direction-09/-12 hardened in PDT and the executor this very wave) now silently disables the R12-B4 post-stop cooldown: fail OPEN with no log line, recurring every scan because the cache is only written on success. Pre-W4 the only failure surface was the read-only sqlite open; W4 added a new one without a fail direction.

**Evidence:** orchestrator.py:2345-2352: `try:\n    _stopped_at = self._post_stop_cooldown_until(signal.symbol)\n    if _stopped_at: ... return False\nexcept Exception:  # noqa: BLE001 — cooldown must never block the loop\n    pass`. orchestrator.py:112 `if is_trading_day(prev):` inside the loop that computes the cutoff; time.py:73 `schedule = _nyse.schedule(start_date=d, end_date=d)`. No log, no page, no fail-closed branch on that path (contrast the same wave's pdt_guard.py:132-136 and executor.py:329-334 which log.error and refuse).

**Trigger:** Calendar library raising on the day after a touch-stop (Monday after a Friday stop-out — the exact 08-24 QQQ re-entry the fix cites) -> symbol re-enters into the same move with nothing in the log.

**Consequence:** If the cooldown query raises for any reason (DB unopenable/locked from a non-trade_v2 cwd or WAL recovery, bad exit_time, date-specific calendar fault), the R12-B4 post-stop cooldown is silently disabled for every symbol on every scan with no log line or page, and a symbol that was touch-stopped re-enters into the same move — the exact 08-24 QQQ pattern the rule exists to prevent — while the executor's own guards (which only check market hours, not cooldown) let the order through.

**Suggested fix:** In _try_execute replace the bare `except Exception: pass` at orchestrator.py:2351 with log.error('post_stop_cooldown_check_failed_entry_refused', symbol=..., error=str(e)) and `return True` (symbol handled, refuse this pass), optionally with an _alert_gate streak page like the blackout gate at :2310-2332; add an executing test (same rig as the probe: real sqlite file, is_trading_day patched to raise, and a second case with the DB unopenable) asserting the entry is refused and the event is logged; consider constructing the ro URI from self._state's db path instead of the relative literal.

## time-calendar-01  [logic | live_money | live_path=True]

**Where:** src/ait/bot/orchestrator.py:3711-3728 (_sessions_until: line 3723 today = datetime.now().date(); 3725-3727 `if (today + timedelta(days=i)).weekday() < 5: sessions += 1`; docstring 3716-3718); consumer src/ait/bot/orchestrator.py:2303-2308 (`_sessions <= _blackout`); config.yaml:59 pre_event_blackout_days: 1; test tests/test_r16_round2_fixes.py:156 (pins only _sessions_until(3)==0)

**Claim:** The live pre-event blackout converts calendar days to sessions by counting WEEKDAYS, so every NYSE holiday is counted as a trading session; the count is inflated across a holiday and the gate under-blocks (fails OPEN), while its own docstring asserts the opposite ('erring toward MORE sessions is the fail-safe direction for a gate that blocks entries') — more sessions makes `_sessions <= _blackout` LESS likely, i.e. fewer blocks.

**Evidence:** Executed with datetime.now pinned to Fri 2026-09-04 10:00 (Labor Day 09-07 is a holiday per pandas_market_calendars 5.3.1.2, is_trading_day(2026-09-07)=False): `Fri 09-04 -> event 2026-09-09 (Wed) d2e=5: _sessions_until=2 true_sessions_between=1`; `-> 2026-09-10 (Thu) d2e=6: _sessions_until=3 true_sessions_between=2`. With blackout=1 the gate at :2304 blocks when sessions<=1: a Wednesday-after-Labor-Day event is NOT blocked from the preceding Friday although exactly one real session (Tue) separates them. Code: `if (today + timedelta(days=i)).weekday() < 5: sessions += 1` — no is_trading_day call, even though the same module already imports it for the cooldown cutoff (orchestrator.py:102,112).

**Trigger:** A credit entry on the last session before any NYSE holiday when a table event falls on the second session after the holiday (e.g. Fri before a Monday holiday, event Wednesday; Wed before Thanksgiving, event the following Monday). No event in the currently loaded 2026/2027-H1 tables sits in that shape, so this is an enforcement gap today, not a live divergence.

**Consequence:** On the last session before any NYSE holiday, a credit (iron_condor) entry ahead of an event on the second real session after the holiday passes the pre-event blackout with 2 counted sessions when only 1 real session separates entry from the event — the gate under-blocks (fails OPEN) in exactly the direction its docstring claims it cannot. Latent today: no loaded event through 2027-06-25 sits in that shape, but Labor Day/Thanksgiving/Presidents Day + Wed/Mon event recurs every year.

**Suggested fix:** In _sessions_until count `is_trading_day(today + timedelta(days=i))` (function-local import from ait.utils.time, like :102) for i in 1..d2e-1 (strictly between), fix the docstring to say holidays must NOT be counted because more sessions weakens the block, and add an executing test with datetime pinned to 2026-09-04 asserting _sessions_until(5)==1 (blocked at blackout=1) and 2026-11-25 -> d2e=5 == 1.

## time-calendar-02  [enforcement_gap | live_money | live_path=True]

**Where:** src/ait/bot/orchestrator.py:2345-2352 (caller: `except Exception:  # noqa: BLE001 — cooldown must never block the loop` / `pass`); src/ait/bot/orchestrator.py:84-119 (_post_stop_cooldown_cutoff -> is_trading_day at :102,:112, NYSE calendar dependency added by W4); src/ait/bot/orchestrator.py:2151 (`sqlite3.connect("file:data/ait_state.db?mode=ro", uri=True)`), :2161-2166; contrast: src/ait/risk/pdt_guard.py:131-147 and orchestrator.py:2310-2320 (calendar failures fail CLOSED + logged)

**Claim:** W4 made the R12-B4 post-stop cooldown depend on the NYSE calendar (pandas_market_calendars) and on a fresh read-only sqlite connection, but the gate's caller still swallows every exception with a bare `pass` and emits no log line — a calendar error or an SQLITE_BUSY window turns the cooldown into a silent fail-OPEN gate, the exact class W2 closed for the sibling calendar-dependent gates (PDT fail-direction-09, executor market-hours fail-direction-12, pre-event blackout).

**Evidence:** Executed against a real sqlite file holding a QQQ 'short_strike_touch' close at 2026-09-04T10:00 with _cooldown_now pinned to Tue 2026-09-08 10:00: `healthy calendar -> blocked (exit_time returned): 2026-09-04T10:00:00`; with ait.utils.time.is_trading_day patched to raise: `calendar exception -> RAISES RuntimeError pmc schedule failed` and the replicated caller block printed `entry blocked = False (no log line emitted)`; with sqlite3.connect raising OperationalError('database is locked'): `locked db -> RAISES OperationalError -> caller except-pass -> entry allowed`. Caller source: `except Exception:  # noqa: BLE001 — cooldown must never block the loop\n            pass` (orchestrator.py:2351-2352). Neither reports register covers this except-pass (fail-direction-08 covers the 120-min GUARD #2 in _duplicate_guard_verdict, not the R12-B4 gate).

**Trigger:** Any exception inside _post_stop_cooldown_until during a scan: pandas_market_calendars schedule failure, a locked/busy data/ait_state.db (dashboard/status/mirror readers are documented concurrent users), a malformed exit_time string (fromisoformat at :2161) — on a symbol stopped out within the last session, e.g. re-entering QQQ the session after a touch stop, the 08-24 shape R21/W4 were built to prevent.

**Consequence:** Any exception inside the R12-B4 cooldown check (calendar fault on the day's first cutoff computation, a busy/locked ait_state.db read, a restated/malformed exit_time) silently converts the post-stop cooldown into a fail-OPEN gate: the bot re-enters the symbol it was just stopped out of (the 08-24 QQQ shape) with no log line and no page — the exact class W2 closed for every sibling gate.

**Suggested fix:** Replace the bare pass with the blackout gate's pattern: log.error('post_stop_cooldown_check_failed_failing_closed', symbol=..., error=...) and return False for this pass, with a _blackout-style streak counter that pages after 3 consecutive failures; add executing tests that patch ait.utils.time.is_trading_day to raise and sqlite3.connect to raise OperationalError and assert _try_execute refuses the entry and emits the log event.

## concurrency-04-drain-unreachable-windows-make-outbox-a-one-day-ttl  [logic | research_validity | live_path=True]

**Where:** src/ait/bot/orchestrator.py:1094-1113 (_trading_cycle returns while breaker tripped, before the drain), :1149-1151 (returns in the last 15 min: scheduler.py:114-120 `0 < remaining < 900`), :1159 (the ONLY call to _drain_exit_outbox; none in run() :455-544, _pre_market :552-627, _monitor_positions_fast :878-978, _post_market :3357-3519), :686-691 (stale skip: `if exit_d != today: ... continue` BEFORE stats, breaker, trade_maxloss delete, PDT and Thompson); post-market reconciler closes: orchestrator.py:3402 -> reconciler.py:1170/1227/855 -> state.close_trade enqueues

**Claim:** The drain runs only in _trading_cycle after two early returns, never in the fast monitor, pre-market, post-market or startup; combined with the prior-day skip, any orphan enqueued after the day's last reachable cycle (15:45-16:00, the 16:00+ post-market reconcile, a day the breaker stays tripped, or an R18-class cycle that raises before step 6b) is claimed the next morning and discarded: it never reaches daily_stats of its day, Thompson (lifetime arm state), PDT (5-business-day rolling window), or the trade_maxloss_ delete. The W1 PLAN entry claims reconciler closes now 'reach daily stats, the circuit breaker, PDT, and Thompson'; they do only when enqueued before 15:45 on a healthy, untripped day.

**Evidence:** Executed probe: close enqueued with exit_time = yesterday 15:50 (an EOD reconciler booking), drain today -> `stale_exit_booking_skipped exit_time=2026-09-02T15:50:00 pnl=-272.29`; `outbox after drain: []`; `daily_stats(today).total_pnl: 0.0 | daily_stats(yesterday).total_pnl: 0.0`; `breaker daily_pnl: 0.0 | thompson.record_outcome calls: 0 | pdt.record_day_trade calls: 0`; `trade_maxloss_T-D still present: 800`. Code order in _trading_cycle: `if self._circuit_breaker.is_tripped: ... return` (:1094-1113) and `if self._scheduler.should_avoid_new_trades(): ... return` (:1149-1151) both precede `await self._drain_exit_outbox()` (:1159). grep '_drain_exit_outbox' -> definition :641 and the single call :1159. Live DB: 5 of 25 closes have exit_time outside 09:35-15:45 (e.g. stale_pending_never_filled at 2026-07-06T15:58:40), and every _post_market reconcile booking lands after 16:00 by construction.

**Trigger:** Post-market reconcile books an ITM expiry (reconciler_expired_intrinsic) or a position the operator flattened in TWS at 14:00 that the monitor never wanted to exit (reconciler_ibkr_realized) at 16:0x; or a crash-lost executor booking after 15:45 (the 08-31 restart was at 15:50); or any close discovered during a daily-loss halt (pause_seconds=0, no auto-resume) -> next morning: claimed + skipped. Thompson never learns the outcome, the DAILY SUMMARY and daily_stats of that day exclude it, PDT undercounts a same-day round trip for five sessions (pdt_protection: true in config.yaml:25).

**Consequence:** Any real-P&L close enqueued after ~15:45 (post-market reconciler ITM-expiry or manual-flatten bookings, a vanished-position booking in the last 15 minutes), or any close discovered on a day the breaker is tripped, is claimed the next morning and discarded: it never enters daily_stats of its day (DAILY SUMMARY / MTM brake realized term wrong), Thompson never learns the outcome (lifetime arm state), and PDT undercounts a same-day round trip for its 5-session window.

**Suggested fix:** Call _drain_exit_outbox from _post_market (before the DAILY SUMMARY) and from _monitor_positions_fast, and move it ahead of the tripped/last-15-min returns in _trading_cycle. For prior-day rows book into get_daily_stats(exit_d) (or skip only the breaker) but always record Thompson/PDT. Executing test: enqueue at 15:50 ET, run the post-market path, assert booked; enqueue while tripped, run a cycle, assert booked.

## concurrency-05-drain-and-callback-book-different-side-effects  [regression | research_validity | live_path=True]

**Where:** src/ait/bot/orchestrator.py:668-723 (_drain_exit_outbox booking body) vs :725-876 (_process_completed_exits): the drain omits the commission true-up :749-780, the trueup_pending marker :770-775 (so _post_market :3381-3397 never revisits), update_trade_commission :830-836, drift record :846-865 and _exit_attempts.pop :813; race sites: executor.py:1194 (close_trade commit inside check_fills) ... :817 (`await cb(exits)` after up to 3 rerun passes with 8s broker awaits) vs orchestrator.py:1159 drain with a 120s grace (state.py:773-786); loser path :744-745 `log.warning('exit_booking_already_claimed'); continue` skips ALL of its side effects

**Claim:** Which task wins the outbox claim decides the booking semantics of a close. The drain (always the booker for lost callbacks and for closes whose callback claims >120s after the commit) books the fill-time flat $0.65/leg estimate, never stamps trades.commission, never sets trueup_pending, never records drift; the callback path does all of these. So the very closes W1 set out to recover ('bookings lost to a crash') land in the ledger with the pre-R15 commission estimate permanently, and a callback that loses the race abandons its own true-up.

**Evidence:** Executed probe, identical seed (iron_condor qty 1, 8 execution rows at the W5-measured 0.9168/contract-leg, executor-style close with the $5.20 estimate embedded): callback path -> `realized_pnl_commission_trueup delta=-2.13 real_comm=7.33` -> trades = ('closed', 1.9, 1, 62.67, 7.3344); drain path -> `orphan_close_booked pnl=64.8` -> trades = ('closed', 1.9, 1, 64.8, 0.0), trueup_pending: []. orchestrator.py:744-745: `if not _claimed: log.warning('exit_booking_already_claimed', ...); continue` — the commission stamp at :830-836 is after that continue. go_live.py:328-329 builds the verdict from `r['realized_pnl']` directly, so drain-booked rows carry the $2.13/contract error into the IC PF.

**Trigger:** (a) Any executor close whose callback is lost: an exception in a rerun pass (executor.py:819 event_fill_check_failed discards `exits`), or a kill after the close_trade commit at executor.py:1194 — exactly the concurrency-2 case W1 claims to close. (b) A callback that reaches its claim >120s after the commit: check_fills_safe loops up to 4 passes (executor.py:865-873) each able to await _broker_open_order_ids (8s timeout) and _confirm_order_not_working for OTHER orders against a slow Gateway, while the main task's cycle drain at :1159 runs; the callback then hits :744 and skips its true-up/commission stamp/drift. Result: realized_pnl off by 5.20-7.33 = $2.13 per condor contract and trades.commission = 0 on those rows; direction_correct/drift never recorded for directional trades.

**Consequence:** Rows booked by the drain (lost executor callbacks) carry realized_pnl overstated by (real commission - $5.20 flat estimate) ≈ $2.13 per condor contract and trades.commission = 0.0 forever, feeding the go-live IC profit-factor verdict and the 'commissions recorded' line; drift accuracy and exit-attempt backoff are also never recorded for drain-booked closes.

**Suggested fix:** Factor the booking body into one function both paths call, taking a flag for whether the P&L embeds the fill-time estimate (executor closes: true up against the ledger or set trueup_pending; reconciler closes: stamp commission only). Executing test: the same executor close booked via callback and via drain yields identical trades/daily_stats rows.

## data-integrity-02  [logic | research_validity | live_path=True]

**Where:** src/ait/bot/orchestrator.py:1846-1857 (eff_conf constant in observe mode), :2244 (_try_execute(signal, confidence, ...)), :2567-2570 (save_trade_context confidence=confidence); src/ait/execution/executor.py:382 (ml_confidence=signal.confidence); src/ait/ml/meta_label.py:520 (primary_confidence = entry_confidence); data/ait_state.db trade_context rows T-20260901-105959-c4ee1b, T-20260901-110001-944d47

**Claim:** Since the 09-01 observe-mode commit (43d0409), trade_context.entry_confidence is written as the config constant ml.observe_mode_neutral_confidence (0.60) for every direction-neutral entry, while trades.ml_confidence for the same trade keeps the directional model number; the meta-labeler's primary_confidence feature therefore becomes a constant for all observe-mode condors while earlier rows carry a real model probability.

**Evidence:** Executed rows: T-20260901-105959-c4ee1b trades.ml_confidence=0.3786956 vs trade_context.entry_confidence=0.6; T-20260901-110001-944d47 0.2802374 vs 0.6. Pre-observe rows agree (T-20260824-100040-f1d18e 0.8977734 in BOTH columns; T-20260817-141950 0.5448729 both). Code: orchestrator.py:1848-1850 `elif (not self._settings.ml.entry_gates_enabled and signal.strategy_name in RANGE_GATED_STRATEGIES): eff_conf = float(self._settings.ml.observe_mode_neutral_confidence)`; :1873 `_try_execute(signal, eff_conf, ...)`; :2569 `confidence=confidence` into save_trade_context; executor.py:382 `ml_confidence=signal.confidence`; meta_label.py:520 `"primary_confidence": r.get("entry_confidence", 0)`. config.yaml ml.observe_mode_neutral_confidence: 0.60 (PLAN 2026-09-01).

**Trigger:** Every iron_condor/short_strangle entry while ml.entry_gates_enabled=false (the current operating mode); the meta-labeler will train on a feature that is 0.60 for the whole observe-mode sample and model-derived before it.

**Consequence:** For the whole observe-mode sample — the only sample the project is now accumulating, IC-only — trade_context.entry_confidence is the literal config constant 0.60 while trades.ml_confidence for the same trade holds the model's number (0.3787 and 0.2802 on the two 09-01 entries). The meta-labeler's most important feature, primary_confidence, therefore becomes zero-variance for every post-09-01 row and model-derived for every earlier one: when meta_label is regenerated per its documented procedure (meta_label.py:55-67) the feature is either useless or, worse, a perfect proxy for 'was this trade taken after 2026-09-01', which the classifier can latch onto. Any later analyst comparing the two columns has two different 'entry confidences' for one trade with nothing recording which is which.

**Suggested fix:** Journal the model's own number: pass signal.confidence (and p_in_range when the range model produced one) into save_trade_context, and if the gating baseline is worth keeping, store it in a separate column (e.g. entry_gate_confidence) with the mode that produced it. Add an executing test that runs an observe-mode entry through the real path and asserts trade_context.entry_confidence == trades.ml_confidence for the same trade_id (it fails today on both 09-01 rows).

## data-integrity-03  [enforcement_gap | research_validity | live_path=False]

**Where:** src/ait/bot/orchestrator.py:1683-1693 (range_prediction log.info only when range_pred truthy), :3062-3090 (_entry_signals_json writes META_FEATURES only); config.yaml:258-259 (max_bytes 20MB, backup_count 10); PLAN.md 2026-09-01 OBSERVE MODE PRE-REGISTRATION ('compare closes where p_in_range was above vs below 0.65'); data/ait_state.db trade_context.entry_signals for the 09-01 rows

**Claim:** The pre-registered observe-mode judgment ('at 50 closes compare closes with p_in_range above vs below 0.65') cannot be executed from any store: p_in_range is never persisted, is only log.info'd when predict() returns a prediction (it returns None for SPY/QQQ, so nothing is emitted for them at all), and ait.log retention is ~8 trading days.

**Evidence:** entry_signals for T-20260901-105959-c4ee1b = {atr_pct, bb_position, hour_of_day, iv_rank, macd_hist, price_vs_sma_20, realized_vol_20, rsi_14, rsi_7, sma_10_20_cross, volume_confirmation, volume_sma_20_ratio, weekly_trend_aligned} — no range field; same for T-20260901-110001-944d47 and T-20260824-100040-f1d18e. orchestrator.py:1689 `if range_pred:` guards the only p_in_range emission (`log.info("range_prediction", ..., p_in_range=...)`); grep 'probability_in_range' in src/ait/bot shows no DB write. PLAN 09-01: 'predict() returns None for SPY and QQQ'; 09-01 log shows 16 range_gate_unavailable_entries_blocked and 0 range_prediction lines for those symbols. Oldest retained ait.log.10 line: 2026-08-24T15:09:08Z (10 x 20MB rotation; config comment: 'held ~20 min under IBKR error spam').

**Trigger:** Any attempt to run the pre-registered evaluation after more than ~8 trading days of logs have rotated (i.e. at 50 closes); for SPY/QQQ the number does not exist even in logs today.

**Consequence:** The pre-registered go/no-go for observe mode cannot be executed as written. At 50 closes the analyst must join p_in_range to each close; that number is nowhere in the databases, exists in logs for at most ~8 trading days of a multi-month accumulation, and for SPY/QQQ — 2 of the 3 universe symbols and both entries taken since the decision — was never emitted at all, with no record distinguishing 'model had no skill' from 'model was never asked'. The range model has already been retrained since (models/range.pkl trained_at 2026-09-05T20:52:47), so the values cannot be reconstructed after the fact either. The decision that authorized trading with gates off becomes unfalsifiable.

**Suggested fix:** Persist the range probability per entry regardless of gate state: add p_in_range (plus threshold/horizon and, when predict() returns None, an explicit NULL with the reason — no_skill / untrained / not_attempted) to trade_context or the entry_signals payload, written for every direction-neutral candidate that is executed. Add an executing test asserting an observe-mode entry journals the range probability (or the null-reason), and mirror the same field on the backtest side so the pre-registered comparison can run from the ledger instead of the logs.

## data-integrity-04  [bug | research_validity | live_path=True]

**Where:** data/historical.db intraday_prices (SPY/QQQ/IWM, source='TRADES'); src/ait/bot/orchestrator.py:1369-1378 (intraday persisted only inside _scan_symbol) and :1210-1215 (economic_event_skip returns before the universe scan); src/ait/data/market_data.py:319-324 (elapsed_days -> duration), :394-397 (_days_to_ibkr_duration '<=14 -> f"{days} D"'), :429-433 (endDateTime='', useRTH=True); src/ait/ml/features.py:326-403 (session-shape features from load_intraday)

**Claim:** The intraday store is systematically missing the final 25-45 minutes of 37.5% of IBKR-era sessions (15 of 40 per symbol) and has no bars at all for 09-02/09-03/09-04, because bars are persisted only as a side effect of the entry scan and the next-morning incremental fetch asks IBKR for '1 D' ending now (current session only), so a prior session's tail is never recovered unless a weekend/outage makes the request >=2 D; the session-shape ML features are computed on these truncated 'closes'.

**Evidence:** Executed per-day probe (SPY, source=TRADES): TRUNC 07-08 last 17:30 (49 bars); 07-15/07-16/07-20/07-21/07-22 last 19:25 (72); 08-03 19:45; 08-04 19:25; 08-17 19:20; 08-18 19:20; 08-19 19:15 (70); 08-20 19:25; 08-24 19:25; 08-31 19:30 (73); 09-01 19:20 (71) — every truncated day is followed by a stored day 1 calendar day later; all 10 Fridays (next fetch after a 3-day gap) end at 19:55 with 78 bars. QQQ/IWM identical (25/40 complete). No bars exist for 2026-09-02 (bot down), 09-03, 09-04 (economic_event_skip x70/x69 for 'NFP jobs report 2026-09-04' returned at orchestrator.py:1215 before `for symbol in universe: _scan_symbol`, so :1377 save_intraday never ran; last bar 2026-09-01T19:20Z). market_data.py:321 `elapsed_days = max(1, int((now - since_utc).total_seconds()/86400)+1)` -> 18h gap => 1 => '1 D'; :429 `endDateTime=""`. features.py:378-403 compute power_hour_momentum/closing_imbalance/closing_range_position from the last bars of each session; range.pkl symbol_models lgb split counts: QQQ closing_imbalance 22, SPY closing_range_position 10, i.e. the model consumes them.

**Trigger:** Every Mon-Thu session whose last entry scan happens before 15:55 ET (the normal case; entry scanning stops around 15:15-15:30) and every blackout/halt/outage day; recovery happens only when the next request spans >=2 calendar days.

**Consequence:** For 15 of 40 stored sessions per symbol the model's 'close' is 15:15-15:25 ET, so closing_imbalance, closing_range_position, power_hour_momentum/vol_accel/vwap_cross and the session-shape family are computed on a stump that omits the highest-volume 25-45 minutes of the day — permanently, since nothing ever re-requests a past session's tail. The range model that gates iron condors trains and serves on that mixture (25 honest sessions, 15 stumps, with no flag distinguishing them), and the deficit is not random: it is exactly the days the bot stopped scanning early. On top of that the store has no bars at all for 09-02 (outage) or 09-03/09-04 (macro blackout returned before the scan), so blackout days are invisible to every session-shape feature and to the 2-year fractal window.

**Suggested fix:** Request max(elapsed_days, 2) D in get_intraday_since (or anchor endDateTime to the prior session close) so a missed tail is always re-fetched, and backfill the 15 truncated sessions now — IBKR still serves 5-min bars 7 days back for the recent ones. Decouple persistence from entry gating: move the intraday save into the 30s monitor loop or a post-close job that runs even on blackout/halt days. Add a store-continuity check to the data validators (expected RTH bar count per session and no missing sessions vs the trading calendar) and have _merge_intraday_features skip or flag sessions whose last bar is not within one bar of the close.

## data-integrity-05  [enforcement_gap | research_validity | live_path=True]

**Where:** src/ait/bot/orchestrator.py:1603-1609 (_persist_daily_iv called only after the chain fetch inside _scan_symbol), :3730-3758 (_persist_daily_iv; empty `ivs` -> silent return at :3745-3748), :3781-3800 (iv_rank reads ONLY daily_prices.implied_vol; `_age_days > 5` -> proxy); :1210-1215 (blackout return); src/ait/data/historical.py:104-128, :505-630 (daily_iv/intraday_iv tables); scripts/backfill_historical_data.py:333,:412 (only writers); data/historical.db daily_prices.implied_vol + daily_iv/intraday_iv

**Claim:** Of the two IV stores, daily_iv and intraday_iv are EMPTY (0 rows, no production writer or reader) and daily_prices.implied_vol — the only series iv_rank reads — is refreshed only as a side effect of a completed entry scan, so it has holes on every non-scanning day and goes stale past the 5-day gate: rows exist for 08-17..08-25 then only 09-01 (missing 08-26/27/28, 08-31, 09-02, 09-03, 09-04); on 08-31 every iv_rank ran on the realized-vol proxy, and on 09-08 the 7-day-old head trips iv_rank_store_stale again.

**Evidence:** Executed: `select symbol,count(*) from daily_iv` -> no rows; intraday_iv -> no rows; grep shows save_daily_iv_bars/save_intraday_iv_bars called only from scripts/backfill_historical_data.py:333/412 and load_*_iv_bars called from nowhere. daily_prices implied_vol non-NULL dates since 08-14 for SPY/QQQ/IWM: 08-17,18,19,20,21,24,25,09-01 (bot_state iv_saved_SPY/QQQ/IWM = '2026-09-01'); daily_prices OHLCV rows exist for 09-02 and 09-03 with implied_vol NULL. Logs 08-31: 684 atm_iv_unavailable (tag no_iv_data), 57 iv_rank_store_stale ('latest 2026-08-25, age_days 6'), 57 iv_rank_realized_proxy, 0 daily_iv_persisted (code path :3745 `ivs=[...]; if not ivs: return` logs nothing). 09-03/09-04 ait.log: 0 scan_symbol_timing/intraday_saved/chain_source/daily_iv_persisted events, 70/69 economic_event_skip. orchestrator.py:3783-3785 `SELECT date, implied_vol FROM daily_prices WHERE symbol=? AND implied_vol IS NOT NULL ...`; :3789-3797 age>5 -> rows=[] -> proxy. Adjacent to research-to-live-05 (two definitions in one column, the 07-10..08-16 hole); this is the persistence-coupling mechanism that keeps reopening holes and the empty parallel store.

**Trigger:** Any day the universe scan does not reach the chain fetch (macro blackout, halt file, circuit-breaker pause, hour blocked, outage) or chains lack atm_iv (08-31); first scan of each symbol on 2026-09-08 (head 09-01 is 7 days old > 5).

**Consequence:** The IV series that every live iv_rank is computed from only advances on days the entry scan reaches the chain fetch, so a macro blackout (09-03, 09-04), an outage (09-02, 08-26..08-30) or a chain with no atm_iv (08-31, 684 events) permanently loses that session — with no log line, no alert and no backfill. Once the head passes 5 days old the gate silently switches every symbol's iv_rank from an implied-vol percentile to a realized-vol proxy: that already happened on 08-31 (57 iv_rank_store_stale / 57 realized_proxy — every entry decision that day was made on realized vol) and happens again on the next session, 09-08 (head 09-01, age 7). Meanwhile two schema'd IV tables sit at 0 rows with no production reader, so the repo looks like it has an IV store it does not have.

**Suggested fix:** Persist chain ATM IV from a path that runs even when entries are gated — the 30s monitor loop or a dedicated once-per-session job — instead of as a side effect of _scan_symbol, and log/page (not silently return) when a session ends with no IV persisted, including the empty-ivs branch at :3745. Add an IV-row-per-session continuity check to the data validators. Either wire daily_iv/intraday_iv into a scheduled backfill and make iv_rank read one consistent series, or delete the two empty tables and their unused loaders so the store's shape stops implying a capability that does not exist.

## docs-policy-01  [logic | research_validity | live_path=True]

**Where:** PLAN.md:1718-1722 (pre-registered judging rule); PLAN.md:1698, config.yaml:194-196 ("models keep predicting and logging"); src/ait/bot/orchestrator.py:1682-1693 (range_prediction is log.info only); src/ait/bot/orchestrator.py:2580 + :3062-3090 (_entry_signals_json persists only META_FEATURES); src/ait/bot/state.py:166-176 (trade_context schema); src/ait/ml/range_predictor.py:476-480 (predict() returns None at log.debug when edge < min_edge)

**Claim:** The observe-mode pre-registration's judging rule ("at 50 closes ... compare closes where p_in_range was above vs below 0.65") cannot be executed: the range prediction at entry is not recorded per trade anywhere, and for SPY/QQQ (the symbols the decision was about) nothing is logged at all because predict() returns None before the range_prediction log line. The pre-registered evaluation is impossible with what the bot records today.

**Evidence:** Live DB probe (read-only): trade_context columns = trade_id, entry_direction, entry_confidence, entry_regime, entry_vix, entry_iv_rank, entry_sentiment_score, entry_signals, model_version; no table has a range/p_in_range column. entry_signals for the two observe-mode trades (T-20260901-105959 SPY, T-20260901-110001 QQQ) = the 13 META_FEATURES keys only; model_version = the DIRECTION ensemble's 'v-20260901-082214'. grep of src/scripts/status.py/web_logs.py for p_in_range|range_prediction: only the producer (orchestrator.py:1690) and range_predictor.py — no scorer exists. Log evidence 09-01: range_prediction lines = IWM only (8+18+18+18 across ait.log..ait.log.3, horizon 9); range_no_edge for SPY (+0.046 vs 0.050) and QQQ (+0.046) at DEBUG (range_predictor.py:478) — so the two trades actually taken under observe mode have no p_in_range anywhere. The only record is logs/ait.log rotating 10x20MB: ait.log.10 dated Aug 24 (~12 days retention; 09-01 alone consumed 4 files), while go_live pace = 1.2 verdict closes/wk -> '~32 wk to 50'. Also the serving range model is rebuilt at every boot (range_models_saved horizon 9 daily, see docs-policy-09), so even the model that produced a prediction is unrecoverable.

**Trigger:** Any observe-mode close: when the 50-close review arrives, the 'above vs below 0.65' comparison has no per-trade input; today it already has zero rows for SPY/QQQ and the two open observe-mode condors.

**Consequence:** The 2026-09-01 observe-mode pre-registration cannot be executed as written. At 50 closes there is no stored p_in_range to split on: SPY/QQQ never produce one (predict() bails at the edge gate before computing it), and IWM's only record is an INFO line in a log that rotates out in ~12 days with no join key to a trade. The stated purpose of observe mode — making the ML gate's value MEASURABLE rather than assumed, so it is either re-armed on evidence or retired — silently degrades to un-auditable faith, and the risk explicitly accepted (entries the gate would have blocked may lose money) is paid without buying the measurement it was paid for.

**Suggested fix:** In orchestrator._try_execute, persist the range evidence per trade regardless of ml.entry_gates_enabled: p_in_range, range_min_confidence, threshold_pct, horizon_days, range model artifact version, and the no-skill case (weighted_edge, min_edge threshold, predicted=None) — either as new trade_context columns or as reserved keys merged into _entry_signals_json. Make predict() compute and return the probability with a `no_edge` flag instead of returning None (let the caller decide to veto), so the observed quantity exists for the symbols observe mode was created for; promote range_no_edge to INFO. Add a scorer that joins trades.exit_reason/realized_pnl to the stored prediction and reports the above-vs-below-0.65 split. Pin with an executing test that a condor entered with entry_gates_enabled=False carries a non-null prediction row (fails today).

## docs-policy-05  [hardcoded | research_validity | live_path=True]

**Where:** src/ait/bot/orchestrator.py:253-256 (RangePredictor(threshold_pct, horizon_days) only); src/ait/ml/range_predictor.py:83 (min_edge_over_baseline: float = 0.05 ctor default) and :476 (threshold used by predict); config.yaml:156 (backtest.min_edge_over_baseline 'Min range-model CV edge to activate the gate'); src/ait/orchestration/master.py:830 (same); PLAN.md:1687,1700; src/ait/backtesting/walkforward.py:226 (research resolves it from config)

**Claim:** The threshold that decided observe mode (edge +0.046 < 0.05 -> predict() None -> W2 fail-closed gate blocked every IC entry) is a code literal on the live path; config.yaml's backtest.min_edge_over_baseline is read only by research. PLAN's pre-registration treats 'lowering min_edge_over_baseline 0.05->0.04' as a config lever it rejected — that lever does not exist for the live gate. This is the R19b precedence-hole class (config claims a trading value the live code ignores) and a live-vs-research fork.

**Evidence:** Executed probe: `s = load_settings(); rp = RangePredictor(*live_range_spec(s))` -> `config backtest.min_edge_over_baseline = 0.05`, `live RangePredictor._min_edge_over_baseline = 0.05 (ctor default)`; after `s.backtest.min_edge_over_baseline = 0.04` a fresh predictor built the orchestrator's way still reports 0.05. grep min_edge_over_baseline in src: consumers are engine.py:142/285/746, walkforward.py:127/226/1427/1754, optimizer.py:120/211 — no orchestrator/main/trainer site. Neither register mentions min_edge.

**Trigger:** Any config edit to backtest.min_edge_over_baseline; with entry_gates_enabled re-armed, this literal alone decides whether all IC entries are refused fail-closed.

**Consequence:** The threshold that produced the observe-mode decision is a code literal the operating config cannot move. Today it is what makes predict() return None for SPY and QQQ (edge +0.046 < 0.050), which is why those two symbols log no range_prediction at all — so the same literal is also the direct cause of the observe-mode experiment having no data (docs-policy-01). It is a live-vs-research fork: any walk-forward or Optuna run that adopts a different min_edge is validating a gate the live bot does not run. And when entry_gates_enabled is re-armed, this un-configurable 0.05 alone decides whether every IC entry on SPY/QQQ is refused fail-closed (orchestrator.py:1719-1731) — an operator editing config.yaml to fix that would change nothing and see no error.

**Suggested fix:** Pass the config value at the live construction site: `RangePredictor(threshold_pct=_range_threshold, horizon_days=_range_horizon, min_edge_over_baseline=self._settings.backtest.min_edge_over_baseline)` at orchestrator.py:253-256 — or, better, resolve it inside the ctor with the same resolve_config_value helper already used there for min_training_samples, so every construction site (including the retrain subprocess script embedded at master.py:830, which additionally still hardcodes threshold 0.05 / horizon 30) inherits it. Add it to the R19 config-authority test file (tests/test_r19_config_authority.py) and pin with an executing test that setting backtest.min_edge_over_baseline=0.04 changes the live predictor's effective threshold — that test fails today.

## hardcoded-live-01-observe-mode-judgment-variable-not-persisted  [doc_vs_code | research_validity | live_path=True]

**Where:** src/ait/bot/orchestrator.py:1682-1704 (range predict + log only inside `if range_pred:`), :1845-1857 (eff_conf = ml.observe_mode_neutral_confidence), :2567-2582 (save_trade_context(confidence=confidence)); src/ait/bot/state.py:711-741 (trade_context.entry_confidence); src/ait/ml/range_predictor.py:466-481 (no-edge gate returns None before any probability is computed); config.yaml:245-256 (observe decision), :258-259 (log retention 10x20MB); PLAN.md '2026-09-01 - OBSERVE MODE PRE-REGISTRATION' + 'EXECUTED' entries; data/ait_state.db trade_context (read-only probe)

**Claim:** The pre-registered observe-mode judgment ('compare closes where p_in_range was above vs below 0.65') has no durable data source: every observe-mode iron-condor entry journals the config constant 0.60 as entry_confidence, the raw p_in_range is written only to ait.log (which retains roughly two sessions), and for SPY/QQQ — the two symbols the decision was about — predict() returns None at the min-edge gate before any probability exists, so no range_prediction line is emitted for them at all.

**Evidence:** DB probe (2026-09-03, mode=ro): T-20260901-105959-c4ee1b SPY iron_condor entry_confidence=0.6; T-20260901-110001-944d47 QQQ iron_condor entry_confidence=0.6 (trade_context columns: entry_confidence, entry_vix, entry_iv_rank, entry_signals... no range-probability column). Log probe over all retained logs/ait.log*: 'range_prediction' lines dated 2026-09-01 = 62, ALL symbol IWM, zero SPY/QQQ; since 09-01: 128 'range_no_edge' events; retained files cover only Aug 24, Aug 25, Sep 1 and Sep 3 (ait.log.1-.4 rotated within 2026-09-01 09:49-15:05). Code: range_predictor.py:478-481 `log.debug("range_no_edge", symbol=symbol, weighted_edge=..., threshold=...); return None` sits BEFORE `features = self._feature_engine.compute(...)`; orchestrator.py:1689-1693 `if range_pred: log.info("range_prediction", ... p_in_range=...)`; :1849 `eff_conf = float(self._settings.ml.observe_mode_neutral_confidence)`; :2567-2570 `self._state.save_trade_context(trade_id=trade_id, direction=..., confidence=confidence, ...)`. PLAN.md 09-01 pre-registration: 'The ML models keep predicting and logging (evidence accumulates) but never veto' and 'every logged range_prediction is scored against the realized outcome, so at 50 closes we can ask whether the gate WOULD have helped (compare closes where p_in_range was above vs below 0.65)'.

**Trigger:** Every observe-mode IC entry from 2026-09-01 onward; at the 50-close review no SPY/QQQ close can be assigned an entry-time p_in_range (none was ever produced), and IWM's values will have rotated out of the logs weeks earlier.

**Consequence:** The pre-registered observe-mode verdict ('compare closes where p_in_range was above vs below 0.65') cannot be executed as written: for SPY/QQQ (the symbols the decision was about) no entry-time probability was ever produced because predict() exits at the min-edge gate; for IWM the value exists only in rotating logs and will be gone weeks before the 50th close; the nightly-overwritten range.pkl makes offline reconstruction impossible. At the review the only honest answer will be 'unmeasurable', so the gate can be neither re-armed nor retired on evidence.

**Suggested fix:** In _scan_symbol, when ml.entry_gates_enabled is false call predict(..., min_edge_override=-1.0) (the kwarg already exists at range_predictor.py:445) to obtain the raw probability, and record per entry: p_in_range (or None), weighted edge, and the gate's counterfactual verdict (would_block: no_edge/untrained/below_threshold/pass) into trade_context — a new column or keys inside entry_signals JSON — keeping entry_confidence as the risk-validated number. Add an executing test: an observe-mode IC entry on a no-edge symbol writes a numeric p_in_range and a verdict string. Optionally persist every range_prediction to a small table (symbol, ts, p, threshold, horizon_end) so the reliability curve survives log rotation.

## hardcoded-live-07-inference-history-window-shadows-ml-lookback-days  [hardcoded | research_validity | live_path=True]

**Where:** src/ait/bot/orchestrator.py:1311-1312 (`days=504`); src/ait/ml/trainer.py:235-237 (`days=self._config.lookback_days`); src/ait/config/settings.py:334 (ml.lookback_days 504); config.yaml:158; adjacent literal windows: orchestrator.py:3816 (252 for the iv-rank proxy), :3570 (180 thesis), :590/:615 (60 correlation/regime)

**Claim:** The live feature frame handed to the direction and range predictors is a 504-day literal while training uses ml.lookback_days; they agree only because the config value currently equals the literal, so an operator edit to ml.lookback_days forks the training frame from the serving frame (the comment itself says the window exists for 'iv_rank, vol percentiles, trend' — frame-length-dependent features).

**Evidence:** orchestrator.py:1311-1312 `# 2 years of history for robust features (iv_rank, vol percentiles, trend)` / `hist_task = self._market_data.get_historical(symbol, days=504)`; trainer.py:236 `symbol, days=self._config.lookback_days`; grep: no other reader of ml.lookback_days on the live scan path.

**Trigger:** Any change to ml.lookback_days (e.g. 504 -> 756 for a longer training history): models retrained on the new window are served features computed on 504 rows.

**Consequence:** Any ml.lookback_days edit below ~500 (permitted 60..2520; 365 is the documented research window) retrains the direction and range models on frames whose expanding-window fractal features (psd_beta — a top-10 feature in 2 of 12 model tables — hurst_psd_divergence, multifractal_*) are distributed differently from the 500-504-row frame the scan hard-codes; with ml.entry_gates_enabled re-armed the range gate would score live entries on inputs the model never saw. Today (values equal, observe mode) the exposure is confined to the logged observe-mode predictions that the pre-registered judgment depends on. Raising the value is a silent no-op.

**Suggested fix:** Read self._settings.ml.lookback_days at orchestrator.py:1312 and log the served row count; add an executing test that ModelTrainer and _scan_symbol request the same `days` from the same MarketDataService (the smoke test's literal 504 pin should become the config value); longer term replace the expanding fractal windows with fixed trailing windows so no feature depends on frame length.

## hardcoded-research-01  [logic | research_validity | live_path=False]

**Where:** src/ait/optimization/optimizer.py:453-478 (bt_kwargs), src/ait/optimization/optimizer.py:494-514 (trial Backtester call); src/ait/backtesting/engine.py:97 (slippage_pct=0.01), engine.py:138 (hurst_hard_veto_multiplier=1.5), engine.py:107 (trailing_stop_enabled=False), engine.py:565-578 (hard veto); src/ait/backtesting/walkforward.py:57-58, :102, :115 (WalkForwardConfig 0.03 / True / 0.0), walkforward.py:1408-1409, :1416, :1423 (OOS Backtester), walkforward.py:1733-1777 (StrategyOptimizer construction)

**Claim:** Every per-window Optuna trial is scored by a Backtester whose NON-searched knobs differ from the OOS Backtester that then evaluates the chosen params: trials run slippage 0.01 (OOS 0.03), the fractal HARD VETO ON at multiplier 1.5 (OOS 0.0 = disabled), and trailing stops OFF (OOS ON). StrategyOptimizer has no parameter for any of the three and bt_kwargs omits them, so the engine constructor defaults apply in trials while WalkForwardConfig's different literals apply OOS. The hard veto alone removes 29-43% of trading days from trial entries on the live universe, so the 'best' wing_k/max_hold_days/threshold values are selected under a gate the OOS run never applies.

**Evidence:** Executed (Python313, real StrategyOptimizer._run_backtest with a recording Backtester): 'slippage_pct | <not passed -> engine default 0.01> | 0.03', 'hurst_hard_veto_multiplier | <not passed -> engine default 1.5> | 0.0', 'trailing_stop_enabled | <not passed -> engine default False> | True'. optimizer.py:453-478 bt_kwargs keys: initial_capital, stop_loss_pct, profit_target_pct, min_confidence, position_size_pct, trailing_stop_pct, breakeven_trigger_pct, max_hold_days, delta_short, delta_long, iv_floor, wing_floor_dollars, wing_k, delta_iv_scale, max_concurrent_positions, max_entry_vol_annual, hurst_regime_threshold, hurst_regime_penalty, multifractal_max_width, iv_rank_rise_threshold, min_edge_over_baseline (no slippage/hurst_hard_veto/trailing_stop_enabled). engine.py:573-578: '_veto_base = max(self._hurst_regime_threshold, 0.20); _hard_veto_threshold = _veto_base * self._hurst_hard_veto_multiplier; if _neutral_strat and self._hurst_hard_veto_multiplier > 0 and spread > _hard_veto_threshold: ... continue'. walkforward.py:115 comment on the OOS side: 'hurst_hard_veto_multiplier: float = 0.0   # 0=disabled; Exp 20 post-mortem: QQQ spread never < 0.43, any multiplier blocks all entries'. Executed FeatureEngine on load_daily_ohlcv(730d): SPY frac(hurst_scale_spread > 0.30) = 0.30, QQQ 0.29, IWM 0.43 (median 0.15/0.15/0.25). docs/GAP_AUDIT_R7.md:702 flagged only the trailing half ('[medium] Optuna trials run with trailing_stop_enabled defaulting to False') and it is still unfixed; slippage and the hard veto are not in either 2026-08-25 register (grep 'hurst_hard_veto' / 'slippage_pct': 0 hits).

**Trigger:** Any optimize_per_window run: `python run_backtest.py --optimize-per-window`, scripts/run_integration_test.py section E (line 610), dashboard walk-forward with optimization. Bites on every trial for iron_condor/short_strangle/put_credit_spread (NEUTRAL_CREDIT_GATED) whenever hurst_scale_spread > 0.30 (30-43% of days).

**Consequence:** On every optimize_per_window run (CLI flag, integration-test section E / dashboard archives) the per-window 'best' wing_k / max_hold_days / gate thresholds for iron_condor are chosen by trials that refuse entry on ~29-43% of trading days (spread>0.30) and are then evaluated OOS on a book that enters on those days with only a confidence penalty — the selected params are tuned to a different entry population than the one that scores them, so per-window optimizer results are biased noise for the live strategy.

**Suggested fix:** Thread slippage_pct, hurst_hard_veto_multiplier, trailing_stop_enabled (and the other non-searched OOS knobs: commission_per_contract, credit_loss_limit_mult, ic_min_credit, ic_min_credit_width, macro_event_gate, pre_event_blackout_days, spread_*) from WalkForwardConfig into StrategyOptimizer.__init__ and bt_kwargs exactly as PR#7 did for stop/profit/min_confidence; add an executing test that patches ait.backtesting.engine.Backtester with a recorder, runs one optimize_per_window window, and asserts every kwarg not in STRATEGY_SPACES is identical between the trial and OOS constructions.

## hardcoded-research-02  [hardcoded | research_validity | live_path=False]

**Where:** src/ait/backtesting/learner.py:27-32 (MIN_TRADES_TO_LEARN 15, MIN_TRADES_TO_DISABLE 25, multiplier bounds 0.30/2.0, MAX_CONFIDENCE 0.85), learner.py:234-258 (win_rate < 0.30 -> x0.80, > 0.58 with >= 6 trades -> x1.20), learner.py:279-281 (+0.05 confidence steps); src/ait/backtesting/walkforward.py:1012 (BacktestLearner constructed unconditionally in sequential mode), :1142-1146 (learned min_confidence + strategy disables), :1166 (symbol disables), :1328-1331 (effective_position_size = position_size_pct * min(avg_mult, 2.0)), :1334-1338 (confidence_threshold = effective_min_conf when not optimize_per_window), :810/:960 (parallel mode: learner disabled); src/ait/orchestration/master.py:758-770 (nightly run_backtest.py invocation, no n_jobs flag); config.yaml learning.paper_trading_mode: true

**Claim:** The walk-forward that produces every research number (including the nightly 'strategy health' backtest master.py launches) runs a self-learning overlay with hardcoded thresholds and no off switch: after >= 6 trades at > 58% win rate a strategy's OOS position size is boosted x1.20 per window up to 2x, losing strategies are cut x0.80, symbols are removed, and min_confidence is raised in +0.05 steps to 0.85. Live paper deliberately runs a FIXED ruleset (learning.paper_trading_mode: true makes the adaptor a no-op 'so results stay comparable to the backtest'), so the comparison the config comment promises is between a fixed live book and an adaptive research book. The overlay also silently switches OFF when optimize_n_jobs > 1, so identical WalkForwardConfigs yield different rulesets depending on job count.

**Evidence:** walkforward.py:1012 'learner = BacktestLearner(base_confidence=self._config.min_confidence)' inside the sequential branch (no config flag; grep 'learning_enabled|learner_enabled' in walkforward.py/run_backtest.py: 0 hits); :1328-1331 'effective_position_size = self._config.position_size_pct * min(avg_mult, 2.0)'; :1334-1338 'confidence_threshold = (window_cfg.min_confidence if self._config.optimize_per_window else effective_min_conf)'; learner.py:256-257 'elif win_rate > 0.58 and avg_pnl > 0 and total >= 6: new_mult = min(current_mult * 1.20, MAX_STRATEGY_MULTIPLIER)'; learner.py:280 'new_conf = min(self._state.min_confidence_override + 0.05, MAX_CONFIDENCE)'; walkforward.py:810 'warm-starting and BacktestLearner adaptation are disabled' (parallel path). config.yaml learning.paper_trading_mode comment: 'True = paper measures the fixed ruleset so results stay comparable to the backtest.' master.py:760-770 runs run_backtest.py with --symbols/--strategies/--capital only (sequential default). Neither register mentions BacktestLearner/learner.py (grep: 0 hits).

**Trigger:** Every sequential walk-forward (run_backtest.py default, the nightly master run, every scripts/*_study.py that does not set optimize_n_jobs>1) once any strategy accumulates >= 6 trades with > 58% wins (IC sim win rates run 60-84% per PLAN) or >= 15 trades under 30%.

**Consequence:** Every sequential walk-forward (all PLAN study scripts, run_backtest.py, the weekly health backtest) ramps iron_condor OOS sizing from 5% to 10% of capital across windows once 15 cumulative trades sit above 58% WR with positive avg P&L (IC sims run 60-84% WR per PLAN), so absolute P&L, drawdown and total_return of gated arms are sizing-path-dependent and arm comparisons are confounded by the learner amplifying whichever arm wins early; the same WalkForwardConfig yields a different trade list depending only on optimize_n_jobs, and the 'fixed ruleset comparable to the backtest' promise in config.yaml is false.

**Suggested fix:** Add WalkForwardConfig.self_learning: bool (default resolved from settings.learning, OFF while paper_trading_mode is true) and pass learner=None when off; move MIN_TRADES_TO_LEARN/DISABLE, the 0.80/1.20 multipliers, 0.30/0.58 WR thresholds, 0.30/2.0 bounds and 0.85 cap into a LearningConfig-backed dataclass; add executing tests that (a) a sequential and a parallel run of two windows produce identical trade lists and position sizes with the default config, and (b) BacktestLearner boosts only after MIN_TRADES_TO_LEARN (pinning the real threshold).

Unjustified: no rationale for 0.4/0.4/0.2 anywhere in PLAN.md, docs/, or code comments.

## logic-entry-03  [logic | research_validity | live_path=True]

**Where:** src/ait/bot/orchestrator.py:2286-2311 (pre-event entry gate; :2304 '_sessions <= _blackout'), :3711-3728 _sessions_until (:3728 'return max(0, sessions - 1)'), :1211-1215 (scan-level should_skip_trading under AIT_SKIP_MACRO_EVENTS); src/ait/data/economic_calendar.py:273-311 (is_pre_event_day/should_skip_trading); src/ait/execution/exit_policy.py:47-62 + src/ait/execution/portfolio.py:798 (condor exempt from macro flatten); config.yaml risk.pre_event_blackout_days: 1; PLAN.md:639 (08-03 '4 -> 1 DAY': '<=4 window blocked ~half of all trading days')

**Claim:** The '1-day' pre-event blackout blocks THREE sessions per macro event — 49% of all sessions from 2026-09-02 to 2026-12-31 (13 of 20 in September) — because (a) _sessions_until returns intervening sessions and the gate is '<=', so blackout=1 also blocks event-2 (Wed before a Fri release), and (b) the scan-level should_skip_trading is a second, independent gate that blocks event-1 and event-0 regardless of the config knob. Meanwhile the only live strategy HOLDS THROUGH the same events (condor exempt from the flatten since 08-04), so the gate's stated rationale at :2286-2291 ('don't OPEN credit structures the macro flatten will force-close within days') no longer applies to it: the bot refuses the richest pre-event premium for no protective benefit and halves the verdict sample rate. The 08-03 decision claimed to have fixed exactly this coverage problem.

**Evidence:** Executed with the repo's EconomicCalendar + is_trading_day over 2026-09-02..2026-12-31: 'trading days 84 entry-gate blocked 41 scan-skip 29 either 41 = 49% of sessions'; rows: ('2026-09-02','Wed','d2e',2,'ses',1,'entry_blocked',True,'scan_skip',False), ('2026-09-03','Thu',d2e 1,ses 0,True,True), ('2026-09-04','Fri',d2e 0,ses 0,True,True) and the same triple around every CPI/FOMC/PCE/NFP/GDP date. Live: 70 'economic_event_skip' lines on 2026-09-03 (NFP 09-04) in logs/ait.log. The two open condors (T-20260901-105959 SPY, T-20260901-110001 QQQ, DTE 24) hold through NFP 09-04, CPI 09-10 and FOMC 09-16 while entries are refused on 09-02/03/04, 09-09/10/11, 09-14/15/16. exit_policy.py:57-62 MACRO_FLATTEN_WINDOW_DAYS has no iron_condor entry; :89 'None = no macro flatten for this strategy (defined-risk holds through)'. Only test on the math: tests/test_r16_round2_fixes.py:156 '_sessions_until(3) == 0' (Fri->Mon), nothing pins the event-2 block or the session coverage.

**Trigger:** Every week containing a scheduled release (all but ~2 weeks per quarter): 2-3 consecutive sessions with zero condor entries; a 50-close verdict that needs ~2x the calendar it was planned for; and no gap protection in return because positions opened on event-3 carry the event anyway.

**Consequence:** Every scheduled release costs three sessions of condor entries instead of the two the config/tests describe: 41 of 84 sessions (49%) blocked through year-end versus 29 (35%) intended, i.e. +12 sessions / +14% lost to the off-by-one alone; the 50-close observe-mode verdict sample accrues ~30% slower than registered, with no gap protection gained since the open condors carry every one of those events anyway.

**Suggested fix:** Change the gate to '_sessions < _blackout' (or make _sessions_until count the event-eve session inclusively) so blackout=1 means exactly the last session before the release, port the same helper to the engine, and replace the arithmetic-only test with an executing one that drives _try_execute on a Wednesday before a Friday event (must pass) and the Thursday (must block); pre-register the expected '% of sessions blocked' and pin it with a calendar test over a fixed quarter.

## logic-entry-05  [logic | research_validity | live_path=True]

**Where:** src/ait/bot/orchestrator.py:1689-1693 (range prediction only log.info'd), :2567-2571 (save_trade_context confidence=eff_conf), :1870 (collect tuple carries no range_pred); src/ait/bot/state.py:727-741 (entry_confidence column = that constant); src/ait/ml/meta_label.py:419 ('primary_confidence: trade.get("entry_confidence", 0.60)'); PLAN.md:1720-1726 (pre-registered judgement: 'compare closes where p_in_range was above vs below 0.65'); config.yaml logging.max_bytes 20MB x backup_count 10

**Claim:** The observe-mode decision's own pre-registered judging criterion is unexecutable by construction: p_in_range is never persisted per trade (only a log line, and logs retain ~10 days, ~2.5 days under IBKR warning spam), while the journal now records a constant 0.60 as entry_confidence for every observe-mode condor and the directional number only in trades.ml_confidence — so at 50 closes there will be no per-trade range probability to score the gate against, and the meta-labeler's primary_confidence feature is a constant for the very sample observe mode was created to build.

**Evidence:** grep across src/ait for probability_in_range/p_in_range/range_prediction outside range_predictor/backtesting: only orchestrator.py:1683-1703 (log.info at 1691). DB probe on the two observe-mode trades: T-20260901-105959 trades.ml_confidence 0.3787 / trade_context.entry_confidence 0.6; T-20260901-110001 0.2802 / 0.6 (every pre-09-01 row has the two equal); trade_context.entry_signals keys = ['iv_rank','rsi_14',...,'hour_of_day',...] with no range probability; reports/daily_20260901.json contains 0 range_prediction entries. Log retention: first timestamp in logs/ait.log.10 = 2026-08-24T15:09Z with ait.log.1-.4 all rotated on 2026-09-01 (09:49, 11:34, 13:22, 15:05). PLAN.md:1722-1724: 'every logged range_prediction is scored against the realized outcome, so at 50 closes we can ask whether the gate WOULD have helped'.

**Trigger:** Every observe-mode entry from 2026-09-01 on: by the time the 50th close lands, the p_in_range that admitted each of the first ~40 has rotated out of the logs, and the only durable confidence column reads 0.60 for all of them.

**Consequence:** By the 50th observe-mode close the p_in_range that admitted each of the first ~40 trades will have rotated out of logs/ait.log*, the durable journal will read entry_confidence 0.60 for all of them, and the meta-labeler's primary_confidence feature is a constant over exactly the sample observe mode was created to build -- the pre-registered 'would the gate have helped' judgement cannot be executed as written.

**Suggested fix:** Persist range_pred.probability_in_range, threshold_pct, horizon_days, the range model version and the directional final_confidence into trade_context at entry (new nullable columns or entry_signals keys, written in the same save_trade_context call), keep entry_confidence as the number that admitted the trade, and add an executing test that an observe-mode condor entry writes a non-null range probability; optionally also copy each scan's range_prediction into a small SQLite table so rejected/unfilled scans are scorable too.

## logic-entry-08  [hardcoded | research_validity | live_path=True]

**Where:** src/ait/strategies/iron_condor.py:144 (AIT_IRON_CONDOR_IV_FLOOR '15'), :189-190 (AIT_IC_DELTA_MIN/MAX '0.15'/'0.30'), :210-211 (AIT_IC_EM_MIN/MAX '0.6'/'1.3'); src/ait/risk/manager.py:435 (AIT_IV_RANK_CAP '85'), :470 (AIT_MAX_PER_EXPIRY '3'); tests/test_r19_config_authority.py:76-79 (scan limited to CONTRACT_DEFAULTS keys); src/ait/config/runtime_env.py:72-88, 112-118 (contract + CONFIG_BACKED omit all six)

**Claim:** Six entry-side trading values that decide which condors are admitted live as raw os.environ.get literals with no config.yaml home and no CONTRACT_DEFAULTS entry, so the R19c policy ('config must be the ONLY place with values that manage trading') and its enforcement test do not cover them: the literal in the source IS the live operating value, and the R19 scanner cannot see a future divergence.

**Evidence:** iron_condor.py:144 'iv_floor = float(os.environ.get("AIT_IRON_CONDOR_IV_FLOOR", "15"))'; :189 '_band_lo = float(os.environ.get("AIT_IC_DELTA_MIN", "0.15"))'; :210 '_em_lo = float(os.environ.get("AIT_IC_EM_MIN", "0.6"))'; manager.py:435 '_iv_cap = float(os.environ.get("AIT_IV_RANK_CAP", "85"))'; manager.py:470 '_max_per_expiry = int(os.environ.get("AIT_MAX_PER_EXPIRY", "3"))'. test_r19_config_authority.py:77 pattern = 'environ\.get\(\s*"(' + '|'.join(k for k in CONTRACT_DEFAULTS ...) — none of the six is in CONTRACT_DEFAULTS. .env declares only AIT_LIQ_MAX_SPREAD / AIT_LIQ_MIN_OI / AIT_LIQ_MIN_VOLUME; run_orchestrator.py and main.py set none of the six. Prior registers cite the names only as parity facts (live-bt-mirrors-06/07), not as config-authority violations.

**Consequence:** Six gates that decide which condors are admitted (IV floor, delta band, EM band, IV cap, per-expiry cap) run from source literals; an operator edit of config.yaml cannot move them, a study cannot vary them through the declared config path, run_backtest's parity manifest reports them from a second private copy, and the R19 enforcement test passes regardless — the exact 'private fallback' class R19 closed for the other nine keys is open for these.

**Suggested fix:** Add the six keys to CONTRACT_DEFAULTS + CONFIG_BACKED with settings/config.yaml homes (options.ic_iv_rank_floor, options.ic_delta_band_min/max, options.ic_em_band_min/max, risk.iv_rank_cap, risk.max_positions_per_expiry); migrate the readers and run_backtest.py:145 to contract_float; extend TestNoReaderReintroducesItsOwnFallback to fail on ANY `environ.get("AIT_...", <literal>)` outside runtime_env.py with an explicit allowlist, proven failing pre-fix.

## logic-entry-09  [parameter | research_validity | live_path=True]

**Where:** src/ait/strategies/iron_condor.py:138-146 (IV floor 15 and its rationale comment); src/ait/strategies/selector.py:237-243 (IC recommended only when iv_rank > 30 — no callers); src/ait/bot/orchestrator.py:1530-1534 (regime consulted only for HIGH_VOLATILITY); src/ait/ml/regime.py:236-238 (IV_CRUSH); docs/AUDIT_R12.md:69-70 (only VRP evidence: top bucket); data: trade_context T-20260901-105959 entry_regime 'iv_crush', entry_iv_rank 18.25

**Claim:** The live IV-rank floor of 15 is justified in code solely by sample-collection rate ('Trade quality drops but data collection rate matters more right now'), not by any measured edge; the package's own recommendation logic says condors only above 30; the repo's single VRP measurement covers the top bucket only; and the 09-01 SPY condor was opened at iv_rank 18.25 in a regime the bot's own detector stamped iv_crush, which nothing on the entry path reads. With a credit/width floor already binding at 0.10 and $7.33 round-trip cost, the bottom-quintile entries the floor admits are the thinnest-premium trades the strategy can take.
