Trade quality drops but data collection rate matters more right now).' selector.py:240-243: 'if iv_rank > 50: ["iron_condor","short_strangle"] elif iv_rank > 30: ["iron_condor"] else: ["long_straddle"]' (grep: get_recommended_strategies has no caller). orchestrator.py:1531-1534 only 'if regime.regime == MarketRegime.HIGH_VOLATILITY: direction = NEUTRAL'. DB: ('T-20260901-105959-c4ee1b', ..., 'iv_crush', 18.25). Telemetry: 70 of 289 candidates rejected at the 0.10 ratio floor (median 0.0955), accepted median 0.11 — the floor is the equilibrium, so low-IV entries have no margin over cost.

**Trigger:** unjustified: no rationale anywhere beyond sample rate — fires on every low-IV session (SPY 08-25 at 13.9 blocked, 09-01 at 18.3 traded: the floor flips on a 4-point move).

**Consequence:** The one gate that decides whether a low-IV session sells premium is set by a sample-rate rationale written when the floor was loosened, with the package's own recommendation logic (>30) dead code and the regime detector's classification unread at entry; the verdict sample therefore admits the thinnest-premium entries the stack allows (credit/width pinned at the 0.10 floor, $7.33 round-trip cost) with no measured VRP for that bucket — at 50 closes the IC verdict will be partly a verdict on trades no evidence said to take.

**Suggested fix:** Measure bottom-bucket VRP from the 252-row daily IV store (entry IV vs realized over the hold) and pre-register the floor from it; until measured, home the floor in config (see logic-entry-08) with the sample-rate rationale written beside it and a revisit trigger at the verdict; either wire regime/IV_CRUSH into the condor entry path or delete get_recommended_strategies so the code carries one opinion.

## logic-entry-10  [doc_vs_code | research_validity | live_path=True]

**Where:** src/ait/strategies/iron_condor.py:77-81 (docstring 'Mirrors the backtest's wing sizing'), :237 ('_vol_scaled_width(price, short_put, short_call_hint=None, chain=chain)' -> put wing off short-put IV only), :252 (call wing off put/call average); src/ait/backtesting/engine.py:1684-1687 (one iv, symmetric wings: 'long_call_strike = short_call_strike + wing_width; long_put_strike = short_put_strike - wing_width')

**Claim:** Live condors are asymmetric — the put wing is sized off the skewed short-put IV alone while the call wing uses the put/call average — so live max_loss is the put-side width, 3-33% wider than the call side on every live condor; the engine builds symmetric wings off a single IV. The docstring claiming the live sizing mirrors the backtest is false, and research credit/width (and max_loss per credit) is systematically more favourable than live's, which is where 24% of live candidates die at the 0.10 floor.

**Evidence:** DB legs of all 8 live condors since 08-01: put_w/call_w = 30/29, 35/31, 45/45, 39/31, 55/49, 49/45, 36/33, 60/45 (ratios 1.03-1.33; T-20260901-110001 QQQ put 60 vs call 45, CAR $5,356 set by the put side). iron_condor.py:85-90 averages implied_vol over '(short_put, short_call_hint)' — None on the put call site. engine.py:1684 'expected_move = S * iv * (dte / 365.0) ** 0.5' with one iv for both wings (W5 added skew to leg PRICING at :1689, not to wing WIDTH). live-bt-mirrors-09 covers the $2/$5 floor and budget cap only.

**Trigger:** Every live condor with put IV > call IV (all of them under normal skew): research measures a structure with ~10-25% less capital at risk per dollar of credit than live trades.

**Consequence:** Live max_loss is the skewed put-wing width while the engine risks a symmetric ATM-IV width: for the same short strikes research measures a structure with less capital at risk per dollar of credit than live trades, so the engine's credit/width and max_loss-per-credit are systematically more favourable than live's — where 70 of 306 live candidates (23%) already die at the 0.10 credit/width floor.

**Suggested fix:** Pick one rule — size both live wings off chain.atm_iv (matching the engine's single-IV expected move) or give the engine per-side expected moves from _get_leg_iv at the short strikes — and pin it with an executing parity test that pushes one synthetic chain through IronCondor.generate_signals and Backtester._build_credit_position and asserts equal put and call widths; correct the docstring meanwhile.

## logic-entry-11  [logic | research_validity | live_path=True]

**Where:** src/ait/bot/orchestrator.py:2370 ('SETTLE_SECONDS = 300'), :2386 ('if hour_min < 10.5 and not _neutral and confidence < 0.85' — condors exempt), :1950-1951 (first-hour trade budget 2); src/ait/config/settings.py:225 (backtest.entry_window_start_et default '10:30'); src/ait/backtesting/engine.py:846-856 (research entries only from the first bar in the window)

**Claim:** Live condors may enter from ~09:35 and half of the real IC fills did (09:36, 09:53, 09:56, 10:00), while every research entry with intraday bars is taken at or after 10:30 — so half the go-live verdict sample is executed in the opening window that no walk-forward run simulates and where delayed-data paper quotes are widest; no register entry covers the live side of the entry-window fork R20 resolved for research only.

**Evidence:** DB entry times of filled iron_condor trades: 09:36 (T-20260707), 09:56 (T-20260804 QQQ), 09:53 (T-20260810), 10:00 (T-20260824), 10:51, 10:59, 11:00, 14:19 x2 — 4 of 8 real fills before 10:30. orchestrator.py:2385-2400: '_neutral = signal.strategy_name in ("iron_condor", "short_strangle")' and the gate applies only 'not _neutral'; no other time-of-day entry gate exists for condors. engine.py:849-856: 'window_bars = session_bars[... self._is_in_entry_window(ts)]; if window_bars.empty: continue'; settings.py:225 'entry_window_start_et: str = Field(default="10:30"'; config.yaml has no entry_window keys, so 10:30 is the research operating value (PLAN R20: 'entry-window fork resolved to config's 10:30'). logs/ait.log.10 head: TWS 'Requested market data is not subscribed. Displaying delayed market data.'

**Trigger:** Any morning with a free slot and a generated condor between 09:35 and 10:30 (the two-phase scan runs every 300 s from the open): the live fill sits outside the research entry population and inside the widest-spread half hour of delayed paper quotes.

**Consequence:** Four of the ten iron-condor closes that currently make up the go-live verdict sample were entered between 09:36 and 10:00 — a window no walk-forward run ever simulates (research enters at the 10:30 bar) and the half hour where delayed paper quotes are widest — so the live record and the research record are drawn from different entry populations, and every future morning with a free slot adds to the gap.

**Suggested fix:** Make backtest.entry_window_start_et the single authority: have _try_execute refuse NEW entries for every strategy before that ET time (or deliberately set the research window to 09:35 if opening-window entries are the intended policy), and add an executing test that feeds 09:45 and 10:35 timestamps to both the live gate and Backtester._is_in_entry_window and asserts they agree.

## ml-leakage-01  [bug | research_validity | live_path=True]

**Where:** src/ait/ml/range_predictor.py:495 (predict: `X = features.reindex(columns=feature_names).fillna(0.0).iloc[[-1]]`); src/ait/ml/range_predictor.py:268 (`self._scaler.fit(X)` on NaN-bearing X); src/ait/ml/features.py:147-178 (_merge_intraday_features left-join, `if len(session) < 10: continue`); src/ait/ml/features.py:201 (`compute_intraday_features` returns {} when `len(intraday_df) < 20`); src/ait/bot/orchestrator.py:1683-1688 (live range predict with intraday_store=self._historical)

**Claim:** The 26 VLMC intraday features are encoded as NaN (missing) in training but as 0.0 at live serve time whenever today's session is absent or shorter than 20 five-minute bars (i.e. every scan before ~11:10 ET and any scan whose intraday fetch failed), and the served artifact maps those two encodings to materially different P(in range) — crossing the 0.65 range_min_confidence floor in both directions.

**Evidence:** Artifact probe (models/range.pkl, range-v-20260904-073226, h=9): per-symbol StandardScaler.n_samples_seen_ is an array — 372/366/377 rows for base features but only 58 for every VLMC feature, i.e. 83% of training rows carried NaN VLMC and were fitted as XGBoost/LightGBM 'missing'. Code: training never fills VLMC (features.py:178 left-join keeps NaN; range_predictor.py:268 scaler.fit on NaN; model.fit on NaN), but predict() (range_predictor.py:495) `reindex(...).fillna(0.0)`. Sessions with <20 bars produce no VLMC row (features.py:201 `if intraday_df is None or len(intraday_df) < 20: return {}`), so the live last row is NaN -> 0.0 until 20 bars exist (09:30 + 20x5min ≈ 11:10 ET). Executed probe with the served per-symbol models and the real 2026-09-01 feature row: QQQ P(in range) = 0.816 with VLMC=NaN (training shape) vs 0.431 with VLMC=0.0 (predict path); IWM 0.510 (NaN) vs 0.743 (0.0); SPY 0.992 vs 0.992. Journal: the only IC entries carrying feature snapshots were placed at 2026-08-24T10:00:40 (QQQ), 2026-09-01T10:59:59 (SPY), 2026-09-01T11:00:01 (QQQ) — all inside the pre-11:10 window where the gate ran on the 0.0 encoding. Register check: research-to-live-03 flagged zero-fill only in the RESEARCH OOS feature-cache path for the direction ensemble; the live range-gate path is not registered.

**Trigger:** Any live scan of a range-trained symbol before ~11:10 ET, or any scan where get_intraday/get_intraday_since fails (intraday=None) — the range gate (and, when re-armed, the 0.65 veto) is computed on a feature encoding no training row ever had.

**Consequence:** Every range probability the bot logs before ~11:10 ET (and any scan whose intraday fetch failed) is computed at a feature encoding no training row ever had — 26 columns forced to a real z-score of (0 - mean)/std instead of the 'missing' branch the trees were fitted with. Measured shift on the real 2026-09-03 QQQ row: 0.7415 vs 0.9219. That is the observe-mode record the PLAN says will decide whether to re-arm the gate, so the re-arm evidence is being written at the wrong encoding; on re-arm the same error moves entry admit/veto across the 0.65 floor directly.

**Suggested fix:** Make serve equal train: in predict()/predict_from_features fill only the non-VLMC columns and leave the VLMC block NaN so trees take the missing branch (e.g. X = features.reindex(columns=feature_names); X[non_vlmc] = X[non_vlmc].fillna(0.0)) — or drop VLMC from the range feature set until the intraday store spans the training window. Executing test: train a RangePredictor against a store covering only the tail of the frame, then assert predict() on a <20-bar session returns the same probability as scoring the row with VLMC=NaN (fails pre-fix by ~0.18).

## ml-leakage-02  [logic | research_validity | live_path=True]

**Where:** src/ait/ml/range_predictor.py:348-366 (_walk_forward_split: expanding folds, `fold = n // (n_splits + 1)`); src/ait/ml/range_predictor.py:268-272 (final fit on all rows after CV); src/ait/ml/range_predictor.py:473-477 (edge gate from cv_scores/fitted_weights); src/ait/ml/features.py:147 (`load_intraday(symbol, days=len(features)+10)`)

**Claim:** The CV score that arms the range gate and fixes the ensemble weights never evaluates a model that has seen a non-missing VLMC value: all 58 VLMC-present rows (the intraday store starts 2026-06-01) fall in the LAST validation fold and in no training fold, while the final all-data fit does learn VLMC splits — so cv_scores/fitted_weights/edge describe a different estimator than the one serving, and VLMC 'missingness' is a perfect proxy for calendar date (>= 2026-06-01) that the served model can exploit but CV cannot detect.

**Evidence:** Executed probe on the live-shaped frame (504 daily rows ending 2026-09-03, VIX/SPY/FRED context, real intraday store): SPY folds -> fold1..3 train VLMC-present=0, fold4 train VLMC-present=0, val 2026-04-20..08-21 VLMC-present=58 (identical pattern for QQQ/IWM). Artifact probe: served QQQ xgboost puts 10.2% of split importance on VLMC features (10 non-zero), IWM xgboost's 3rd-most-important feature is intraday_vwap_position (0.052); SPY xgboost 0.0. Scaler n_samples_seen_ = 58 for VLMC vs 372-377 for the rest confirms the block is contiguous-recent. Intraday coverage: data/historical.db intraday_prices SPY/QQQ/IWM = 65 sessions, 2026-06-01..2026-09-01, vs 613 daily rows from 2024-03-26.

**Trigger:** Every retrain while the intraday store covers < the full training window (today: 65 of ~386 feature rows); the gate decision `edge < min_edge_over_baseline` and the fitted_weights are then evidence about the VLMC-blind CV models, not the served model.

**Consequence:** cv_scores and fitted_weights — the numbers that arm/disarm the range gate at range_predictor.py:473-477 and set the ensemble weights — are computed for an estimator that never saw a non-missing VLMC value, while the artifact actually served has 22% of its xgb importance on VLMC. The gate's edge-vs-0.05 decision and the observe-mode record are evidence about a different model than the one predicting.

**Suggested fix:** Either exclude VLMC from the range feature set until intraday coverage spans the training window, or compute cv_scores with VLMC dropped so CV and the final fit share feature availability; log the VLMC coverage fraction alongside range_trained. Executing test: build a frame whose last 15% of rows carry VLMC, call _walk_forward_split, and assert that whenever the final fit's feature_names include VLMC at least one fold's TRAINING index set contains a VLMC-present row (fails today for all three live symbols).

## ml-leakage-03  [logic | research_validity | live_path=True]

**Where:** src/ait/ml/range_predictor.py:348-366 (_walk_forward_split, n_splits=4, expanding); src/ait/ml/range_predictor.py:395-396 and 427-428 (`balanced_accuracy_score(y[val_idx], model.predict(X_val))` per fold); src/ait/ml/range_predictor.py:473-477 (edge = weighted cv - 0.50 vs min_edge_over_baseline=0.05); config.yaml:156 (min_edge_over_baseline: 0.05); config.yaml:190-196 / PLAN.md:1682-1729 (observe-mode decision built on these scores)

**Claim:** The gate statistic is degenerate: with ~85% positive labels and ~74-row validation folds, folds contain 0-4 negatives, and sklearn's balanced_accuracy_score on a zero-negative fold collapses to majority accuracy (~0.99) while a 3-negative fold moves in 1/6 steps — so the 4-fold mean that is compared against a 0.05 edge threshold is dominated by degenerate folds, and the ARMED/BLOCKED verdict flips on a single day's data.

**Evidence:** Executed probe (live-shaped 504-row frame, same params/splits as _train_xgb): SPY val folds n_neg = 1, 0, 17, 3; xgb fold scores [0.651, 0.986, 0.491, 0.5], mean 0.657 (edge +0.157 ARMED); dropping the zero-negative fold gives 0.547 (edge +0.047 BLOCKED). IWM fold4 n_neg=4; QQQ fold1 n_neg=3 (score 0.174). Degeneracy check: balanced_accuracy_score([1]*74,[1]*73+[0]) = 0.9865; 3 negatives all-missed = 0.5, one caught = 0.667. Live log evidence of the flip: range_trained SPY xgboost 0.5410 at 2026-09-03 12:19Z -> 0.6398 at 2026-09-04 07:32 (artifact cv_scores {'xgboost': 0.6398, 'lightgbm': 0.5705}) from one additional trading day; QQQ 09-03 fitted_weights {'xgboost': 1.0, 'lightgbm': 0.0} (lgb 0.484 < 0.5). The observe-mode decision (PLAN.md:1682-1729, config.yaml:190-196) and the 'edge +0.045 vs 0.05' framing rest on this statistic.

**Trigger:** Any retrain: in_range_rate 0.72-0.85 at ±5%/9d with fold size n//5 ≈ 74 puts <5 negatives in most folds; the gate result is then a property of which fold the few breakouts landed in, not of model skill.

**Consequence:** The quantity that decides whether the range model may gate iron condors at all is a 4-fold mean of a statistic that degenerates to plain accuracy on folds containing no breakouts. On SPY it reads ARMED (+0.099) only because one fold has zero negatives; removing degenerate folds turns it into BLOCKED (-0.009), and the production logs show the live verdict flipping BLOCKED->ARMED on a single day's new data. Both the 'edge +0.045 vs 0.05' framing that justified observe mode and any future re-arm decision are properties of where the few breakouts happened to land.

**Suggested fix:** Score CV with a threshold-free, class-count-robust statistic pooled across folds — out-of-fold AUROC or Brier skill on concatenated OOF predictions — with a minimum-negatives-per-fold guard, or use stratified purged folds; log per-fold n_neg with range_trained so a degenerate fold is visible; re-derive min_edge_over_baseline for whatever statistic replaces it. Executing test: feed _train_xgb a y whose negatives are clustered so one validation fold has zero, and assert the returned score is not driven by that fold (fails today: the fold contributes 0.93).

## ml-leakage-05  [logic | research_validity | live_path=False]

**Where:** src/ait/backtesting/engine.py:479 (`hist = self._data.iloc[: i + 1]` includes today's completed daily bar); engine.py:539 (`_get_direction(hist, ...)` -> features_df.iloc[-1] from that bar; engine.py:1247/1262 cache masked `<= today`, predict_from_features(features_df.iloc[-1])); engine.py:842-877 (intraday entry: limit_price from the first entry-window bar / hist_partial, fill on subsequent bars); src/ait/backtesting/walkforward.py:1400,1451 (OOS Backtester gets intraday_store=_oos_intraday_store); scripts/ablation_*.py:58-62 (db_path=data/historical.db)

**Claim:** On every OOS day that has 5-minute bars in the intraday store, the research engine decides the entry (direction model, range gate, fractal gate, vol gate, drawdown veto) from a feature row built on the day's COMPLETE daily bar — including that day's 16:00 close, high, low and full volume — and then fills the order at the 10:30 scan bar; `hist_partial` is used only to price the limit order, never for features. This is a same-bar lookahead that the live bot cannot replicate.

**Evidence:** engine.py:479 `hist = self._data.iloc[: i + 1]`; engine.py:539 `direction, confidence, features_df = self._get_direction(hist, market_context=...)`; engine.py:1247 `mask = self._features_cache.index <= today` then :1262 `predict_from_features(features_df.iloc[-1], ...)`; engine.py:863-869 `hist_partial = HistoricalDataStore.slice_intraday_up_to(session_bars, scan_time.time()) ... limit_price = float(hist_partial['Close'].iloc[-1])` (no feature recomputation from hist_partial); engine.py:873 `_try_limit_fill(limit_price, bars_after_scan, ...)`. The intraday store is wired into OOS by walkforward.py:1400/1451 and every pre-registered study passes db_path=data/historical.db (scripts/ablation_confirm.py:62, ablation_armB.py:58, ablation_rerun.py:62, ablation_study.py:58). Store coverage: 5-min bars exist 2026-06-01..2026-09-01, i.e. exactly the sim-vs-live comparison window. Register: blindspot_composition_hunt_20260825.md:641 lists 'same-bar close in features' as NEVER-AUDITED; no entry covers it.

**Trigger:** Any walkforward/backtest window whose test days fall on or after 2026-06-01 with db_path set: e.g. on 2026-09-01 (QQQ close -1.27%, SPY -0.69%) the 10:30 entry decision already sees daily_return/high_low_range/realized_vol of the full day and the range model's containment probability computed from the day's close.

**Consequence:** On every OOS day with stored 5-min bars, the engine chooses the direction, range probability, fractal/vol/drawdown verdicts AND the condor's strike centre from the completed daily bar, then books the fill at ~10:30 that morning. A trending day (e.g. 2026-09-01 QQQ -1.27%) has its short strikes centred on the price the underlying actually ended at, which is exactly the quantity the iron condor is betting on — the OOS PF/DD numbers those studies produce are unattainable live, where the 10:30 decision cannot see the close.

**Suggested fix:** When an intraday fill is simulated, build the decision features and the entry underlying from hist.iloc[:i] plus a partial bar aggregated from session bars up to scan_time (or simply hist.iloc[:i]) rather than the completed bar, and pass the scan-time price into _build_position instead of row['Close']. Executing test: run the engine over two identical daily frames differing only in day i's post-10:30 path (same open/first-hour bars, different close/high/low), and assert the entry decision, range probability and chosen strikes are identical (fails today on all three).

## ml-leakage-06  [bug | research_validity | live_path=True]

**Where:** src/ait/ml/features.py:918 (`series.reindex(df.index, method="ffill")` for FRED series); features.py:932 (`us_10y_change_20d = y10.diff(20)`), :940 (`yield_curve_change_20d = curve.diff(20)`), :949 (`dxy_change_5d = dxy.pct_change(5)`); src/ait/ml/trainer.py:355 (macros fetched at retrain, joined by observation date); src/ait/data/macro.py:34-39 (DGS2, DGS10, T10Y2Y, DTWEXBGS); src/ait/bot/orchestrator.py:1287-1292 (live macros ffilled to the scan row)

**Claim:** The macro features are not point-in-time: training joins each FRED observation to its own observation date although DGS2/DGS10 are published the next business day and DTWEXBGS (dollar index) about a week later, whereas the live scan row can only see ffilled stale values — so the model is trained on a feature regime (same-day yields, real 5-day DXY changes) that never exists at decision time.

**Evidence:** Executed FRED probe on 2026-09-05: DGS10 last observation 2026-09-03 (4.77), DGS2 2026-09-03, DTWEXBGS last observation 2026-08-28 (118.7479) — i.e. during the 09-01..09-04 scans the live DXY series ended 08-28 and the yields ended 09-02/09-03, while the next retrain will attach 09-01..09-04 values to those same rows. Code: features.py:918 ffill onto the price index then diff/pct_change on the ffilled series (:932, :940, :949), so live `dxy_change_5d` is computed over a constant tail (e.g. dxy(08-28)/dxy(08-27)-1 = +0.33% for every row 08-31..09-04) while training rows get the true 5-day change. Served-artifact importances (models/range.pkl): SPY xgboost yield_curve_change_20d = 0.050 (4th-ranked feature), all 9 macro features together 0.157 of SPY xgboost importance; QQQ 0.105; IWM 0.094.

**Trigger:** Every live scan (the last 1-2 rows for yields, last ~5-7 rows for DXY are stale-filled) and every backtest/walkforward window (features use values not yet published on the row date) — the gap is largest around FOMC/CPI days when yields move and the model's training rows already 'know' the move.

**Consequence:** Row d in training and in every walkforward/backtest window carries FRED values that were not published on day d (DTWEXBGS by up to a week), while the live scan row can only carry a stale ffilled tail — dxy_change_5d degenerates toward 0.0 across the last 5-6 live rows. The measured train-vs-serve gap on dxy_change_5d is larger than the feature's own standard deviation and reverses sign, so the ~13-18% of production model importance sitting on macro features is being evaluated on one distribution and served on another.

**Suggested fix:** Shift each FRED series by its real release lag before the reindex in features.py:906-918 (DGS*/T10Y2Y: one business day; DTWEXBGS: its H.10 weekly lag, or drop it), so training, backtests and live all see the same as-of value. Executing test: build features on a synthetic frame whose macro series ends N days before the price index and assert that the macro value on row d equals the observation dated <= d - lag, and that the last row's dxy_change_5d matches what a same-day fetch could compute (fails today: training gets the unshifted value).

## ops-substrate-04  [enforcement_gap | research_validity | live_path=True]

**Where:** src/ait/orchestration/master.py:980-1040 (backup_state_db: `src = DATA_DIR / "ait_state.db"` only; mirror = ~/Documents/ait_backups/ait_state.latest.db); data/backups/ (listing), C:/Users/prith/OneDrive/ait_backups/ (listing); data/historical.db, data/ait_analytics.duckdb, data/counterfactual_log.json, data/thompson_state.json, .env, C:/IBC/config.ini

**Claim:** The only backup mechanism protects ait_state.db and nothing else: the ML/research store historical.db (intraday_prices 28,338 5-min bars 2026-06-01->09-01 that IB will not re-serve beyond its lookback, plus the 3,159-quote option_spread_samples that W5's skew/commission calibration was measured on), the DuckDB analytics store (314 trade rows vs 30+95 in sqlite - the pre-reset history the dashboard/analytics read), the counterfactual/Thompson JSON state, .env and the IBC credential file have no snapshot anywhere, while the digest reports 'mirror current'.

**Evidence:** master.py:988 `src = DATA_DIR / "ait_state.db"`; :1008 prunes `bdir.glob("ait_state.*.db")`; :1018-1024 mirrors only `ait_state.latest.db`. `ls data/backups`: ait_state.2026081?..0904.db + two pre_d1 files, nothing else; `ls ~/OneDrive/ait_backups`: ait_state.latest.db (+stale -shm/-wal from Jul 14), count 3. Read-only probe of data/historical.db: daily_prices 9,380 rows (2024-03-26..2026-09-03), intraday_prices 28,338 rows (2026-06-01T13:30Z..2026-09-01T19:25Z), option_spread_samples 3,159 rows, option_spread_params 3 rows. DuckDB read-only probe: trades 314, trade_context 357, daily_stats 39; sqlite: trades 30, trades_legacy 95. The box has one drive (C: 363 GB used / 79 GB free), an unexpected power loss on 2026-08-12 08:43 (System event 41/6008), and journal_mode=delete on historical.db (per the R23 register) - a crash mid-write corrupts the only copy.

**Trigger:** Any C: drive loss/corruption or an unclean power cut during the bot's save_intraday write (event-41 class already observed 08-12): the retrain's training store, the W5 calibration sample, and the analytics history are unrecoverable while every backup check stays green; a rebuild of the box also has no copy of .env or C:/IBC/config.ini (MATURITY_R10's rebuild path lists neither).

**Consequence:** A C: loss or an unclean power cut during a save_intraday write destroys the only copy of the 28,338-bar intraday store (IB will not re-serve that lookback) and of the 3,159 option_spread_samples the W5 skew/commission calibration was measured on, plus the DuckDB analytics history — while the daily digest keeps printing 'mirror current' because that phrase describes ait_state.db alone.

**Suggested fix:** Extend backup_state_db to snapshot historical.db through the same sqlite3 .backup + integrity_check path, copy counterfactual_log.json/thompson_state.json into the same dated folder, and either EXPORT DATABASE the DuckDB store or copy it when no writer holds it; hash-verify each into the OneDrive mirror exactly as ait_state.latest.db is, and make the digest name which artefacts the 'mirror current' verdict covers. Test asserts every artefact is present in the mirror after one run.

## ops-substrate-05  [doc_vs_code | research_validity | live_path=True]

**Where:** PLAN.md:1717-1724 (observe-mode judgment: 'every logged range_prediction is scored against the realized outcome ... compare closes where p_in_range was above vs below 0.65'); src/ait/bot/orchestrator.py:1690-1693 (log.info('range_prediction', p_in_range=...) is the only sink with gates off), orchestrator.py:1697-1703 (confidence override gated on entry_gates_enabled), orchestrator.py:1841-1849 (observe mode writes the 0.60 neutral baseline as eff_conf); data/ait_state.db trade_context columns; config.yaml:258-259 (ait.log 20 MB x 10)

**Claim:** The pre-registered observe-mode decision rule cannot be evaluated: with entry_gates_enabled=false the range model's p_in_range is written ONLY to the rotating ait.log (retention ~12 calendar days, ~4 RTH days at active-day rates) - not to trade_context (no range column; entry_confidence carries the 0.60 neutral constant) nor any other durable store - while reaching 50 IC closes takes months, so the per-close p_in_range needed for the 'above vs below 0.65' comparison will have rotated away for nearly every close.

**Evidence:** orchestrator.py:1690-1693 `log.info("range_prediction", symbol=symbol, p_in_range=f"{range_pred.probability_in_range:.3f}", ...)` followed by the comment 'with gates OFF the prediction is LOGGED (line above, for future studies) but must not flow into eff_conf' and `if self._settings.ml.entry_gates_enabled: ... s.confidence = range_pred.probability_in_range` (1697-1703, skipped in observe mode). orchestrator.py:1843-1845 `eff_conf = float(self._settings.ml.observe_mode_neutral_confidence)`. Read-only probe: `pragma table_info(trade_context)` = ['trade_id','entry_direction','entry_confidence','entry_regime','entry_vix','entry_iv_rank','entry_sentiment_score','entry_signals','model_version'] - no range field; grep of src for probability_in_range/p_in_range finds no DB/JSON writer outside backtesting. Retention: ls logs/ait.log*: ait.log.10 = 08-24 13:07, ait.log.1 = 09-01 15:05, ait.log = 09-05 (20,971,xxx bytes each; 6 files consumed on 08-24/08-25 alone). Verdict pace: 10 IC closes since 07-06 (PLAN.md 2026-08-31 entry) -> 50 closes is months away.

**Trigger:** Unjustified by construction: the scoring PLAN.md:1720-1722 commits to at 50 closes requires p_in_range for each close's entry; any close whose entry is more than ~2 weeks old has no surviving record, so the re-arm/retire decision will be made on a handful of recent closes or on nothing.

**Consequence:** The pre-registered observe-mode decision rule cannot be executed. p_in_range for a live candidate exists only in a rotating 20 MB x 11 log whose whole retained window is ~13 calendar days (and ~2 days at active-scan rates), while the sample needs 50 IC closes and stands at 12 — so at verdict time nearly every close will have no surviving prediction and the re-arm/retire call gets made on a handful of recent closes or on nothing.

**Suggested fix:** Persist the prediction where the decision can reach it: add p_in_range / threshold_pct / horizon_days columns to trade_context (written alongside entry_confidence in save_trade_context) for executed trades, plus a small append-only range_predictions JSONL or table written next to the log.info at orchestrator.py:1690 for every candidate; backfill whatever the surviving ait.log.* still contain. Executing test: run one observe-mode scan through the hot-path rig and assert the persisted row carries the model's p_in_range, not the 0.60 baseline (fails pre-fix).

## params-ml-research-01  [logic | research_validity | live_path=True]

**Where:** src/ait/ml/range_predictor.py:462-481 (edge veto returns None before any probability is computed; only a DEBUG 'range_no_edge' at :478); src/ait/bot/orchestrator.py:1682-1693 ('range_prediction' logged only `if range_pred:`); src/ait/bot/orchestrator.py:2567-2570 + data/ait_state.db trade_context schema (no p_in_range column); config.yaml:190-195; PLAN.md:1695-1697, 1710-1721; scripts/ (no consumer of 'range_prediction')

**Claim:** Observe mode's pre-registered judgement ('every logged range_prediction is scored against the realized outcome ... compare closes where p_in_range was above vs below 0.65') is impossible for SPY and QQQ - the two symbols the decision was made for - because the min_edge veto inside predict() returns None BEFORE a probability exists, so no range_prediction is ever logged or persisted for them; config.yaml:193-194 and PLAN.md:1695-1697 ('The ML models keep predicting and logging; they no longer veto') are false for SPY/QQQ.

**Evidence:** range_predictor.py:477-481: `if edge < _threshold: log.debug("range_no_edge", ...); return None` precedes feature computation and the weighted probability. Executed log census since observe mode (logs/ait.log + ait.log.1-3, 2026-09-01 onward): event=range_prediction -> IWM 116, SPY 0, QQQ 0; event=range_no_edge -> SPY 118, QQQ 120 (e.g. `{"symbol": "QQQ", "weighted_edge": "+0.046", "threshold": "0.050", "event": "range_no_edge", "level": "debug"}`). trade_context columns = [trade_id, entry_direction, entry_confidence, entry_regime, entry_vix, entry_iv_rank, entry_sentiment_score, entry_signals, model_version] - no range probability; the two observe-mode trades (T-20260901-105959 SPY, T-20260901-110001 QQQ) carry entry_confidence 0.6 and entry_signals without any p_in_range. grep of scripts/, src/ait/monitoring, src/ait/reporting for 'range_prediction' -> no scorer exists.

**Trigger:** Every SPY/QQQ scan while the per-symbol CV edge is below 0.05 (the current artifact: SPY 0.541/0.541, QQQ 0.521/0.484 balanced acc). At the 50-close checkpoint the re-arm/retire decision for SPY/QQQ will have zero observations; IWM is the only symbol with a record.

**Consequence:** The pre-registered observe-mode judgement (score every logged range_prediction against realized outcome; compare closes with p_in_range above vs below 0.65) cannot be run for any condor entered on a symbol-day the CV-edge veto fired: both observe-mode trades so far (SPY, QQQ on 09-01) have no probability logged or persisted, and future coverage is decided by a day-to-day flapping CV statistic, so the 50-close re-arm/retire decision will be made on a selection-biased subset (IWM-heavy) rather than the record the PLAN promised.

**Suggested fix:** With entry_gates_enabled=false call predict() with min_edge_override=0.0 (or compute and emit the weighted probability alongside range_no_edge), persist p_in_range and weighted_edge into trade_context.entry_signals at entry, add the scorer the PLAN presupposes, and add an executing test that a gates-off scan on a symbol whose CV edge is below threshold still produces a logged and persisted probability.

## params-ml-research-02  [hardcoded | research_validity | live_path=True]

**Where:** src/ait/ml/range_predictor.py:83 (`min_edge_over_baseline: float = 0.05`), :130; src/ait/bot/orchestrator.py:253-256 (live constructor passes only threshold_pct/horizon_days); src/ait/orchestration/master.py:830 (retrain subprocess, same); config.yaml:156; src/ait/config/settings.py:325-328; PLAN.md:1699-1701

**Claim:** config.yaml backtest.min_edge_over_baseline (described as 'Min range-model CV edge to activate the gate') never reaches the LIVE range predictor: the orchestrator and the master retrain subprocess construct RangePredictor without the kwarg, so live runs the constructor literal 0.05. The observe-mode pre-registration debated 'lowering min_edge_over_baseline 0.05->0.04' as if the config value governed live; it does not (R19c config-authority violation on a live decision input).

**Evidence:** orchestrator.py:253-256: `self._range_predictor = RangePredictor(threshold_pct=_range_threshold, horizon_days=_range_horizon,)`; range_predictor.py:83 `min_edge_over_baseline: float = 0.05,` and :130 `self._min_edge_over_baseline = min_edge_over_baseline` with no resolve_config_value (contrast :92-100 where min_training_samples IS resolved from config). master.py:830 `range_pred = RangePredictor(threshold_pct=0.05, horizon_days=30)`. Only research paths thread it (walkforward.py:226-228, optimizer.py:211-213, engine.py:746 min_edge_override).

**Trigger:** Any operator edit of backtest.min_edge_over_baseline: research walk-forward/optimizer change, live gate/observe record unchanged - the PLAN's rejected option 1b (0.05->0.04) would have been a no-op live.

**Consequence:** The value that decides whether the live range model vetoes (gates ON) or is even logged (gates OFF, see finding 01) is a code literal; an operator changing backtest.min_edge_over_baseline changes every research study (walk-forward, optimizer, engine) but not live, so research and live silently run different gate thresholds the moment the config value is touched, and the 09-01 decision reasoned about a knob that does not reach the live path.

**Suggested fix:** In RangePredictor.__init__ default min_edge_over_baseline to None and resolve it via resolve_config_value(None, 'backtest', 'min_edge_over_baseline', BacktestConfig, settings) exactly as min_training_samples is; pass it explicitly from orchestrator.py and master.py; add an executing test that loads settings with min_edge_over_baseline=0.11 and asserts the live-constructed predictor's veto threshold (and the range_no_edge threshold field) equals 0.11.

## params-ml-research-03  [parameter | research_validity | live_path=True]

**Where:** config.yaml:156 (min_edge_over_baseline 0.05), :187-188 (lookback_days 504, min_training_samples 100); src/ait/ml/range_predictor.py:348-368 (_walk_forward_split n_splits=4, fold=n//5, gap=horizon), :394-396 and :427-428 (balanced_accuracy_score per fold), :286-297 and :473 (edge = sum w*(acc-0.50)); src/ait/optimization/param_spaces.py:36-38, 53-57; src/ait/optimization/optimizer.py:444-446; PLAN.md:1243-1245, 1682-1690; git e24cce6 (2026-06-08)

**Claim:** The 0.05 edge threshold has no recorded rationale anywhere (introduced 2026-06-08 as 'MIN_EDGE check'; the only 'study', Exp 28's Optuna search over [0.02,0.15], was proven vacuous by R20 because the dimension never reached the trial engine), and it is applied to a statistic whose sampling noise is of the same size: with lookback 504 (~502 trading rows after Yahoo '2y' + tail(504)) the range model trains on ~372 labeled rows, and the 4 purged validation folds contain 1/0/18/2 breakouts for SPY - balanced accuracy on a fold with 0 or 1 negative is quantized in 0.5 steps. The observe-mode decision's premise ('the model is telling the truth: it has no demonstrated skill', SPY edge +0.045 vs 0.05) and the 09-01 all-entries-blocked incident both rest on a threshold comparison that is noise-dominated.

**Evidence:** Executed probe replicating the trainer frame (store tail(502), FeatureEngine.compute, 9-day/5% labels, _walk_forward_split(n, 4, gap=9)) reproduces the live artifact's in-range rates exactly (SPY 85.2% / QQQ 72.4% / IWM 78.8% vs range_trained log 85.22% / 72.40% / 78.78%) and gives per-fold validation negatives: SPY [1, 0, 18, 2] of 74/74/74/67 rows; IWM [10, 15, 16, 4]; QQQ [3, 10, 21, 22]. Live artifact cv_scores: SPY xgb 0.5410 / lgb 0.5411 -> weighted edge +0.041; QQQ xgb 0.5210 / lgb 0.4839 -> fitted_weights {xgb 1.0, lgb 0.0}, edge +0.021 (log shows +0.046 for QQQ on the 09-01 artifact). param_spaces.py:53-57 gives only 'Range [0.02, 0.15]: 0.02 = accept almost any skill, 0.15 = require strong edge' - no derivation of 0.05. PLAN.md:1686-1688: 'edge over baseline +0.045 / +0.045 / +0.081 against a min_edge_over_baseline of 0.05. So predict() returns None for SPY and QQQ'.

**Trigger:** unjustified: no rationale anywhere for 0.05; concretely, on 2026-09-01 the SPY/QQQ decision (+0.045 vs 0.050) sat inside one breakout-sample of noise on 3 of 4 folds.

**Consequence:** The skill test that blocked every SPY/QQQ condor on 2026-09-01 (fail-closed gate) and that the observe-mode decision cites as 'the model is telling the truth: it has no demonstrated skill' compares a 0.05 margin against a statistic whose day-to-day swing on the same data is 0.04-0.10 and whose SPY folds contain 0-3 breakouts; the same model was judged 'skilled' on 08-31 15:50 and 09-04 and 'unskilled' in between, so both the incident and the premise are noise outcomes, and any re-arm at 50 closes that keeps this veto will flap the same way.

**Suggested fix:** Replace the point comparison with a statistically meaningful test: require a minimum count of validation negatives per fold (skip or merge folds below it), report a bootstrap CI on the weighted edge and gate on its lower bound, or switch to AUROC/log-loss over more, larger folds using the full 504-day lookback; record the derivation of whatever threshold results and re-register the observe-mode premise against it.

## params-ml-research-04  [doc_vs_code | research_validity | live_path=False]

**Claim:** Both pieces of evidence for range_min_confidence 0.65 (the original sweep and the 08-11 pre-registered floor study) were run on 30-day range models; W4's new range_spec.py asserts the opposite ('floor calibrated on the 9-day question') and used that to move live to the 9-day model without re-sweeping. At 9d/5% the in-range base rate is 72-85%, so a floor calibrated for the strictly harder 30-day question is now too permissive on the easier one (a no-skill model outputs ~the base rate, above 0.65). Separately, the pre-registered floor-study rule (PLAN.md:923-926) selects 0.60, not 0.65, on the study's own artifacts, and no verdict was ever recorded.

**Evidence:** reports/range_floor_study summaries: floor_0.65 PF 2.87 / DD 2.10% / n=89; floor_0.60 PF 2.90 / DD 2.09% / n=90; floor_0.55 PF 2.55; floor_0.50 PF 1.19; floor_0.70 PF 2.95 / DD 2.32%. Rule (PLAN.md:923-925): 'pick the LOWEST floor whose PF >= 0.95x the 0.65 arm's PF AND whose max DD <= the 0.65 arm's DD, requiring n >= 30' -> 0.60 (2.90 >= 2.727, 2.09 <= 2.10, n=90). grep PLAN.md for a floor-study verdict -> only the pre-registration (912) and 'floor sweep' mentions (937, 958, 995, 1236, 1271). Live artifact in_range_rate at horizon 9: SPY 85.22%, QQQ 72.40%, IWM 78.78% (range_trained events 2026-09-03T12:19). range_floor_study.py passes no horizon; git log shows _range_label_horizon introduced 1ed13be 2026-08-25, after the study (artifacts dated 2026-08-12/13).

**Trigger:** Inert live today (entry_gates_enabled false) but it is the engine's research gate (engine.py:748) and the value the re-arm decision at 50 closes will restore; when re-armed at horizon 9 it admits near-base-rate outputs.

**Consequence:** The W4 change that moved the live range model to 9 days rests on a false statement of the evidence: the 0.65 floor was calibrated at 30d (04-28 sweep) and 21d/adaptive-threshold (08-12 study), never at 9d/5%, so when the gate is re-armed at 50 closes the floor it restores has no study at the question it now asks, and the ledger carries a pre-registered floor study whose own rule picked 0.60 without any recorded outcome - the re-arm decision would inherit an unexamined 0.65 as if validated.

**Suggested fix:** Correct range_spec.py:12-18, orchestrator.py:241-249 and the W4 PLAN entry to state the floor's actual calibration horizons (30d fixed / 21d adaptive); record the floor-study outcome in PLAN (0.60 by rule, or explicitly void because it predates horizon 9, the touch stop and W5); re-sweep range_min_confidence on the 9-day/5% model under the W5 cost model before any re-arm.

## params-ml-research-09  [parameter | research_validity | live_path=False]

**Where:** config.yaml:138-140 (iv_floor 0.20 '= SPY/QQQ in normal markets'), :178-180 (skew_slope_per_pct_otm 0.0736 anchored at atm_iv 0.1289); src/ait/config/settings.py:199-201, 280-283; src/ait/backtesting/engine.py:1414-1420 (priority-1 stored IBKR IV), :1522 (`if strategy in CREDIT_STRATEGIES and iv < self._iv_floor: return None`), :1381-1387 (skew_adj += base_iv * slope * otm*100); data/historical.db daily_prices.implied_vol (probe)

**Claim:** The W5 skew constant is anchored at SPY atm_iv 0.1289, an IV level the engine can never price a condor at: engine.py:1522 refuses every credit entry with iv < iv_floor 0.20. Because the term is multiplicative in base_iv, at the lowest admissible IV (0.20) the total put slope is 0.10 + 0.20*100*0.0736 = 1.572 (1.5x the measured 1.049), and at 0.30 it is 2.31 (2.2x) - the 'relative' functional form is an untested extrapolation from a single low-vol calibration day (2026-08-11) into exactly the high-vol regimes that drive the drawdown numbers. In the same breath iv_floor's rationale ('0.20 = SPY/QQQ in normal markets') is contradicted by the repo's own stored IBKR IV: SPY spent 204 of the last 222 IV days below 0.20, so with the stored-IV path the engine excludes 92% of the recent SPY regime that live trades in.

**Evidence:** Executed probe on data/historical.db daily_prices (implied_vol > 0, date >= 2025-09-01): SPY 222 rows, 204 with IV < 0.20, min/max/avg 0.1054/0.2625/0.149, last 2026-09-01 = 0.1232; QQQ 111/222 below 0.20 (avg 0.2047); IWM 57/222 (avg 0.2201). engine.py:1415-1420 returns the stored implied_vol first when present. settings.py:280-283: 'Default 0.0736 makes the TOTAL put slope hit that 1.049 at the finding's own probe anchor (SPY atm_iv 0.1289, skew_factor 1.0): 0.10 + 0.1289 x 100 x 0.0736 = 1.049'. engine.py:1384 `skew_adj += base_iv * self._skew_slope_per_pct_otm * (otm * 100.0)`. Registered live-bt-mirrors-06 covers rank-vs-absolute gate divergence at VIX-proxy 0.18 but not the stored-IV exclusion magnitude nor the W5 anchor/iv_floor conflict.

**Trigger:** Every engine credit-structure build/reprice in the pending W5 walk-forward rerun: wings priced 1.5-2.3x steeper than measured in every admissible regime, and the SPY entry population restricted to the ~8% of recent days with IV >= 0.20.

**Consequence:** In the pending W5 walk-forward rerun every admissible credit build/reprice prices put wings on a slope of 1.57-2.31 (IV 0.20-0.30) against a measured ~0.94-1.14 that the repo's own data shows does not rise with IV — long-put wings 1.4-2.2x too expensive and short puts correspondingly rich, biasing credits, wing_k and drawdown conclusions in an uncalibrated direction; combined with the stored-IV path admitting only ~8% of recent SPY days, the 'corrected' cost model is being evaluated on a regime and a surface that were never measured.

**Suggested fix:** Fit the put slope in ABSOLUTE IV per unit |ln(K/S)| (the quantity actually regressed), or fit slope-vs-ATM-IV across the 15 groups (and across dates once daily samples accumulate) before choosing a functional form; make the calibration anchor lie inside the admissible IV region by re-deriving iv_floor from the operating IV distribution or converting it to the rank floor live uses (live-bt-mirrors-06); add an executing test that _get_leg_iv reproduces the measured slopes at each symbol's own ATM IV within tolerance; re-run W5 only after both.

## params-trading-04  [enforcement_gap | research_validity | live_path=True]

**Where:** PLAN.md:1719-1725 (observe-mode judging rule); src/ait/bot/orchestrator.py:1689-1704 (p_in_range only log.info'd; override only when gates on); src/ait/bot/orchestrator.py:1845-1857 (eff_conf = constant observe_mode_neutral_confidence); src/ait/execution/executor.py:382 (trades.ml_confidence = signal.confidence); config.yaml:258-259 (20 MB x 10 rotation)

**Claim:** The pre-registered rule for re-arming or retiring the ML gate ('compare closes where p_in_range was above vs below 0.65') depends on per-entry range probabilities that exist only in size-rotated log lines; the two DB confidence columns now hold a directional number (trades.ml_confidence) and a constant 0.60 (trade_context.entry_confidence), neither of which is the gate's prediction, so the observe-mode experiment cannot be scored from durable state once logs rotate.

Log retention measured: ait.log.10 first timestamp 2026-08-24T15:09Z, ait.log last 2026-09-03T21:00Z (~8 trading days) vs IC holds of 3-14 days and a 50-close verdict months away.

**Trigger:** Any close evaluated more than ~8 trading days after its entry (all of them at the 50-close read), or any log rotation before the analysis is run.

**Consequence:** The pre-registered rule for re-arming or retiring the ML gate cannot be scored from durable state: the per-entry range probability exists only in log lines that rotate out in ~9 trading days, trades.ml_confidence now silently changes meaning across epochs (range probability before 09-01, directional confidence after), and trade_context.entry_confidence is a constant 0.60 for every observe-mode entry - so at the 50-close read the experiment is unanswerable and any confidence-bucketed analysis mixes two definitions.

**Suggested fix:** At entry in observe mode persist range_pred.probability_in_range, threshold_pct and horizon_days (into entry_signals JSON or new trade_context columns) plus an eff_conf provenance tag; implement the scorer the PLAN describes (range_prediction vs realized range at horizon) against that durable store; add an executing test that a taken observe-mode trade round-trips p_in_range into trade_context.

## regress-live-08  [logic | research_validity | live_path=True]

**Where:** src/ait/bot/orchestrator.py:1846-1856 (observe-mode eff_conf = ml.observe_mode_neutral_confidence); 2244 (_try_execute(signal, confidence=eff_conf)); 2435 (TradeRequest.confidence); 2570 (save_trade_context(confidence=confidence)); src/ait/risk/position_sizer.py:101 (conf_adj from confidence); src/ait/ml/meta_label.py:419,520 (primary_confidence <- entry_confidence); orchestrator.py:1690-1693 (the only place p_in_range is ever written — an INFO log per symbol scan, and only when predict() returned a value)

**Claim:** The neutral-confidence sentinel (0.60) is not confined to the risk manager's min_confidence gate: it is the `confidence` argument of _try_execute and therefore (a) feeds the position sizer's confidence multiplier (inert today only because max_contracts_per_trade=1 clamps to 1 contract), and (b) is persisted as trade_context.entry_confidence for every observe-mode condor, so meta_label's primary_confidence feature becomes a constant 0.60 for the entire observe-mode sample. Meanwhile the quantity the 2026-09-01 pre-registration says will be scored per close ('compare closes where p_in_range was above vs below 0.65') is persisted nowhere per trade — only as a per-symbol-scan INFO log, and for SPY/QQQ predict() returns None (skill below min_edge) so it is not logged at all. The observe-mode verdict as registered cannot be computed from the database, and the directional confidence the risk gate used to see is lost from the journal.

**Evidence:** orchestrator.py:1849-1850 `eff_conf = float(self._settings.ml.observe_mode_neutral_confidence)`; 2570 `confidence=confidence,` inside save_trade_context(...) where `confidence` is _try_execute's parameter; position_sizer.py:101 `conf_adj = 0.5 + 0.5 * ((confidence - min_conf) / (1.0 - min_conf))`; meta_label.py:419 `"primary_confidence": trade.get("entry_confidence", 0.60)`. grep -rn 'p_in_range\|probability_in_range' src/ait shows no state.py/DuckDB writer — only orchestrator.py:1690-1693 (log.info) and the backtest engine/walkforward. PLAN 2026-09-01: 'every logged range_prediction is scored against the realized outcome ... compare closes where p_in_range was above vs below 0.65'.

**Trigger:** Every observe-mode condor since 2026-09-01: entry_confidence=0.60 in trade_context; at the 50-close review there is no per-trade p_in_range column to split on, and for SPY/QQQ there is no prediction at all.

**Consequence:** At the 50-close review the above/below-0.65 split can only be built from log lines and only for symbol-days whose model cleared min_edge that morning (IWM on 09-01; SPY today; never QQQ) — the SPY and QQQ condors already in the observe-mode sample have no p_in_range at all — so the pre-registered verdict is computable for a shifting, model-dependent subset rather than the sample; meanwhile trade_context.entry_confidence is a constant 0.60 for every observe-mode condor, which makes meta_label's primary_confidence a proxy for 'entered after 2026-09-01' and discards the directional confidence the journal used to carry (sizing is unaffected only because max_contracts_per_trade=1).

**Suggested fix:** At entry persist range_p_in_range (float or NULL) plus range_edge/range_reason into trade_context (new columns) or entry_signals JSON, logging range_no_edge at INFO with the raw ensemble probability instead of suppressing it; store the directional final_confidence as entry_confidence and the sentinel separately (e.g. validation_confidence) so meta_label keeps a real feature; add an executing test that runs the observe-mode scan with a below-edge symbol and asserts the persisted row carries NULL-with-reason rather than nothing.

## regress-research-02  [logic | research_validity | live_path=False]

**Where:** src/ait/backtesting/engine.py:1352-1387 (_get_leg_iv: put-side multiplicative skew applied at reprice as well as entry); engine.py:2127-2138 (_get_current_iv: 0.70*entry + 0.30*rv20*1.15, floored, unchanged by W5); engine.py:2140-2170 (_reprice_position uses _get_current_iv as the base for every leg); engine.py:2321-2337 (touch/gap booking); config.yaml:138 (backtest.iv_floor 0.20) and :177 (skew_slope_per_pct_otm 0.0736); tests/test_w5_engine_cost_model.py:197-201 (gap_bt fixture forces iv_floor=0.10) and :205-262 (asserts only gap > strike ordering, never the level against pre-W5); PLAN.md:1554-1557 ('all three corrections push the same pessimistic direction')

**Claim:** The W5 skew term is NOT pessimistic on the loss path: because the same moneyness-scaled uplift is applied at reprice, the long put wing gains far more IV than the short put on a touch/gap day, so the buyback the engine books for EVERY touch_stop is CHEAPER than the pre-W5 engine booked it. Under the shipped config (iv_floor 0.20, slope 0.0736) the strike-touch buyback at the finding's own geometry drops from 9.233 to 8.122 (-$111/contract of loss), the 1% gap buyback from 12.080 to 10.895 (-$119), the 2% gap from 15.480 to 14.284 (-$120), while entry credit only falls 7.787 -> 7.222 (-$57). Net: each touch-stop event is ~$55-63/contract MORE optimistic than before W5. The register's second prong for touch-gap-pricing (the exit-IV blend can sit BELOW entry IV on the crash day; suggested fix: floor exit IV at entry IV plus a crash bump) was not implemented, so PLAN's claim that the three W5 corrections all push pessimistically is false for the skew term.

**Evidence:** Probe (engine as shipped, condor 620/666 shorts, 576/710 wings, entry_iv 0.18, 21 DTE, held 7d): `floor 0.2: W5 value@640 d0 7.222 | touch@620 d7 8.122 | gap@613.8 d7 10.895 | gap@607.6(2%) 14.284` vs `floor 0.2: legacy(slope 0) value@640 d0 7.787 | touch@620 d7 9.233 | gap@613.8 d7 12.080 | gap@607.6(2%) 15.480`; with iv_floor 0.10 the same ordering (6.993 vs 8.186 at the strike). Crash-day exit IV: `_get_current_iv(pos, hist with 24 flat days then -4%)` = 0.1747 < entry 0.18 (register prong 2 reproduced); at exit IV 0.24 the 1% gap buyback would be 10.851 vs 9.758 at iv_floor 0.10. Per-leg IVs at the gapped open S=613.8: long_put K=576 0.2628, short_put K=620 0.1747 (ITM, no skew), i.e. the wing is inflated relative to the short strike exactly when the loss is booked. test_gap_loss_scales_with_gap_size asserts only `out['exit_price'] > prev`; no W5 test compares a touch-day booking against the slope-0 engine.

**Trigger:** Every touch_stop / gap exit in every walk-forward, Optuna, ablation or shadow run executed after W5 with the shipped config (skew_slope_per_pct_otm 0.0736, iv_floor 0.20). The planned 'corrected cost model' re-run (PLAN: the next milestone) would book smaller condor losses than the pre-W5 engine on the exact days the touch stop exists to price.

**Consequence:** Every touch_stop/gap exit in every post-W5 walk-forward, Optuna, ablation or shadow run under the shipped config books a buyback ~$110-126/contract cheaper than the pre-W5 engine (net ~$55-62/contract less loss per touch event after the smaller entry credit), because the moneyness-relative skew inflates the long put wing exactly when spot sits at the short strike while the base IV can even fall below entry IV on the crash day. The planned 'corrected cost model' re-run would therefore under-book condor losses on the precise days the touch stop exists to price, and PLAN's 'same pessimistic direction' statement is false for the skew term on the loss path.

**Suggested fix:** Apply the multiplicative skew relative to each strike's moneyness at ENTRY (sticky-strike: cache per-leg IV uplift in the position at open and reuse it in _reprice_position), or at minimum implement register prong 2 — on touch/gap days floor the reprice base IV at entry_iv plus a calibrated crash bump before _get_leg_iv — and add an executing test under the SHIPPED config asserting a touch-day booking is >= the slope-0 booking (fails today: 8.122 < 9.233; 8.65 < 9.833 through _check_exit).

## tests-honesty-08  [regression | research_validity | live_path=True]

**Where:** src/ait/ml/range_spec.py:39-41 (`LIVE_RANGE_THRESHOLD_PCT: float = 0.05`), 28-31 (docstring: 'live deliberately stays at the fixed +/-5% for now'); src/ait/backtesting/walkforward.py:2226-2253 (`_adaptive_range_threshold`, clip 0.02-0.15), 2360 and 2388 (window range models trained at the adaptive threshold), 1473; tests/test_w2_orchestrator_gates.py:456 (`assert threshold == 0.05`), 405-413 (mirror test compares horizon only); reports/relationship_defect_hunt_20260825.md:24-30 (units-scale-05: '9-day/adaptive-threshold question'; suggested enforcement test on '(threshold_pct, horizon_days) ... vs _range_label_horizon/_adaptive_range_threshold'); PLAN.md:1530-1534 (W4 '[units-scale-05] NEW range_spec.py = the ONE authority'); config.yaml:189 (range_min_confidence 0.65); PLAN.md:1683-1690

**Claim:** units-scale-05 is recorded as fixed by W4, but only the horizon half of the registered pair was closed: the live gate still asks P(stay within a fixed +/-5%) while the research that calibrated the 0.65 floor trains and evaluates every window at a vol-scaled adaptive threshold (0.02-0.15). The new tests pin the literal (`threshold == 0.05`) and mirror only the horizon, i.e. the register's own suggested enforcement test was written for one of the two numbers. The threshold is also a trading value homed in code, not config.yaml (no range-threshold key in config.yaml or settings.py), contrary to R19c.

**Evidence:** range_spec.py:28-31 admits it: 'Research uses _adaptive_range_threshold (clipped 0.02-0.15); live deliberately stays at the fixed +/-5% for now'. walkforward.py:2226-2253: `low_clip=0.02, high_clip=0.15 ... return 0.05 # insufficient data` else `np.clip(raw, low_clip, high_clip)`. Consequence already materialized: the 09-01 retrain at live_range_spec()=(0.05, 9) produced CV AUC 0.545 (PLAN.md:1683-1690) — a model answering a question the research floor was never validated on — and observe mode followed.

**Trigger:** unjustified: no rationale for 0.05 beyond 'the wing width the live condor actually sells'; the pair diverges whenever recent realized vol makes the adaptive threshold != 0.05, i.e. nearly always.

**Consequence:** The 0.65 range floor is validated by walk-forward evidence in which every window model answers P(stay within a vol-scaled band, ~3.5-8.3% for a 9-day horizon) while the live model answers P(stay within a fixed +/-5%). The floor is therefore loose in quiet regimes (live question easier than the validated one) and tight in volatile ones, and no test can fail when the two drift. When observe mode ends and entry_gates_enabled is re-armed, that mis-calibrated floor becomes the sole veto on every iron-condor entry again — with the ledger recording the pair as closed.

**Suggested fix:** Route the research window models through `live_range_spec()` (or have live adopt `_adaptive_range_threshold`) so the 0.65 sweep validates the live question; home the number as `ml.range_threshold_pct` in config.yaml -> MLConfig and have range_spec.py read it; and replace the `assert threshold == 0.05` pin in tests/test_w2_orchestrator_gates.py:456 with the two-number mirror the register asked for (threshold AND horizon vs `_adaptive_range_threshold`/`_range_label_horizon`).

## tests-honesty-10  [test_honesty | research_validity | live_path=False]

**Where:** tests/test_predict_from_features.py:175, 187, 207 (pytest.skip on `result is None`); tests/test_dynamic_exits.py:611-614 (`if not result: pytest.skip("Training did not succeed (possible env issue)")`); consumers: src/ait/backtesting/engine.py:1258-1261, src/ait/backtesting/walkforward.py:746 (predict_from_features), config.yaml meta_label.enabled false

**Claim:** Four default-lane tests skip on this box on every run, so their invariants are green-by-skip: 3 of the 5 RangePredictor.predict_from_features tests (probability in [0,1], confidence == max(p,1-p), predict_from_features == predict on the same row) skip with 'model returned None — no edge on synthetic data', and the only MetaLabeler train->predict round-trip skips with 'Training did not succeed'. Both are research-side (per-bar research predictions; meta-label is disabled live).

**Evidence:** `pytest tests/test_predict_from_features.py tests/test_dynamic_exits.py tests/test_vlmc_session_tiering.py -rs` -> '77 passed, 4 skipped'; SKIPPED lines: test_predict_from_features.py:175 'model returned None — no edge on synthetic data', :187 'model returned None', :207 'one or both predictions returned None', test_dynamic_exits.py:614 'Training did not succeed (possible env issue)'.

**Trigger:** Every run: the predict_from_features parity invariant between the walk-forward per-bar path and predict() has never actually been asserted on this box.

**Consequence:** Four default-lane tests are green-by-skip on every run on this box. The one that matters — that RangePredictor.predict_from_features(last_row) returns the same probability as predict(full_df) — has never executed its assertion, so a drift between the walk-forward per-bar range path and the path the live gate uses would leave the whole suite green while every walk-forward range gate decision (and the OOS numbers built on it) silently diverges from what live would have computed.

**Suggested fix:** Give the RangePredictor tests a fixture with a deterministic edge (e.g. a trending/mean-reverting synthetic series whose containment label is predictable) or construct with min_edge_over_baseline=0 / inject a pre-fitted model, so predict() returns a prediction; then delete the three `pytest.skip` guards so the invariants actually run. For test_dynamic_exits.py:611-614, keep the `except ImportError` skip (a genuine env gate) but turn the `if not result` skip into an assertion on a fixture built to train — a MetaLabeler that cannot train on its own build_training_data output is the defect the test should report, not a reason to pass.

## data-integrity-01  [bug | ops_reporting | live_path=False]

**Where:** data/ait_analytics.duckdb trades row T-20260707-093745-d65049 + daily_stats rows 2026-07-13 / 2026-07-22; src/ait/monitoring/duckdb_analytics.py:32-42 (_real_close_sql), :326,:481,:507,:536,:562,:589,:656 (call sites), :600-630 (get_rolling_sharpe); src/ait/dashboard/app.py:1210; src/ait/monitoring/analytics.py:134-137 (duck-first get_performance); scripts/restate_r16_dailystats.py (no duck mirror); bot_state keys r16_dailystats_0722_backfill, straddle_restatement

**Claim:** The DuckDB analytics mirror still holds PRE-restatement rows that the sqlite ledger was later corrected on, and the W6 rewiring of every duck consumer to the not-real-close authority now EXCLUDES a real $-575.33 close from the mirror: duck lifetime real-close P&L is +$1,063.59 (18 closes) vs sqlite +$488.26 (19 closes); duck daily_stats sums to +$1,088.89 (12W/6L) vs sqlite +$488.26 (11W/8L).

**Evidence:** Executed diff: trades T-20260707-093745-d65049 duck exit_reason_detailed='reconciler_unknown_exit' vs sqlite 'stop_loss_manual_flatten_restated (no combo quote; fills 0.49C+3.63P vs 9.72 entry)'; go_live.is_real_close('reconciler_unknown_exit') -> False, bucket 'reconciler_needs_review'. daily_stats 2026-07-13: duck (0,2,2,-103.2) vs sqlite (0,1,3,-128.5) [sqlite matches the 4 closes that day: +27.18,-127.69,-15.71,-12.28]; duck has NO 2026-07-22 row, sqlite has (0,0,1,-575.33). Same predicate run on both stores: sqlite (19, 488.26) vs duck (18, 1063.59); 90-day daily_stats window duck (17 rows, 1088.89) vs sqlite (18 rows, 488.26). duckdb_analytics.py:36-42 builds the predicate from go_live.NOT_REAL_CLOSE_PATTERNS ('%reconciler_unknown%'), used at :326,:481,:507,:536,:562,:589,:656. restate_d1.py:210-222 mirrors its own edits into duck, but the R16 daily_stats restatement and the 08-03 straddle restatement (bot_state 'straddle_restatement', 'r16_dailystats_0722_backfill') wrote sqlite only; sync_from_sqlite (duckdb_analytics.py:735) has no production caller.

**Trigger:** Any duck-backed surface whose window reaches 2026-07-13/07-22: dashboard rolling Sharpe (app.py:1210, lookback 90d -> cutoff 2026-06-07, today), get_strategy_breakdown(60d, cutoff 2026-07-07), any lifetime panel; the daily-summary Telegram / daily_report get_performance(30d/1d) are unaffected only until a restatement lands inside their window.

**Consequence:** Every duck-backed surface whose window reaches 2026-07-13/07-22 reports a book that is $600.63 better than the ledger: dashboard rolling-Sharpe (90d) sums 17 days at +$1,088.89 instead of 18 at +$488.26 (the -$575.33 day is simply absent, so the worst single day in the record never appears in the Sharpe series), and any lifetime/60d duck panel reports 18 closes at +$1,063.59 / 12W-6L instead of 19 at +$488.26 / 11W-8L. W6 made it worse rather than better: before W6 the duck copy of the predicate did not know '%reconciler_unknown%', so that real close still counted; after W6 the stale mirror string silently excludes it. Any restatement that lands inside a 30d/1d window (daily_report, daily-summary Telegram, which go duck-first via analytics.py:134) will diverge the same way with no error.

**Suggested fix:** Re-derive the mirror from the ledger rather than patching it: run sync_from_sqlite (duckdb_analytics.py:737) at bot boot and after every restatement, and extend it to daily_stats (today it has no production caller at all). Make restatement mirror-aware — restate_d1.py must also UPDATE exit_reason_detailed, restate_r16_dailystats.py must upsert the duck daily_stats row — or forbid direct sqlite edits without a mirror step. Add a referee/smoke check that asserts trades count+SUM(realized_pnl) under _real_close_sql() and daily_stats count+SUM(total_pnl) agree between the two stores, which would have failed the moment this row diverged.

## data-integrity-06  [regression | ops_reporting | live_path=False]

**Where:** src/ait/ml/trainer.py:221-222 (prev_scores = predictor.cv_scores), :290-296 (new_scores = all_symbol_mean), :365-376 (_should_rollback averages both), :515-521 (bot boot path loads models first); src/ait/ml/ensemble.py:368 (`self._cv_scores = accuracies` per symbol), :399 (pkl stores last symbol's cv_scores); src/ait/orchestration/master.py retrain script (~:815-850: fresh DirectionPredictor, no load_models); logs/ait.log 2026-09-04T11:32:28Z; logs/retrain_20260904_073000.log

**Claim:** The R17/R19 'auto-rollback now real' guard compares mismatched baselines — prev_scores is the LAST-symbol-only cv_scores loaded from the pickle while new_scores is the all-symbol mean — so on 09-04 an identical-skill boot-trained ensemble was declared degraded and rolled back, while the daily 07:30 retrain subprocess (which produces the live model) never runs the check at all because it starts with an unloaded predictor (prev_scores={}).

**Evidence:** ait.log 2026-09-04T11:32:28Z model_performance_degraded prev_scores={'xgboost': 0.6131510194804524, 'lightgbm': 0.5874935476139768} (= IWM-only, the last symbol of the 07:30 run; identical to models/ensemble.pkl cv_scores) new_scores={'all_symbol_mean': 0.5463513706183006} rolling_back_to='v-20260904-073031'. retrain_20260904_073000.log per-symbol ensemble accuracies: SPY 0.5295/0.4803, QQQ 0.5270/0.5406, IWM 0.6132/0.5875 -> mean 0.54635 == new_scores exactly, i.e. the boot model had the same skill as the one it was rolled back to. retrain_20260904_073000.log contains no model_performance_degraded/rollback line (subprocess prev_scores empty: trainer.py:296 `if prev_scores and results`). trainer.py:372-376 `prev_avg = sum(prev_scores.values())/len(...); return new_avg < prev_avg - 0.05`. Artifact skew after the rollback: models/ensemble.pkl version v-20260904-073031 (07:30 subprocess) while models/range.pkl trained_at 2026-09-04T07:32:26 and vol_magnitude.pkl 07:32:28 (boot run, not rolled back).

**Trigger:** Any bot boot training after a subprocess retrain where the last-trained symbol's accuracy exceeds the 3-symbol mean by >0.05 (09-04); conversely a genuinely degraded 07:30 retrain is never rolled back.

**Consequence:** The auto-rollback guard is wrong in both directions. False positive, already fired: on 2026-09-04 an ensemble with per-symbol accuracies identical to the incumbent was declared degraded and reverted, leaving models/ensemble.pkl on the 07:30 artifact while vol_magnitude.pkl stayed on the 07:32 boot artifact — a mixed-provenance model set with no record that the two came from different runs. It fires whenever the last-trained symbol's accuracy exceeds the 3-symbol mean by >0.05, which the current universe ordering (IWM last, consistently the strongest symbol) makes the normal case, so every boot retrain is liable to be reverted. False negative, the dangerous half: the 07:30 subprocess that actually produces the live artifact starts from an unloaded predictor, so prev_scores is empty and the rollback branch is never entered — a genuinely collapsed retrain is written to models/ensemble.pkl and served to the bot with no check at all.

**Suggested fix:** Compare like with like: persist the all-symbol mean (or the full per-symbol score map) at the pickle's top level so a loaded baseline has the same shape as new_scores, and have _should_rollback assert the shapes match rather than averaging whatever it is handed. Have the master retrain subprocess call predictor.load_models() before train_all_symbols so the production path actually carries a baseline and the guard runs where it matters. Roll the range and vol-magnitude artifacts back together with the ensemble, or stamp the surviving artifacts with the run that produced them. Pin it with an executing test: three symbols whose new run reproduces the incumbent's per-symbol accuracies exactly must NOT roll back (fails today), and a retrain from an unloaded predictor must still evaluate the guard.

## data-integrity-08  [enforcement_gap | ops_reporting | live_path=False]

**Where:** src/ait/execution/executor.py:436-440 (only writer of _order_ctx_map, inside ENTRY placement, keyed on signal.entry_price), :1549 (ledger reads ctx default (0,0,0)); src/ait/reporting/go_live.py:306-309 (`WHERE e.live_mid > 0 ... ORDER BY e.exec_time DESC LIMIT 20`); data/ait_state.db executions

**Claim:** Fill-quality context (signal_price/live_mid/nbbo_spread) is captured for entry BAG rows only; every exit fill is written with 0/0/0, so the pinned 'trailing 20 fills' slippage gate is computed on 7 entry fills spanning 08-04..09-01 and exit slippage — where limit chasing actually happens — is unmeasured on every surface.

**Evidence:** Executed: rows with live_mid>0 = 7: T-20260804-095630 (6.44 vs mid 6.085), T-20260804-105137 (4.31/4.24), T-20260810-095326 (5.44/5.85), T-20260817-141947 (4.29/4.335), T-20260817-141950 (6.12/6.26), T-20260901-105959 (5.32/5.34), T-20260901-110001 (6.43/6.43) — all entry BAG rows. Exit BAG rows e.g. T-20260824-100040 exec 000182cd.6a9550c9.01.01 (order 1651506, price 2.99): signal_price 0.0, live_mid 0.0, nbbo_spread 0.0; same for every exit of the 08-04..08-31 condors (per-trade sums: 9 of 10 rows have live_mid=0). The 08-24 entry BAG row has signal_price 6.57 but live_mid 0.0 (pre-W2 nan NBBO) so it is also excluded. executor.py:436 `self._order_ctx_map[trade.order.orderId] = (abs(signal.entry_price or 0), self._last_live_mid, self._last_nbbo_spread)` has no exit-side counterpart (single write site by grep). Adjacent to policy-vs-impl-2 / dead-surface-2 (statistic and zeroing already registered); the exit blind spot is not.

**Trigger:** Every go-live readout today: slip_n=7 over five weeks for a gate specified as trailing 20; any exit chased through the ladder never enters the sample.

**Consequence:** Exit fill quality is unrecorded everywhere, even though the exit path already fetches the combo NBBO to price its own limit — so the one place the bot deliberately crosses the spread (EXIT_CROSS, plus the R13 stale-exit cancel/re-place ladder) has never been measured against a mid, on any surface, and cannot be checked against the engine's exit cost model. CORRECTION to the finder's framing: PLAN.md:498 pins the gate as 'median ENTRY slippage <= 8% of credit over the trailing 20 fills', so exits being outside that sample is correct, not a violation — slip_n=7 is simply the number of combo entries carrying context (7 of 9 entry orders), not starvation caused by the exit gap. Adding exit rows without an entry-side filter in go_live.py:305-311 would silently break the pinned definition.

**Suggested fix:** Capture (target price, live combo mid, NBBO spread) into _order_ctx_map at exit-order placement — the values already exist as the bid/ask locals in orchestrator._close_multi_leg — and either add the three fields to PendingExitOrder.__slots__ or key the ctx map by the exit orderId. Simultaneously add a side/entry filter to the go_live slippage query so criterion [4] stays entry-only per PLAN.md:498, and report exit slippage as a separate line. Test: an exit execution row must carry live_mid > 0, and the go_live slip sample must contain no exit rows.

**Suggested fix:** Write a per-session liveness row from the monitor loop — first cycle ts, last cycle ts, cycles run — into daily_stats or bot_state, and have status.py and the daily report print 'NO SESSION EVIDENCE' for any weekday with zero cycles (and 'DEGRADED SOD BASELINE' when mtm_sod came from the 09:30 fallback rather than the 16:00 prestamp). Do not spend effort on a keeper relaunch bug: keeper.log proves the keeper was down with everything else; if unattended overnight coverage matters, the fix is machine-level (disable sleep / run the keeper as a service that starts at boot).

**Claim:** The documented rollback path does not exist: the only deploy tags are deploy-20260714/deploy-20260714b; every deploy since (R16, R17, R19, R20, R21, W1-W6, observe mode — 96 commits) is untagged, so 'git checkout <last-good-tag>' rolls back to pre-R16 code carrying the live-money bugs those rounds fixed.

**Evidence:** `git tag -l` -> deploy-20260714, deploy-20260714b (nothing else). `git describe --tags` -> deploy-20260714b-96-g43d0409. RUNBOOK.md:94-96: 'For this to exist: TAG every verified deploy going forward ... Only tag AFTER step 4 passes.'
