(iii) Drive `_process_completed_exits` with a `long_straddle` exit and a `bull_put_spread` exit and assert which one records a drift direction, then delete the source-string literal pin. (ii) Delete the getsource half of `test_inertness_is_documented_at_both_sites`; its sibling already proves the behaviour.

## tests-honesty-07  [test_honesty | ops_reporting | live_path=True]

**Where:** tests/test_w1_booking_integrity.py:57-68 (`o._pdt_guard = MagicMock()`, `o._thompson = MagicMock()`, `o._find_trade_by_id = lambda tid: SimpleNamespace(...)`), 106-121 (asserts stats/breaker/thompson only); src/ait/bot/orchestrator.py _drain_exit_outbox (`_t = self._find_trade_by_id(trade_id)` ... `if datetime.fromisoformat(_t.entry_time).date() == today: self._pdt_guard.record_day_trade(_t.symbol)` under `except Exception as _e: log.debug("pdt_record_failed", ...)`), 3706-3708 (`_find_trade_by_id` -> `self._state.get_trade_by_id`); src/ait/bot/state.py:499-507; PLAN.md:1470-1476 ('books the orphans ... into daily stats, the circuit breaker, PDT, and Thompson')

**Claim:** PLAN's W1 claim is proven for stats/breaker/Thompson only. The drain test replaces `_find_trade_by_id` ON THE OBJECT UNDER TEST with a SimpleNamespace factory, so the real lookup (`get_trade_by_id` -> `_row_to_trade` on a CLOSED row) is never executed by any W1 test, and `_pdt_guard` is a MagicMock that is never asserted; in production a failure inside that branch is swallowed at DEBUG ('pdt_record_failed'). 'Reaches PDT exactly once' therefore lives in the ledger, not in the suite. The MagicMock collaborators also violate tests/fakes.py's own no-MagicMock rule (a `record_day_trade`/`record_outcome` signature drift is invisible).

**Evidence:** tests/test_w1_booking_integrity.py:65-67 `o._find_trade_by_id = lambda tid: SimpleNamespace(trade_id=tid, symbol="QQQ", strategy="iron_condor", entry_time=datetime.now().isoformat(), legs="[]")`; :120 `o._thompson.record_outcome.assert_called_once()` is the only collaborator assertion; no `record_day_trade` assertion anywhere in the file (grep).

**Trigger:** `_row_to_trade` raising on a closed row, or a `record_day_trade` signature change: every orphan close silently skips PDT (and Thompson if `_t` is None) while the W1 tests stay green. Would be live_money on a sub-25k account; today pdt_account_under_25k=false.

**Consequence:** The ledger's W1 entry asserts PDT is booked by the drain and cites 13 executing tests as proof; no test asserts it, and the production call sits behind a DEBUG-swallowed inner try with the real trade lookup never exercised on a CLOSED row. Today PDT recording is a config no-op so nothing is miscounted; at the $3k go-live scale (pdt_account_under_25k true) an orphan same-day close that fails inside that branch silently undercounts the 5-day day-trade window — the guard that exists to prevent a broker PDT restriction — with the W1 suite still green.

**Suggested fix:** In `_orch`, drop the `_find_trade_by_id` lambda (the trade is already seeded in the real StateManager by `_seed_trade`) and replace `o._pdt_guard = MagicMock()` with a real PDTGuard built on AccountConfig(pdt_protection=True, pdt_account_under_25k=True) attached to the same StateManager. Then assert the `pdt_day_trades` state grows by one for the same-day orphan close and does NOT grow for the prior-day orphan (which the drain skips at orchestrator.py:686-692). Same for Thompson: a real ThompsonSampler so a record_outcome signature change breaks the test.

## time-calendar-03  [logic | ops_reporting | live_path=True]

**Where:** src/ait/bot/orchestrator.py:1149-1151 (`if self._scheduler.should_avoid_new_trades(): ... return`) then :1159 (`await self._drain_exit_outbox()` — the ONLY call site); src/ait/bot/orchestrator.py:674-692 (drain: `today = _now_et().date()`, `if exit_d != today:` -> `stale_exit_booking_skipped` -> `continue`); src/ait/bot/orchestrator.py:3357-3402 (_post_market reconcile, no drain), :557 (_pre_market reconcile), :472 (startup reconcile); src/ait/execution/reconciler.py:448 and :855 (close_trade writers); src/ait/bot/state.py:383 (exit_time = datetime.now()), :773-786 (pending_exit_bookings); consumer of the lost numbers: src/ait/risk/manager.py:126-147 (_count_recent_losing_days reads daily_stats.total_pnl) -> :577,:604 (sizer recent_losing_days); test tests/test_w1_booking_integrity.py:135-142 (pins the discard only)

**Claim:** The W1 exit-outbox drain is reachable only from _trading_cycle AFTER the last-15-minutes early return and never runs in POST_MARKET, pre-market or the fast monitor, so any orphan close whose exit_time falls in [close-15min, next open) — post-market reconcile closes, book_vanished/sweep closes and crash-lost executor bookings in the final 15 minutes, closes booked by a keeper restart's startup reconcile late in the session — is first drained on the NEXT session, where the `exit_d != today` rule discards it: daily_stats (trades_won/lost, total_pnl), Thompson and the ORPHAN CLOSE BOOKED page never see it, and the day's total_pnl fed to the drawdown-throttle sizer is understated. The day-bucketing rule was designed for overnight crashes but the drain's own schedule manufactures 'prior-day' orphans every session.

**Evidence:** Executed _trading_cycle on a stub orchestrator: `should_avoid_new_trades=False: check_fills_safe called=True _drain_exit_outbox called=True`; `should_avoid_new_trades=True: check_fills_safe called=False _drain_exit_outbox called=False` (log: skipping_new_trades reason=close_to_market_close). grep: `_drain_exit_outbox` appears at :641 (def) and :1159 only; _post_market (:3357-3402) calls self._reconciler.reconcile() which issues state.close_trade (reconciler.py:448) with exit_time = datetime.now() (state.py:383) but never drains. Drain rule: `if exit_d != today: ... log.warning("stale_exit_booking_skipped", ...) ... continue` (orchestrator.py:686-692) — the continue precedes stats, breaker, Thompson and the notification. PLAN W1 entry states the intent as 'Prior-day orphans are claimed but NOT booked'; test_w1_booking_integrity.py:135-142 pins that discard with a 26h-old row and no test exercises a same-session orphan created after 15:45 or in POST_MARKET.

**Trigger:** Any close_trade not booked by the executor callback whose exit_time is after (market_close - 15 min): a manual TWS flatten or assignment discovered by the 16:00-16:15 post-market reconcile, a vanished-position booking from the fast monitor after 15:45, a bot crash between close and callback after 15:45, or a keeper relaunch at 15:5x whose startup reconcile closes stale rows. On early-close days the window is 12:45-13:15 ET. The orphan is claimed and logged as stale_exit_booking_skipped the next morning and is never booked anywhere.

**Consequence:** Every reconciler/sweep/vanished close whose exit_time lands in the last ~15-20 minutes of the session or in the 16:00-16:15 post-market window is claimed the next morning as stale_exit_booking_skipped and never booked anywhere: daily_stats for that day (total_pnl, trades_won/lost) is permanently understated, Thompson never sees the outcome, and no ORPHAN CLOSE BOOKED page fires — so a manual flatten or assignment found at 16:05 is silently missing from the day ledger. Second-order live effect: the sizer's recent_losing_days can read one fewer losing day, weakening the 3-day drawdown throttle in the rare case the missing loss flips that day's sign.

**Suggested fix:** Call _drain_exit_outbox before the should_avoid_new_trades return (and once from _post_market immediately after reconcile()); change the stale rule to bucket by session instead of discard: for exit_d != today skip only the breaker/PDT daily quantities but write pnl/win-loss into get_daily_stats(exit_d), record Thompson, and still page. Add an executing test: close_trade at 15:50 with the scheduler in the last-15-min state, run _trading_cycle, assert the booking landed in that day's stats; and a second test that a 16:05 reconciler close drained the next morning credits daily_stats for the prior date.

## data-integrity-10  [bug | cosmetic | live_path=False]

**Where:** data/ait_state.db bot_state (trade_regime_* 171 keys, mtm_sod_* 41 keys), open_positions.current_price; src/ait/bot/orchestrator.py:2533 (only trade_regime_ writer), :909/:1891 (mtm_sod writers); src/ait/bot/state.py:451-470 (insert sets current_price=entry_price), :595-609 (update_position_mark never touches current_price); data/historical.db daily_prices skeleton rows; src/ait/data/historical.py:279-283 (INSERT OR IGNORE skeleton), :209 (daily SELECT has no NULL-close filter)

**Claim:** Store hygiene defects with misleading values: (a) 141 of 171 bot_state trade_regime_* keys reference trade_ids that no longer exist in trades and the key has zero readers in src (write-only, never pruned); mtm_sod_* keys accumulate without pruning; (b) open_positions.current_price is set once to entry_price and never updated, so both live rows show current_price == entry_price (-5.32 / -6.43) beside pnl_pct 0.148 / 0.009 and mark_time 09-04; (c) daily_prices contains three all-NULL skeleton rows (IWM 2024-06-11, NVDA 2024-06-11, QQQ 2024-06-24: OHLC NULL, volume 0, implied_vol NULL) that the daily loader returns as NaN closes.

**Evidence:** Executed: `select count(*) from bot_state where key like 'trade_regime_%' and substr(key,14) not in (select trade_id from trades)` -> 141 (of 171); grep 'trade_regime_' src -> only orchestrator.py:2533 set_state, no get_state. open_positions rows: T-20260901-105959-c4ee1b entry_price -5.32 current_price -5.32 unrealized_pnl 78.54 pnl_pct 0.1476 mark_time 2026-09-04T15:59:50; T-20260901-110001-944d47 -6.43/-6.43/6.07/0.0094. state.py:606-608 `UPDATE open_positions SET unrealized_pnl = ?, pnl_pct = ?, mark_time = ?` only; grep '.current_price' in src (excluding get_current_price) -> no reader. daily_prices: ('IWM','2024-06-11',None,None,None,None,0,None), ('NVDA','2024-06-11',...), ('QQQ','2024-06-24',...); historical.py:279-283 `INSERT OR IGNORE INTO daily_prices (symbol, date)` creates OHLCV-less rows; :209 `SELECT date, open, high, low, close, volume FROM daily_prices WHERE symbol = ?` returns them (outside the current 504-day training window, inside any >2.3y research request).

**Trigger:** Unbounded growth of bot_state; any consumer that ever reads open_positions.current_price (dashboard/report) shows the entry price as the mark; research frames spanning back to mid-2024 for IWM/QQQ/NVDA get a NaN close row.

**Consequence:** Unbounded write-only growth in bot_state (141 orphan trade_regime_ keys and 41 mtm_sod_ keys, never pruned by the one sweep that exists); a dead open_positions.current_price column frozen at the entry price beside a live pnl_pct and a fresh mark_time, which will read as 'the mark' the first time anyone puts it on a dashboard or report; and daily_prices skeleton rows that historical.load returns as NaN closes — harmless for the live SPY/QQQ/IWM universe inside the 504-day window, but any AAPL frame after 2026-04-27 is entirely NaN closes, and IWM/QQQ frames reaching back to mid-2024 carry a NaN row.

**Suggested fix:** Delete trade_regime_/mtm_sod_ keys in the existing R16 post-market sweep at orchestrator.py:3362-3377 (widen the pattern list; keep mtm_sod for the last ~10 sessions), or drop the trade_regime_ write at :2533 entirely since nothing reads it. Either maintain current_price inside update_position_mark or drop the column. Add `AND close IS NOT NULL` to historical.load's SELECT and purge the skeleton rows; a research-facing loader should also warn when a symbol's tail is IV-only.

## docs-policy-07  [doc_vs_code | cosmetic | live_path=False]

**Where:** config.yaml:120-126 (backtest.wing_k comment block); src/ait/config/runtime_env.py:52-56, :112-118 (CONFIG_BACKED), :242; run_orchestrator.py:35-37

**Claim:** config.yaml's wing_k comment states the opposite of the R19b/R19c authority: 'LIVE runs k=1.6 with ratio floor 0.10 via env in run_orchestrator.py (AIT_IC_WING_K / AIT_IC_MIN_CREDIT_WIDTH — the authority for live). This yaml value feeds backtest/integration paths.' Since R19b, run_orchestrator.py sets nothing; apply_runtime_env_defaults seeds env FROM config.yaml (env > config > CONTRACT_DEFAULTS), so this yaml value IS the live value. An operator following the comment would edit env instead of config.

**Evidence:** grep AIT_IC_WING_K in run_orchestrator.py/src/ait/main.py: no setter; only runtime_env.py:78 (CONTRACT_DEFAULTS) and :113 ('AIT_IC_WING_K': ('backtest','wing_k')). run_orchestrator.py:37 `apply_runtime_env_defaults()`; runtime_env.py contract_str: 'env override > config.yaml > declared default'. PLAN.md R19c: 'config.yaml IS NOW THE OPERATING SOURCE ... env only overrides'.

**Trigger:** Operator reads the comment when changing wing sizing.

**Consequence:** An operator changing wing sizing is sent to a file that sets nothing (run_orchestrator.py) and is told the yaml value is backtest-only. The live hazard is the inverse of the finder's: someone editing config.yaml backtest.wing_k for a research sweep silently changes LIVE wing sizing at the next bot restart, believing the comment that it 'feeds backtest/integration paths'. No value is wrong today (yaml 1.6 == contract default 1.6 == the value every live reader resolves), so nothing mis-trades until someone acts on the comment.

**Suggested fix:** Replace config.yaml:124-126 with the R19c truth: 'THIS value is the live authority (env AIT_IC_WING_K in .env is an explicit deployment/study override only; run_orchestrator.py sets nothing since R19b).' Same for the ic_min_credit_width sentence.

## docs-policy-08  [doc_vs_code | cosmetic | live_path=False]

**Where:** config.yaml:132-136 (backtest.credit_loss_limit comment 'Optuna optimizes per walk-forward window [0.3 – 2.0]'); src/ait/optimization/param_spaces.py (no credit_loss_limit dimension); src/ait/backtesting/walkforward.py:147,1432,2539

**Claim:** The config comment claims Optuna tunes credit_loss_limit per walk-forward window in [0.3, 2.0]; no such search dimension exists — the value is passed through unchanged as credit_loss_limit_mult.

**Evidence:** param_spaces.py key list (grep '^    "[a-z_]*":'): bear_put_spread, bull_call_spread, colsample_bytree, delta_iv_scale, delta_long, delta_short, gamma, hurst_*, iron_condor, iv_floor, iv_rank_rise_threshold, ..., max_entry_vol_annual, max_hold_days, min_confidence, min_edge_over_baseline, multifractal_max_width, profit_target_pct, stop_loss_pct, trailing_stop_fraction, wing_k — no credit_loss_limit. grep credit_loss_limit in src/ait/optimization: no hits; walkforward.py:147 `credit_loss_limit_mult: float | None = None  # env AIT_CREDIT_LOSS_LIMIT` passthrough only.

**Trigger:** unjustified: stale comment; anyone reading it assumes the flat stop is research-tuned.

**Consequence:** The single R19c operating-authority file asserts that the flat credit stop is a research-tuned, per-window-optimised parameter. It is a constant 0 that no Optuna study has ever touched. A reader deciding whether the flat stop is 'off because research said so' vs 'off because nobody wired it' gets the wrong answer from the file that is supposed to be the decision record; no trading value changes.

**Suggested fix:** Delete the 'Optuna optimizes per walk-forward window [0.3 – 2.0]' line (keep the 0.3/0.5/1.0/2.0 units legend), or, if per-window tuning was actually intended, add the dimension to IRON_CONDOR_SPACE — R6/R12-B1 evidence says it should stay disabled, so deletion is the honest fix.

## docs-policy-09  [doc_vs_code | cosmetic | live_path=True]

**Where:** PLAN.md:1683-1685 ('the overnight retrain rebuilt the range model at the corrected horizon 9 (W2's spec-mismatch flag worked)'); logs/retrain_20260901_081942.log, retrain_20260902_200717.log, retrain_20260903_081722.log, retrain_20260904_073000.log; src/ait/orchestration/master.py:830; logs/ait.log (range_model_spec_mismatch CRITICAL daily)

**Claim:** The attribution is wrong: every master.py retrain — including the 09-01 overnight one the ledger credits — trains the range model at horizon 30; the horizon-9 artifact is produced seconds later by the BOT's in-process boot retrain after master restarts it 'for fresh models'. This repeats every morning (master 07:30 writes 30 -> bot 07:31 logs CRITICAL range_model_spec_mismatch -> bot 07:32 retrains at 9), so the live range model is re-fit daily at boot and a CRITICAL fires daily as noise. PLAN registers master.py:830 as STILL OPEN, but records the flag as having 'worked' and cites the boot-retrained model's CV AUC as if it were the scheduled retrain's.

**Evidence:** retrain_20260901_081942.log: 'horizon=30 ... version=range-v-20260901-082016' (all three symbols); ait.log 2026-09-01T12:17:37Z range_model_spec_mismatch loaded_version=range-v-20260901-082016 loaded_horizon_days=30. Same pattern 09-02 20:07 (range-v-20260902-200753 h=30) -> bot 00:11Z range_models_saved horizon 9 (range-v-20260902-201136); 09-03 08:17 (h=30) -> 12:19Z retrain h=9; 09-04 07:30 (range-v-20260904-073033 h=30) -> 11:31:54Z CRITICAL mismatch -> 11:32:04-26Z range_models_saved horizon 9 (range-v-20260904-073204/215/226). models/range.pkl mtime Sep 4 07:32 = the bot's rebuild, not master's.

**Trigger:** Every weekday 07:30-07:32 ET; any real spec mismatch is indistinguishable from the daily one.

**Consequence:** One ledger sentence (PLAN.md:1683-1684) credits the 07:30/overnight scheduled retrain with a rebuild it did not do — that artifact was h=30; the h=9 model was built ~90 seconds later by the bot's boot retrain. A future reader reconstructing the observe-mode decision, or debugging the range model, will look in the wrong log (retrain_*.log, which only ever shows horizon=30) for the model whose AUC the entry quotes. The daily CRITICAL and the daily boot re-fit are real but are the already-registered STILL-OPEN master.py:830 symptom, and no decision number in the entry is wrong.

**Suggested fix:** Correct the sentence to 'the bot's boot retrain (triggered by W2's spec-mismatch flag after master's 08:19 retrain wrote horizon 30) rebuilt the range model at horizon 9'. Then close the registered master.py:830 item by constructing RangePredictor from live_range_spec() inside the retrain subprocess, which also removes the daily CRITICAL that currently masks a genuine spec divergence.

## hardcoded-live-05-entry-pacing-literals-shadow-max-daily-trades  [hardcoded | cosmetic | live_path=True]

**Where:** src/ait/bot/orchestrator.py:1933-1957 (_get_trade_budget: 2 before 10:30, 4 before 14:00), :2189-2191 (COOLDOWN_MINUTES = 120 — moved/added since c9774a2 in _duplicate_guard_verdict), :2370-2375 (SETTLE_SECONDS = 300), :1824 (signals[:4]), :2385-2395 (first-hour 0.85 directional gate); config.yaml:22 (trading.max_daily_trades 12 'Raised for higher trade volume / faster learning'); src/ait/config/settings.py:33

**Claim:** trading.max_daily_trades cannot take effect before 14:00 ET: literal pacing tiers cap entries at 2 in the first hour and 4 until 14:00 regardless of the config value, and the per-symbol+strategy re-entry cooldown (120 min) and post-restart settle (300 s) are additional literal throttles on the verdict-sample accumulation rate with no config home.

**Evidence:** Executed probe (max_daily_trades=12, trades_taken=0, now_et patched): 09:45 -> budget 2; 11:00 -> 4; 13:59 -> 4; 14:01 -> 12. Code: `if hour_min < 10.5: budget = 2 / elif hour_min < 14.0: budget = 4 / else: budget = max_trades / return min(budget, max_trades) - taken` (1950-1957); `COOLDOWN_MINUTES = 120` (2189); `SETTLE_SECONDS = 300  # 5 min` (2370). git diff c9774a2..HEAD shows `+        COOLDOWN_MINUTES = 120` as new code.

**Trigger:** unjustified: no rationale in config; binds on any session with >=4 entries before 14:00 (e.g. two morning take-profits freeing cluster slots plus re-entries across SPY/QQQ/IWM) and on every re-entry within 120 min of a same-symbol entry.

**Consequence:** trading.max_daily_trades is a ceiling, not the operating pacing: before 10:30 ET at most 2 entries and before 14:00 at most 4 are possible regardless of the configured 12, and re-entry on a symbol is throttled 120 min and startup 300 s by literals — none visible in config.yaml. No entry has been lost to these in the retained logs; the effect is a possible delay of the third symbol's condor to 10:30 and a slower post-close re-entry, i.e. an R19c config-authority debt item rather than a wrong trade.

**Suggested fix:** Either delete the tiers so max_daily_trades governs (the daily budget and the duplicate/cooldown guards already pace entries), or give them a home (trading.entry_budget_tiers: [[10.5, 2], [14.0, 4]], trading.reentry_cooldown_minutes: 120, trading.startup_settle_seconds: 300) read from settings; pin with an executing test that the 11:00 budget equals the configured value and that an empty tier list yields max_daily_trades.

## hardcoded-live-08-execution-microstructure-literals-no-config-home  [parameter | cosmetic | live_path=True]

**Where:** src/ait/execution/executor.py:36 (240 s timeout), :126 (0.02 buffer), :145 (`offset = max(0.10, round(0.15 * abs(entry_price), 2))`), :310-311 (8 orders / 60 s), :681-682 (15 x 0.1 s NBBO poll), :700 (disorderly spread > 1.0 x mid, 0.05 mid floor), :708 (signal-vs-live drift > 0.35), :990 (partial remainder 30 s), :1078/:1129/:1253 (exit stale 300 s), :1151 (exit young 10 s), :1285 (zombie 900 s), :1323-1324 (_LADDER_STEPS (0.25, 0.60, 1.00), _LADDER_STEP_SECS 45), :1615 (5 s snapshot TTL); src/ait/bot/orchestrator.py:3107 (mark age 900 s), :3170 (5 x 0.5 s exit NBBO poll), :3258-3259 and :3308-3309 (exit anchor `max(2.0 * mark_cost, entry + 0.25 * width)`)

**Claim:** Every parameter of how much credit the bot concedes on entry and how much it will pay to close is a literal with no config home: the proportional entry offset (15% of mid, min $0.10), the 0.25->0.60->1.00 ladder every 45 s, the 240 s cancel, the GOV-1 drift (35%) and disorderly-spread (100% of mid) tolerances, and the exit price anchors (2x marked cost or entry + 25% of wing width, mark valid 900 s). Config homes exist only for the credit floors and the exit cross, so an operator can tune the floor but not the offset that decides whether the ladder ever reaches it.

**Evidence:** executor.py:145 `offset = max(0.10, round(0.15 * abs(entry_price), 2))`; :1323-1324 `_LADDER_STEPS = (0.25, 0.60, 1.00)` / `_LADDER_STEP_SECS = 45`; :708 `abs(abs(signal.entry_price) - abs(_mid)) / abs(_mid) > 0.35`; :700 `_spread / abs(_mid) > 1.0`; orchestrator.py:3258-3259 `anchor = round(min(width, max(2.0 * mark_cost, float(trade.entry_price or 0) + 0.25 * width)), 2)`; :3107 `if age > 900: return None`. Only exit.exit_cross_amount (settings.py:407) and the credit floors (contract keys) are config-homed.

**Trigger:** unjustified: partial rationale lives in comments ('measured entry slippage was ~15% of credit') but no config home; drift between the offset and the config credit floors is invisible until the ladder parks at step 0 and entries time out.

**Consequence:** No live trade is wrong today. An operator cannot change entry concession, ladder cadence, order timeout, drift/disorderly tolerances or exit-price anchors from config.yaml, contrary to R19c; two of them are env-only knobs with private fallbacks the enforcement test cannot detect; and nothing validates order_timeout >= (len(_LADDER_STEPS)-1)*_LADDER_STEP_SECS, so an env timeout under 90 s silently cancels entries before the marketable step (the pre-R7 failure the 240 comment describes).

**Suggested fix:** Add an execution: config section as the finder proposes (defaults = today's literals), move AIT_ENTRY_ORDER_TIMEOUT and AIT_ENTRY_LADDER_START into CONTRACT_DEFAULTS+CONFIG_BACKED or delete them, narrow _ALLOWED_RAW_READS to the INST-5 key, document AIT_ALLOW_AFTER_HOURS as a deliberate exception, and add a startup assertion that the timeout covers the ladder span.

## hardcoded-live-09-config-keys-with-no-live-reader  [doc_vs_code | cosmetic | live_path=False]

**Where:** config.yaml:70 (options.delta_range), :23 (trading.trading_hours_only), :232 (meta_label.retrain_with_primary), :247 (exit.auto_hedge), :224-225 (learning.min_confidence_floor/ceiling) vs src/ait/learning/engine.py:41 + adaptor.py:81; IC-book-inert keys: exit.trailing_stop_pct / breakeven_trigger_pct / initial_stop_loss_pct / volatility_adjusted_stops (src/ait/execution/portfolio.py:486-517 debit-only branch), exit.partial_exit_levels (portfolio.py:816 `trade.quantity > 1`; qty is pinned to 1), positions.max_portfolio_delta (src/ait/risk/manager.py:560-570, greeks are 0); adaptor overrides 'stored on signal for executor' never read (orchestrator.py:2513, :2516; executor.py has no reader)

**Claim:** R19c makes config.yaml the operating source, yet these keys have no reader at all (zero grep hits outside settings.py) or govern only paths the iron-condor-only book never takes, so the operating config asserts control it does not have — the same class as the registered sentiment 20% claim (policy-vs-impl-8), but for options/exit/learning keys.

**Evidence:** grep -rln over src/ait excluding settings.py: delta_range -> none; trading_hours_only -> none; retrain_with_primary -> none; auto_hedge -> none; min_confidence_floor/ceiling -> src/ait/learning/adaptor.py only (its own AdaptationLimits defaults, engine.py:41 passes no limits=). portfolio.py:486 `if is_credit: ... effective_stop = -_loss_mult if _loss_mult > 0 else -999.0` (trailing/breakeven/vol-adjust only in the else branch :506-517); portfolio.py:816 `if not should_exit and not marks_missing and trade.quantity > 1:`; grep: trailing_stop_pct/profit_target_pct never read in executor.py.

**Trigger:** An operator tunes any of these keys expecting a live effect on the IC book; none occurs and no log says so.

**Consequence:** config.yaml asserts control over a dozen options/exit/learning/positions keys that have no effect on the IC book; an operator edit to any of them changes nothing and no log says so. No trade, verdict or report number is affected.

**Suggested fix:** Delete or tombstone-comment delta_range, trading_hours_only, retrain_with_primary, auto_hedge; construct AdaptationLimits from settings.learning in LearningEngine; annotate the debit-only and qty>1 exit keys and max_portfolio_delta in config.yaml; remove the dead 'stored on signal for executor' writes; add a test that every Settings field has a reader in src/ or is on an explicit tombstone list.

## hardcoded-research-04  [hardcoded | cosmetic | live_path=False]

**Where:** src/ait/backtesting/engine.py:99-101 (stop_loss_pct: float = 0.50, profit_target_pct: float = 1.00, max_hold_days: int = 30), engine.py:97 (slippage_pct = 0.01), engine.py:1025-1037 (compare_exit_modes constructs cls(data, strategies, trailing_stop_enabled=..., **shared)); run_backtest.py:446-450 (compare_exit_modes(data, strategies, initial_capital=args.capital) only); src/ait/config/settings.py:310-320 (BacktestConfig stop_loss_pct 0.35 / profit_target_pct 0.50 — the R20b homes)

**Claim:** R20b gave stop_loss_pct/profit_target_pct config homes (0.35/0.50) and migrated iv_floor/min_confidence/range_min_confidence to None->settings in the engine, but left the engine's own stop_loss_pct=0.50 and profit_target_pct=1.00 constructor defaults as literals that DIFFER from their config homes. Every bare-engine path — run_backtest --compare-exits (which prints an 'EXIT MODE COMPARISON' beneath a walk-forward that ran 0.35/0.50), and every test that constructs Backtester() without those args — runs a different debit stop/target and 3x lower slippage than the walk-forward in the same report.

**Evidence:** Executed: 'config backtest.stop_loss_pct/profit_target_pct = 0.35 0.5 | engine ctor defaults = 0.5 1.0'. engine.py:99-100 'stop_loss_pct: float = 0.50, profit_target_pct: float = 1.00' with no entry in the _resolve_setting block (engine.py:317-360 resolves entry_dte, scan_interval, entry windows, iv_floor, commission, fees, skew, range_min_confidence, min_confidence, time_decay_scaling only). run_backtest.py:446-450 passes only initial_capital to compare_exit_modes. Register live-bt-mirrors-08 covers the config-vs-live 0.35/0.50 fork and the optimizer copies, not the engine constructor defaults.

**Trigger:** `python run_backtest.py --compare-exits` (any debit strategy); any direct Backtester(...) construction without stop/profit args. Credit exits ignore stop_loss_pct, so IC-only runs are unaffected.

**Consequence:** A `run_backtest.py --compare-exits` with any debit strategy prints an 'EXIT MODE COMPARISON' computed at stop 0.50 / target 1.00 / slippage 0.01 directly beneath a walk-forward that ran 0.35 / 0.50 / 0.03, and any future bare Backtester() consumer inherits 2026-07 literals despite config homes existing; today no live or PLAN research path is affected.

**Suggested fix:** engine.py: stop_loss_pct / profit_target_pct / max_hold_days -> None sentinels resolved from settings.backtest in the same _resolve_setting block as iv_floor; give slippage_pct a BacktestConfig home shared by WalkForwardConfig; run_backtest.py --compare-exits should forward the WalkForwardConfig knobs; test: Backtester(data, ['long_call'])._stop_loss_pct == load_settings().backtest.stop_loss_pct.

## logic-exit-risk-08  [hardcoded | cosmetic | live_path=True]

**Where:** src/ait/risk/manager.py:435 (`_iv_cap = float(os.environ.get("AIT_IV_RANK_CAP", "85"))`), :470 (`_max_per_expiry = int(os.environ.get("AIT_MAX_PER_EXPIRY", "3"))`); src/ait/config/runtime_env.py:72-118 (CONTRACT_DEFAULTS / CONFIG_BACKED contain neither); config.yaml risk block (neither key present)

**Claim:** Two live risk-gate values live only as bare env reads with inline literals: the IV-rank ceiling (85) that vetoes new short premium, and the per-expiry position cap (3). Neither has a config.yaml home nor a CONTRACT_DEFAULTS entry, so under the R19c policy ('every trading value lives in config.yaml -> Settings; env is an override; CONTRACT_DEFAULTS is the safety net') an operator editing config cannot reach them and the R19 reader/contract drift class re-opens on the risk side. Not in either register (grep AIT_IV_RANK_CAP / AIT_MAX_PER_EXPIRY: no hits in reports/, PLAN.md, config.yaml, runtime_env.py).

**Evidence:** manager.py:435 and :470 quoted above; runtime_env.py CONTRACT_DEFAULTS keys are KMP/OMP, AIT_MARKET_DATA_TYPE, AIT_IC_WING_K, AIT_IC_MIN_CREDIT_WIDTH, AIT_IC_MIN_CREDIT, AIT_CREDIT_LOSS_LIMIT, AIT_ALLOW_UNDEFINED_RISK, AIT_SKIP_MACRO_EVENTS only. The per-expiry cap is additionally unreachable arithmetic (book saturates at 2 via the correlation cap).

**Trigger:** Any config-side tuning of the IV-rank veto (a real entry gate: 'iv_rank > cap: top-bucket IV carries negative VRP') silently does nothing; only a process env var changes it.

**Consequence:** The IV-rank ceiling (85) that vetoes every new condor when IV rank is rich is a live trading value that config.yaml cannot express (a risk.iv_rank_cap key is rejected at load), is absent from the env contract and from every test, and is invisible to the R19 contract-key scan — so it can be changed or drifted only through a process env var that no config, test, or register tracks; the per-expiry cap (3) shares the same gap but is currently unreachable arithmetic under the 3-ETF index universe.

**Suggested fix:** Add risk.iv_rank_cap (default 85) and risk.max_positions_per_expiry (default 3) to RiskConfig + config.yaml; read them in manager.py via self._risk_config; add AIT_IV_RANK_CAP / AIT_MAX_PER_EXPIRY to CONTRACT_DEFAULTS + CONFIG_BACKED so env stays an override; extend tests/test_r19_config_authority.py so the raw-environ scan also flags any `os.environ.get("AIT_...", <literal>)` outside the contract, with an executing test that a config edit changes validate_trade's verdict.

## ml-leakage-04  [doc_vs_code | cosmetic | live_path=False]

**Where:** config.yaml:191 ('CV AUC 0.545/0.545'); PLAN.md:1685 ('mean CV AUC SPY 0.5453, QQQ 0.5446'); src/ait/ml/range_predictor.py:373-396 and 407-428 (scores are balanced_accuracy_score of hard model.predict at the 0.5 cutoff)

**Claim:** The range model's cv_scores are balanced accuracy of hard 0.5-cutoff predictions, not AUC; the observe-mode registration and config comment describe and reason about them as 'CV AUC'.

**Evidence:** range_predictor.py:395-396 `preds = model.predict(X_val); scores.append(balanced_accuracy_score(y[val_idx], preds))` (same at 427-428); no roc_auc_score anywhere in range_predictor.py (only the direction ensemble, ensemble.py, and walkforward OOS eval compute AUROC). config.yaml:191 and PLAN.md:1685 label the same numbers 'CV AUC'.

**Trigger:** Any reader comparing the 0.545 'AUC' to the walkforward oos_scores auroc (walkforward.py:2022) or to literature baselines; the two statistics are not comparable and the balanced-accuracy one is threshold- and class-weight-dependent (see ml-leakage-03).

**Consequence:** The observe-mode decision record and the operating config describe the arming statistic as AUC, which invites direct comparison with the walkforward oos_scores AUROC and with literature baselines; the actual quantity is threshold- and class-weight-dependent balanced accuracy and, per ml-leakage-03, degenerates on low-negative folds. Documentation-only: no live path reads the label.

**Suggested fix:** Rename the quantity in config.yaml:191 and PLAN.md:1685 to cv_balanced_acc (matching the log key range_predictor.py:399 already emits), or switch the statistic to pooled OOF AUROC as ml-leakage-03 suggests so the existing label becomes true.

## params-trading-06  [doc_vs_code | cosmetic | live_path=True]

**Where:** config.yaml:59 (pre_event_blackout_days: 1 'Block new credit entries within N days'); src/ait/config/settings.py:64-69; src/ait/bot/orchestrator.py:1211-1215 (cycle-level should_skip_trading return); src/ait/data/economic_calendar.py:267-310 (hardcoded event-day + calendar-tomorrow + Fri->Mon window); src/ait/bot/orchestrator.py:2297-2309 and 3711-3728 (per-signal sessions gate)

**Claim:** risk.pre_event_blackout_days is not the authority its description implies: a hardcoded cycle-level skip aborts the whole scan (all strategies, including the event day itself) before the configured gate is ever consulted, so the knob can only tighten (>= 2) and never relax (pydantic allows 0), and the 'credit entries' scope in the config/Field text is false.

**Evidence:** orchestrator.py:1211-1215: `if contract_flag("AIT_SKIP_MACRO_EVENTS"): if self._economic_cal.should_skip_trading(): log.info("economic_event_skip", ...); return`. economic_calendar.py:297: `skip = self.is_event_day(check_date) or self.is_pre_event_day(check_date)` with is_pre_event_day = calendar tomorrow (+Monday on Fridays), no config input. settings.py:64 `pre_event_blackout_days: int = Field(default=1, ge=0, le=7, description="Block NEW credit entries within N calendar days of a macro event...")`. Live evidence 2026-09-03 (NFP 09-04): 70 economic_event_skip lines in logs/ait.log and zero scan_symbol_timing events all session.

**Trigger:** Setting pre_event_blackout_days to 0 (or enabling a debit/event strategy) expecting entries on the pre-event or event day: the cycle skip still returns; conversely any value >= 2 is honoured only by the per-signal gate for CREDIT_STRATEGIES.

**Consequence:** risk.pre_event_blackout_days reads as the authority for the pre-event window but can only widen it: the hardcoded cycle-level skip (event day + calendar day before, Fri->Mon) fires first for ALL strategies, so an operator lowering the knob (even to the permitted 0) sees no change and the 'credit entries' scope in the config/Field text is false - no effect under the shipped config.

**Suggested fix:** Either make should_skip_trading take the configured window (and restrict the cycle skip to credit strategies) or delete the cycle-level return and rely on the per-signal sessions gate; correct the config.yaml:59 and settings.py:64 descriptions to state the union window actually enforced; add an executing test that pre_event_blackout_days=0 permits a credit entry on a non-event day that the cycle skip would have blocked.

## pnl-money-04  [doc_vs_code | cosmetic | live_path=True]

**Where:** src/ait/bot/state.py:451-480 (insert_open_position writes entry_price into current_price at :474); src/ait/bot/state.py:595-609 (update_position_mark writes only unrealized_pnl, pnl_pct, mark_time); src/ait/execution/portfolio.py:474-479 (the only mark writer)

**Claim:** open_positions.current_price is frozen at the insert-time entry price for the life of the position (SPY -5.32, QQQ -6.43 today) because no writer ever updates it; the marks themselves ARE updating every tick (unrealized_pnl/pnl_pct/mark_time). The column is a dead, misleading representation of the mark; no consumer reads it, so the effect is confined to anyone inspecting the table.

**Evidence:** DB: SPY row current_price=-5.32 entry_price=-5.32 unrealized_pnl=-43.57 pnl_pct=-0.0819 mark_time=2026-09-03T15:59:53; QQQ current_price=-6.43 unrealized=-12.76 mark_time=15:59:53. state.py:607 'UPDATE open_positions SET unrealized_pnl = ?, pnl_pct = ?, mark_time = ? WHERE trade_id = ?' (no current_price). Log cross-check at the same instant: IBKR per-leg marks sum to a -5.68 cost-to-close for SPY (log updatePortfolio rows) consistent with a live -36..-44 unrealized, i.e. the mark is moving while current_price is not. grep 'current_price' across src/, status.py, status_server.py, scripts/: only state.py DDL/insert and portfolio's local spot variable — zero readers of the column.

**Trigger:** Always, for every open position.

**Consequence:** open_positions.current_price is a dead column that permanently shows the entry price under a name that promises the live mark; misleads manual DB inspection only.

**Suggested fix:** Either have update_position_mark also write the marked cost-to-close (entry - unrealized/(100*qty)) into current_price, or drop the column from the DDL/insert.

**Claim:** No risk cap is computed in the wrong currency — but the operational log makes it look that way. account_values_fetched logs net_liquidation '202593.60' beside base_currency 'CAD' and usd_to_base 1.3788; that figure is already USD (raw CAD NLV is ~279k). The task premise 'NLV 201,348 CAD' is this misreading: 201,3xx was the USD figure on 09-01. The unit-less log contract invites exactly the wrong conclusion an auditor drew.

**Evidence:** Verified the conversion end-to-end at one instant: account_snapshot 2026-09-03T19:59:53Z 'net_liq': 202593.6, 'unrealized_pnl': -63.64 (account-level tag, base-currency CAD divided by usd_to_base) vs the sum of the 8 open legs' IBKR unrealizedPNL from the most recent updatePortfolio rows before that line (USD, per-contract) = -63.64 exactly. If the division were missing the account figure would read -87.75. Consistency check on the caps: 3% of 202,594 USD = $6,078 admitted the QQQ condor (max_loss 5,356) on 09-01; at a 145k USD NLV it would have been rejected. ## regress-research-04  [logic | cosmetic | live_path=True]

**Where:** src/ait/monitoring/watchdog.py:230-237 (overall = UNKNOWN unless every registered component is HEALTHY) published by src/ait/monitoring/ops_health.py:870-877 as bot_state['watchdog_system']; src/ait/bot/orchestrator.py:1464-1468 (the ONLY market_data heartbeat sits at the end of _scan_symbol, after the ML prediction); orchestrator.py:386 (market_data registered at startup); src/ait/dashboard/app.py component_status_panel (_STATUS_ICONS maps 'unknown' -> white dot)

**Claim:** The new System Health row for the whole bot reads 'unknown' for an entire healthy session whenever the market_data component has not heartbeated, and that component only heartbeats when a symbol scan reaches the prediction stage — so on any day scans are skipped (macro blackout, IV-rank sentinel, entries frozen) the dashboard's 'System' line is a white 'unknown' next to trading_loop_alive=true / ibkr_connected=true, and cannot distinguish 'never scanned' from 'unhealthy'.

**Evidence:** Live bot_state at 2026-09-03 15:59:51 (after 6.5 h of RTH, trading loop started 13:30:00Z): watchdog_system = {"status": "unknown", "uptime_hours": 7.34, "trading_loop_alive": true, "ibkr_connected": true}; watchdog_market_data = {"status": "unknown", "last_heartbeat": ""}; watchdog_trading_loop / watchdog_ibkr healthy. Today's ait.log: scan_symbol_timing 0, ml_prediction 0, economic_calendar_skip 70 (scans skipped), so orchestrator.py:1466 `self._watchdog.heartbeat('market_data')` never ran. watchdog.py: `elif all(s == ComponentStatus.HEALTHY for s in statuses) and statuses: overall = HEALTHY else: overall = UNKNOWN`.

**Trigger:** Any session in which no _scan_symbol call reaches orchestrator.py:1466 (today: pre-event blackout); also every pre-market/early-session window before the first completed scan.

**Consequence:** On any session where no symbol scan reaches the prediction stage (macro blackout days such as 09-03/09-04, IV-rank sentinel, data-quality veto, or simply pre-first-scan), the System Health 'System' row and the Telegram summary read 'unknown' next to trading_loop_alive=true / ibkr_connected=true, so the operator cannot distinguish 'never scanned today' from an unhealthy bot and the new W6 surface carries no signal on exactly the quiet days it would be checked.

**Suggested fix:** Compute the overall status from components that have heartbeated at least once (treat never-heartbeated as 'idle', not a blocker), move the market_data heartbeat to immediately after the successful get_historical at orchestrator.py:~1323 (the A5 intent), and publish an explicit 'idle/not yet scanned' status for components with last_heartbeat == 0; pin with an executing test that a watchdog with ibkr+trading_loop healthy and market_data never heartbeated reports overall healthy (or 'idle'), not unknown.

## tests-honesty-09  [enforcement_gap | cosmetic | live_path=False]

**Where:** tests/conftest.py:64-82 (_isolate_model_artifacts patches MODEL_DIR on 4 modules); src/ait/ml/range_predictor.py:45 (`RESEARCH_MODEL_DIR = MODEL_DIR / "research"`, bound at import), 328 (`train` -> `self._save_models()`), 657-660 (writes `<dir>/range.pkl`); src/ait/ml/vol_magnitude_predictor.py:47; src/ait/backtesting/walkforward.py:2380-2392 (`from ait.ml.range_predictor import RESEARCH_MODEL_DIR` at call time, `model_dir=RESEARCH_MODEL_DIR`)

**Claim:** The autouse fence promises 'Redirect every model save in every test to tmp_path' but patches only MODEL_DIR; RESEARCH_MODEL_DIR is bound at import to the real repo models/research/, so any test that trains a window range model writes the repo tree. The default lane is clean; the writer is in the slow lane (test_walkforward runs with train_window_models=True by default) or a manual run. Nothing reads models/research/ (grep: walkforward.py is the only reference), so today's damage is hygiene only.

**Evidence:** Executed probe: a pytest plugin redirecting RESEARCH_MODEL_DIR and spying `_save_models` across tests/test_r20_research_validity.py, test_r20b_default_migration.py, test_r16_research_fixes.py, test_r21b_pr7_review_round.py, test_w2_orchestrator_gates.py recorded 0 research writes (130 passed). But `models/research/range.pkl` mtime is 2026-09-01 10:47:32, 53 s before commit 43d0409 (10:48:25), while the bot's own logs at 14:47Z show only chain activity ('ibkr_none_greeks_coerced') and no training run — a non-bot process wrote the repo's models tree; .gitignore hides it (`models/`).

**Trigger:** Any `-m slow` run (nightly CI or local) that trains a window range model; becomes a real defect the day anything loads models/research/range.pkl.

**Consequence:** A nightly `-m slow` run silently overwrites the repo's models/research/range.pkl with a model trained on synthetic test OHLCV — three times per test_walkforward.py run — despite the autouse fence claiming every model save in every test is redirected to tmp_path. Nothing reads that artifact today, so the cost is hygiene plus a false sense that the fence is complete; it becomes a real defect the moment any research or dashboard path loads models/research/range.pkl, and the .gitignore'd models/ tree hides the mutation.

**Suggested fix:** Extend tests/conftest.py:_isolate_model_artifacts to patch RESEARCH_MODEL_DIR too — e.g. after the MODEL_DIR loop, `for _mod in (_rp, _vp): if hasattr(_mod, 'RESEARCH_MODEL_DIR'): monkeypatch.setattr(_mod, 'RESEARCH_MODEL_DIR', tmp_path / 'research', raising=False)`. Better still, replace the hand-maintained module tuple with a package scan over ait.ml that patches every attribute ending in MODEL_DIR, so the next derived dir cannot escape the same way (this is the third miss: meta_label in R15 #7, now RESEARCH_MODEL_DIR twice).

---

# Critic: gaps the audit itself could not see

- BROKER PROTOCOL LAYER HAS NO OWNING ANGLE. src/ait/broker/ibkr_client.py (844 lines), orders.py (186), contracts.py (166), account.py (276) were touched by zero of the 16 angles. Unexamined: combo leg ratio/sign and SMART routing on the 4-leg IC, orderRef/permId reuse across restart (the id map that hardcoded-live-03 and regress-live-09 depend on), clientId collision with the keeper (adjacent to OPEN bot-day-08), and execDetails handling of an assignment/exercise event. Register item model-vs-reality-ex-div-assignment-void is still OPEN and no angle looked at it, although a short ITM put on SPY/QQQ/IWM over a dividend date is a live path for this exact book.
- DASHBOARD app.py IS EFFECTIVELY UNAUDITED. 2102 lines, 766 changed in the audited window, and only data-integrity-01 (duck-backed Sharpe) touched it. It contains 12+ direct `FROM trades` queries; app.py:1403 is the one trades reader in the repo that still hand-rolls its own predicate (`exit_reason_detailed != ''`) instead of go_live.not_real_close_sql(), and app.py:659/727/847/883/976/1069 were never checked against that authority. This is the surface the operator will actually read while deciding go-live.
- NOBODY BUILT THE 'WHO FILTERS SENTINELS / WHO DOESN'T' MAP. W3 pushed not_real_close_sql() into go_live, analyzer.py:590, analytics.py:420, duckdb_analytics.py:42, meta_label.py:493, master.py:1156 and part of the dashboard — but the angles verified this per-finding, never as a census. The consumers still missing it are precisely the risk-control ones (daily_stats writer, the breaker realized term). A 30-minute enumeration would have found which side of the line each of the ~25 trades readers sits on.
- THE GO-LIVE GATE THRESHOLDS THEMSELVES ARE UNEXAMINED CODE LITERALS. src/ait/reporting/go_live.py:163-167 — GO_LIVE_PRELIM_CLOSES=50, GO_LIVE_FUNDING_CLOSES=100, GATE_PF=1.3, GATE_DD_PCT=8.0, GATE_SLIP_PCT=8.0 — have no config.yaml home and are NOT among the nine gates hardcoded-live-04 lists. Under R19c these are trading values: they authorize deploying real capital. Also unaudited: _build_criteria() makes criterion [5] (incidents) permanently UNAVAILABLE and [4] (slippage) conditionally UNAVAILABLE, and no angle checked how status.py / the Friday Telegram scorecard aggregate a verdict in which 2 of 5 criteria can never be evaluated — i.e. whether UNAVAILABLE reads as non-blocking.
- CURRENCY AND BUYING-POWER ARITHMETIC HAS NO OWNER. The funding plan is $3k CAD into a USD-margined IBKR account. pnl-money-05 covers a log label; params-trading-01 covers the self-test. Nobody traced broker/account.py's base-currency NLV -> risk/capital_tiers.py -> risk/position_sizer.py -> the 3%-of-NLV budget. A currency mismatch anywhere on that path is a ~35% sizing error on day 1 of real money, and it is the single most likely go-live failure with zero findings against it.
- MARGIN REQUIREMENT VS RISK BUDGET NEVER COMPUTED. Register item model-vs-reality-margin-model-unimplemented is OPEN and untouched by all 16 angles. A %-of-NLV risk budget is not a Reg-T maintenance model: on ~$2.1k USD, a single $5-wide IC requires ~$500 maintenance per contract. No angle established whether the bot can hold the positions its sizer plans, or what IB does when it cannot (forced liquidation is the worst possible interaction with the broken exit path).
- CORRELATION AND STRATEGY-SELECTION GATES READ ONLY THROUGH SYMPTOMS. logic-exit-risk-03 observed a same-day correlated re-entry empirically; nobody opened src/ait/risk/correlation.py (174 lines) or strategies/selector.py (245) / thompson.py to establish whether the correlation gate is armed for the IC book at all. OPEN register items dead-surface-1/2/3 are adjacent and unresolved.
- ops_health.py (991 NEW LINES) WAS AUDITED ONLY VIA ONE SYMPTOM. regress-research-03 covers the log re-read cost; the module's actual protection logic is unexamined: bot_liveness()/LivenessVerdict.should_ping() (the deadman arming logic that OPEN items external-contracts-04 and bot-day-02 were supposed to fix), HealthStatePublisher writing to the SAME bot_state DB whose lock contention drives regress-live-02/04 and time-calendar-02, IncrementalEventCounter._seed/_reset_day across the local-midnight and DST boundary, count_native_crashes, read_channel_state.trustworthy.
- NOTIFICATION PATH UNOWNED. notifications/telegram.py (103 lines) got no angle; external-contracts-03 (429 Retry-After ignored) is still OPEN. Every breaker alert and the Friday go-live scorecard ride this one channel — a silent drop makes most of the ops_reporting findings undetectable in practice, which changes their real severity.
- ENTRY FILL-QUALITY WRITER NEVER TRACED. data-integrity-08 flags that exit fill quality is unrecorded; the mirror gap is that go_live criterion [4] depends on executions.live_mid existing, and go_live itself prints 'no execution carries live_mid on a trade with an entry price' as an expected state. No angle established who writes live_mid, or whether the slippage gate is structurally unmeasurable — which would mean the go-live bar is 3 criteria, not 5.
- MIGRATION / RESTART-SHAPED DATA LOSS. No angle covered state.py's schema ALTER paths on an upgrade restart, nor master.py:1003-1004's sqlite->duckdb copy (a bare source/dest COUNT(*) check) as a data-loss surface. regress-live-04 covers the trip-clearing symptom of a mid-_persist relaunch, not the schema or copy path.
- WRONGLY-REFUTED CANDIDATES WORTH A RE-LOOK: (a) hardcoded-live-10 (sizer VIX ladder diverges from config tiers) was refuted as 'latent' — 'no live effect today' is the wrong lens for a position sizer when funding is the next milestone; logic-entry-01 and params-trading-01 survived on identical latency. (b) hardcoded-live-02 (observe baseline vs min_confidence unenforced) was refuted while data-integrity-02 and params-ml-research-06 survived saying the same thing from the other side — check the refutation was not a duplicate-collision. (c) logic-exit-risk-05/06/07 were ALL refuted in the one angle that produced the two worst confirmed live_money defects; a clean sweep of the neighbours of a broken exit path is implausible — re-look at whichever concerned exit re-pricing/timeout. (d) ml-leakage-07/08 and params-ml-research-11/12 refuted, yet no surviving finding covers pooled cross-symbol CV (SPY/QQQ/IWM rows sharing the same calendar days in a 4-fold split is cross-sectional leakage that inflates the very arming statistic ml-leakage-03 attacks) — if one of the refuted pair was that, it was refuted on the wrong lens. (e) regress-research-05 is the lone refutation in an angle where the other four all landed.

# Critic: severity corrections

- RANK #1, AND MERGE: the 'pre-registered verdict is unexecutable' cluster — hardcoded-live-01, params-ml-research-01, logic-entry-05, ops-substrate-05, data-integrity-03, docs-policy-01, params-trading-04, data-integrity-02 — is currently eight separate research_validity items. As eight it reads as documentation debt; as one it says the only decision this project is running toward cannot be made when it arrives. For a bot whose entire purpose right now is accumulating 50 closes, this outranks every live_money finding on a paper book. Report as ONE blocker with seven cross-references.
- UPGRADE params-trading-05 and regress-live-05 (PDT) from ops_reporting / live:false to live_money-at-funding. On a $3k account PDT is not latent — it is the default reality, it fires on the first same-day close (which the touch-stop path makes routine), and the penalty is a 90-day restriction that ends the funding plan outright. 'live:false because the paper account is $198k' is a lens error given the stated $3k CAD plan.
- UPGRADE ops-substrate-03 (cleanup_old_logs unlinks top-level reports/ files older than 2026-09-01, next fires 2026-10-01) from ops_reporting to blocks-go-live-evidence, and put the date in the finding. reports/daily_*.json is the durable half of the observe-mode record that logic-entry-05 and ops-substrate-05 say the rotating logs cannot supply. It is ~3.5 weeks out and it deletes the evidence for the 50-close verdict. Same reclassification for ops-substrate-04 (single copy of the 28,338-bar intraday store, IB will not re-serve).
- RE-RANK logic-exit-risk-01 + 02 ABOVE all accounting findings. 61/63 exit attempts rejected means the stop-loss does not exist as a mechanism. On paper this only corrupts the sample; at $3k it is what converts a defined-risk condor into a full-width loss. Severity live_money is right, ordering relative to concurrency-*/pnl-money-* is wrong.
- MERGE AND RE-FRAME logic-entry-01 + params-trading-01 as a single DAY-1 CERTAINTY, not a tail risk. At the documented $2.1k USD / 3% budget the pre-funding gate misfires on the first funded session with probability ~1 (self-test reports problems=0 while the sizer forces $1 wings whose credit cannot clear costs). It should be the first thing fixed before any funding, ahead of items with higher nominal severity but lower probability.
- RAISE regress-research-02 and params-ml-research-09 out of 'live:false'. hardcoded-research-10 establishes that --optimize-per-window promotions reach live config (wing_k=1.6 on 2026-08-04). A cost-model defect inside the promotion pipeline is a live-parameter defect one step removed, not a research curiosity. Reclassify as research_validity-that-reaches-live.
- UPGRADE tests-honesty-03 (smoke_deploy.py prints 'SMOKE PASS ... cycle+monitor executed clean' for a build whose armed gates refuse every entry). This is the release gate for the build that will run the funded account; ops_reporting understates a check that certifies a non-trading bot as healthy. Pair with tests-honesty-02 (empty candidate list -> pytest.skip), which makes the same regression shape invisible in the suite.
- UPGRADE pnl-money-05 (USD/CAD log label) from cosmetic to ops_reporting-at-funding, and note that the finding stops one step short: the label is cosmetic, but the same ambiguity in the READER (broker/account.py -> position_sizer) is a ~35% sizing error on a CAD-funded account. See gap #5 — the reader was never audited.
- CONFIRM AS COSMETIC AND DE-PRIORITIZE so they do not compete for the same fix wave: hardcoded-live-09, hardcoded-research-04, docs-policy-07, docs-policy-08, pnl-money-06, data-integrity-10, params-ml-research-07, params-ml-research-13, tests-honesty-09, regress-research-04. Ten of ~90 findings are correctly cosmetic; saying so explicitly is worth more than restating them.
- NOTE ON THE ops_reporting BUCKET GENERALLY: for a project whose next milestone is a judgement made from records, 'ops_reporting' is doing double duty for (a) an operator sees a wrong number and (b) the evidence for the go/no-go decision is wrong or gone. Class (b) — pnl-money-01, logic-exit-risk-04, regress-live-09, concurrency-05, data-integrity-09, ops-substrate-03 — should carry a distinct decision-integrity severity above ops_reporting and below live_money.

# Critic: interaction chains

- BREAKER BLINDNESS TRIPLE — concurrency-01 x regress-live-01 x pnl-money-01/logic-exit-risk-04. A committed stop-out loss is deleted from the outbox and never reaches daily_stats (under-count); a swept never-filled entry resets consecutive_losses to 0 (streak erased); a $0 sentinel books as a LOST trade in daily_stats (over-count). None of the three errors cancels the others, and all three can land in the same session. The sharpened version: W3 propagated go_live.not_real_close_sql() into every REPORTING reader (go_live, analyzer.py:590, analytics.py:420, duckdb_analytics.py:42, meta_label.py:493, master.py:1156) but not into daily_stats or the breaker's realized term — so the fix wave hardened the surfaces that only describe the book and left the one surface that actually stops trading being fooled. The operator's scorecard reads clean while the 3-loss pause and daily-loss cap are running on fiction.
- THE STOP DOESN'T FILL AND THE LOSS ARRIVES WHERE NOTHING WATCHES — logic-exit-risk-01 x logic-exit-risk-02 x regress-live-03 x time-calendar-03. The exit is priced at 6-10x the mark and rejected (61/63); the reject is indistinguishable from a cancel so it re-submits the same unexecutable limit forever; the position therefore rides to expiry and is booked by the post-market reconciler, which regress-live-03 shows bypasses the protection window; time-calendar-03 then mis-stamps that close into the last 15-20 minutes so the daily rollup buckets it wrong. Full-width loss realized, breaker never sees it, digest mis-attributes it. This is the composition that actually empties a $3k account.
- ONE SQLITE LOCK WINDOW DISABLES FOUR INDEPENDENT PROTECTIONS — regress-live-02 x regress-live-07 x time-calendar-02 x regress-live-04, plus OPEN multiprocess-db-1..5. A single contended write on bot_state during a busy scan can simultaneously double-book a loss (breaker -$300 and streak +2 for one loss), make the post-stop cooldown query raise and fail OPEN, and let a keeper relaunch lift the fail-closed trip and overwrite it. New aggravator from the unaudited code: ops_health.HealthStatePublisher now writes into that same DB on a cycle. There is no 'state DB unhealthy -> stop trading' rule anywhere; each guard fails independently and in a different direction, so the failure mode is not degraded protection but incoherent protection.
- POST-STOP RE-ENTRY INTO THE SAME MOVE — logic-exit-risk-03 x regress-live-07/time-calendar-02 x the never-audited correlation gate (gap 7, OPEN dead-surface-1/2/3). Stop out of SPY on a trend day; the cooldown check throws and fails open; a correlated QQQ condor opens into the same directional move (the 08-24 QQQ re-entry 13 minutes later is the observed instance); and per interaction #2 neither position's stop can fill. Correlated, uncapped, un-cooled-down double loss in one session.
- THE GO-LIVE SAMPLE IS OFF-POLICY AND UN-SCORABLE AT THE SAME TIME — logic-entry-11 x ml-leakage-01 x data-integrity-07, then hardcoded-live-01/docs-policy-01. Four of the ten closes in the current verdict sample were entered 09:36-10:00; every pre-11:10 prediction is computed at a feature encoding no training row has; and the pre-10:30 entry gate is spent by orders that never became positions, which over-weights exactly that window. So the most anomalous members of the sample are both out-of-distribution for the model and out-of-sample for the research that set the parameters — and the p_in_range needed to identify them will have rotated out of the logs by close 50.
- THE 50 CLOSES ARE NOT PRODUCED BY ONE MODEL — regress-research-01 x params-ml-research-04 x ml-leakage-02/03 x ops-substrate-04. Each morning's retrain writes an h=30 range.pkl that the marker restart detects as a spec mismatch and forces a fallback; the 0.65 floor was calibrated at h=30 while the live model is h=9; the statistic that arms or disarms the gate is a degenerate 4-fold mean; and the intraday store that feeds all of it exists in one copy. The sample being pooled into a single PF compared against GATE_PF=1.3 therefore mixes armed-h9 days, fallback days, and days on which the arming statistic was meaningless.
- SILENT OUTAGE READS AS A QUIET MARKET, AND THE CLOCK KEEPS RUNNING — ops-substrate-01 x OPEN bot-day-01 x data-integrity-09 x go_live's pace line x OPEN external-contracts-03. Bot dead with the Gateway port closed is an absorbing state the supervisor never escapes; master death leaves a green deadman; a day the bot never observed is indistinguishable in the state DB and in reports/daily_*.json from a healthy flat day. The go-live pace estimate keeps counting calendar days toward 50, and the one channel that could page the operator (telegram, 429 Retry-After ignored, unaudited) may itself be dropping messages.
- CONFIG AUTHORITY IS A FACADE EXACTLY WHERE MONEY IS — hardcoded-live-04 x logic-entry-08 x params-trading-03 x params-trading-10 x docs-policy-05 x hardcoded-research-05, plus the new go_live.py:163-167 gate literals. Nine-plus binding entry/execution gates, the 0.65 range floor, and now the go-live bar itself (50/100/PF 1.3/DD 8%/slip 8%) live in source with no config home — while the R19c policy tests stay green and certify that config.yaml is the authority. At funding, the operator's documented lever cannot tighten a single binding gate nor the bar that authorizes deploying capital, and the suite will tell them it can.
- IV-RANK FEEDBACK LOOP — data-integrity-05 x logic-entry-09 x logic-entry-03 x time-calendar-01. The IV series only advances on days the entry scan reaches the chain fetch; the 3-session event window blocks 49% of sessions and the holiday interaction blocks more; so blackout streaks punch holes in the very history the iv_rank percentile is computed from, and the floor-15 gate then mis-scores IV on the days the bot does scan. The gate that decides whether to sell premium and the data that calibrates it are coupled through the same blackout — and the entries that survive are the ones filling the 50-close sample.
- BIASED, NON-RANDOM OVERSTATEMENT OF THE PF NUMBER — pnl-money-02 x concurrency-05 x hardcoded-live-03 x regress-live-09. Rows booked by the drain instead of the callback keep the $5.20 flat estimate and overstate realized_pnl by ~$2.13; closes with no attributed commission fall back to the same estimate. At ~$40-60 credit per condor that is 4-5% of gross, applied ONLY to the subset of closes affected by a crash/restart or a lost commissionReport. That is not noise around GATE_PF=1.3 — it is a one-sided lift concentrated in exactly the sessions where something else also went wrong.
- EXIT-PATH BREAKAGE FEEDS THE ACCOUNTING BREAKAGE — logic-exit-risk-02 (endless re-submission of an unexecutable limit) x hardcoded-live-08 (exit concession, order timeout and ladder cadence have no config home). The parameters an operator would reach for to work around the rejected-exit bug are precisely the ones that cannot be changed without a code edit. hardcoded-live-08 is rated cosmetic on its own; composed with logic-exit-risk-01/02 it is the reason the live_money defect has no operational mitigation.
